# Debug journal

Notes distilled from maintainer debugging sessions and triage-bot runs on this repo (roughly June–September 2026), written for whoever is triaging an issue next.

Every entry is an observation from a past investigation, not a statement about current main. The code moves. Use these as "look here first", confirm against the working tree before you put anything in a comment, and say what you actually confirmed rather than what this file says.

## Replay the reporter's debug file

The most useful attachment on a bug report is `predbat_debug.yaml` — a full state dump the test harness can replay against current main:

```bash
cd coverage
./run_all --debug_file <scratch>/predbat_debug.yaml > <scratch>/replay.log 2>&1
```

What you get out of it:

- **Every config item the reporter has changed from default**, printed as `- <item> = <value> (default <value>)`. Grep the replay log for that block before reading their yaml — it is where most "the plan is wrong" reports get settled.
- A recalculated plan with a metric before and after, written to `plan_orig.html` and `plan_final.json` in `coverage/`. Both are gitignored, and `git clean -fd` clears them.
- `--redo` recomputes rates, load model and Octopus slots instead of reusing the ones in the dump. Without it you are replaying the exact state they had, which is normally what you want.

Committed examples of the same format live in `coverage/cases/*.yaml`; they run as golden regressions under `./run_all --test debug_cases`.

`--debug_file` **without** `--redo` can crash on an older dump with `KeyError: 0` (GH#4766; a v9.3.2-era replay hit it at `prediction.py:840` — the line drifts, re-anchor on `pv_forecast_minute`) — retry with `--redo` before concluding the dump itself is unusable.

**A replay cannot reproduce ML-forecast-dependent plan choices.** `--redo` resets the load model, and the live ML forecast cannot be replayed from the dump — so a replay differing from the live plan in exactly the optimiser's marginal ~10p slots is expected, not proof the slot is a bug (GH#5213). Treat "replay doesn't show it" as inconclusive when the candidate choice is worth ~10p; the decisive check is whether the *same version's own* plan in the attached log contains the slot — grep `Best export window`/`Best charge window` lines per version window in their log. The same recipe replays a dump outside `--debug_file`: register a temporary test function in `TEST_REGISTRY` that calls `run_single_debug(..., redo=True)` (`tests/test_single_debug.py`), run via `tools/triage_test.sh` — which is also the only way to exercise the fetch-layer threshold logic (`set_rate_thresholds()` + the `rate_scan_window()` rescan) on a dump's rates, since the dump's stored threshold/windows are live-run end state, useful for the symptom but not for re-derivation (GH#5221). And since PR #5427 (9e5f0024, 2026-10-12) the plan-cycle log carries `Replay input:` lines — PV/load/rate/inverter inputs logged on change plus a per-cycle state line — so a log **without** a dump can reproduce a plan cycle's inputs exactly; `tests/test_log_replay_inputs.py` pins the formats, and a comparison run deliberately logs none (that is a tell for "is this line from a comparison?"). Two of the logged inputs have no parser consumer: `Replay input: ML load forecast changed …` (`log_replay_ml_forecast()`, fetch.py) and `Replay input: dynamic load baseline …` (`log_replay_baseline()`, fetch.py, called from `calculate_plan()` under `if publish:`) are logged on main with no regex for either — the replay tool that parses these lines is external (PR #5363, `tests/replay_forward.py` + `dummy_inverter.py`, open at writing), so `log_replay_*` format strings are a cross-repo contract: diff any change against the parser regexes, because an unrecognised line is skipped silently and the replay falls back to the dump's stale value. On open PR #5591 (not merged at writing) `log_replay_changed()` keys its signature on `(self.midnight_utc, hash)`, so identical inputs re-log roughly daily by design; on current main a repeated identical "Replay input:" line still means the value changed. (Under the triage bot's permission mode a bare `./run_all --debug_file` is denied — only `tools/triage_test.sh` runs — so reading the dump's plan state directly is the substitute; say so plainly in the posted comment, as done on GH#5509.)

**A v9.1.0+ dump can crash the loader itself before any Predbat code runs.** Dumps written by v9.1.0+ can carry cached `numpy.dtype` objects (`!!python/object/apply:numpy.dtype`, anchored and aliased through `numpy._core.multiarray.scalar` entries), and the harness's `DEBUG_YAML_LOADER` (`userinterface.py`) raises `SystemError ... bad argument to internal function` from `dtype.__setstate__` on the coverage venv's numpy 2.x while loading (GH#5205 replay, verified three times on a 3.2 MB dump). Lossless replay workaround (throwaway edit next to the loader definition): wrap `yaml.constructor.UnsafeConstructor.set_python_instance_state` and skip state application when `type(instance).__module__` starts with `"numpy"` — the dtype is already fully defined by its constructor args, so skipping the legacy state tuple is lossless. Two guard mistakes that each cost a run: the instance type is not named `dtype` on numpy 2.x (`np.dtype('f8')`'s type is `Float64DType`), and the passthrough must use `*args/**kwargs` — this PyYAML version rejects an `unsafe=` kwarg. If this is ever fixed properly it belongs in the loader definition, not in the replay flow. The class is wider than numpy: dumps carrying other `python/object` tags (`utils.MinuteArray`, `control_ledger.ControlLedger`) crash `safe_load` the same way — a tolerant loader reconstructs a `MinuteArray` from its serialized `_data` and accepts `python/object/*` generically as attribute dicts (GH#5382 replay, verified 2026-10-04). A third loader-crash cause: a dump whose captured `args` carry **`!secret x` tags** — `DEBUG_YAML_LOADER` (userinterface.py) registers no `!secret` constructor, so the load dies with `ConstructorError ... could not determine a constructor for the tag '!secret'` before any Predbat code runs (GH#5748's Teslemetry dump had four; the journal's own `!secret` row below is about apps.yaml *boot* parsing — different loader, different failure). Workaround: sed each `!secret ...` line to a redacted dummy in a copy and replay that. Reading a dump's plan state without replay (GH#5509, from a reporter's v9.3.8 dump, 2026-10-12): rates and window lists are absolute minutes of the dump day while `predict_soc_best`/`predict_metric_best` are minutes-since-now, and the same window's `export_window_best[].target` and its packed `export_limits_best[]` entry are **different numbers** (window-level target vs the post-clip level — read the level via `export_mode_of()`/`export_target_of()`), so cross-check a window's stored `average` against the rate arrays before interpreting any field. A distinct family member, and not a tag problem (GH#5569, triaged 2026-10-16 against 213cb2e2): a **Predbat-level member carrying a tuple-keyed dict** serialises into a YAML mapping whose keys are sequences, and the replay load then dies inside the constructor with `ConstructorError ... found unhashable key` regardless of loader tolerance — the class resolves fine, the key hashing is what fails. First known member `io_slot_log_gate` (`RepeatLogGate`, #5428, shipped v9.3.8; member on `predbat.py`, `logged` keyed by `(key, text-sha1)` tuples in `utils.py`). Two traps: without `apps/predbat` on `sys.path` the standalone load dies at the *first* `!!python/object` tag whose module is not importable, which can be mistaken for the real failure (the v9.4.0 dump triaged for #5569 died at `!!python/object:control_ledger`, line 4919, and the `io_slot_log_gate` block was the sole blocker once the path was set — delete it and the same loader reports `LOADED OK`); and the fix shape is adding the key to `DEBUG_EXCLUDE_LIST` (`utils.py`, shared with the MCP state-query consumer), not loader tolerance — **that is what landed: PR #5575 (2026-10-17) puts `io_slot_log_gate` in `DEBUG_EXCLUDE_LIST` on main, and PR #5574 added the replay regression tests — on a fixed main, new dumps simply carry no gate**. The Octopus component's own `log_gate` is already safe via the `components` exclusion; don't "fix" that one. An older tree's loader fails earlier with `cannot find 'RepeatLogGate' in the module 'utils'` — same block, tag predating the class. The **reverse direction hits too**: a *newer* codebase's dump replayed on an older checkout dies on tags from modules the older tree lacks — a v9.4.6 dump carries `!!python/object:control_monitor.ControlMonitor` and `control_ledger.ControlLedger` and a v9.3.8 checkout fails with `No module named 'control_monitor'` (GH#5719, hit in a cross-version A/B replay 2026-10-24); lossless workaround is a dump copy with those top-level blocks nulled (`control_monitor:`/`control_ledger:` — one of each on that dump; `MinuteArray`/`array.array` tags resolve on both trees).

Two lines in that output are normal and are not the reporter's bug:

- `Prediction kernel stale binary ... - using Python engine` — the local C++ kernel is older than the Python side expects, so the pure-Python engine runs instead. The plan is still correct, just slower.
- `Config item ... is below the minimum ... - clamping to ...` — routine clamping of an out-of-range setting.

## Debug yaml can contain real secrets

A `predbat_debug.yaml` or debug-history snapshot attached to a bug report can carry live, unredacted credentials. Read it locally; never quote a raw block from one in a public comment without checking it first.

- **The `args_from_apps_yaml` leak is fixed — do not tell a reporter to rotate credentials on the strength of this entry.** For a while both dump paths special-cased only the field literally named `args`, so `self.args_from_apps_yaml` — a second full copy of apps.yaml taken before Predbat's own defaulting, added in PR #4500 — went out unmasked with every `!secret` already resolved, through `create_debug_yaml()`, every rolling debug-history snapshot, and the `get_state` agent/MCP tool. GH#4765 caught a real reporter that way: their debug yaml carried a plaintext `mcp_secret`. PR #4910 (`0c7747e4`) closed it at the source — `predbat.py:1865` is now `self.args_from_apps_yaml = mask_secret_args(self.args)`, so the copy cannot carry a secret however it is later dumped, and both dump paths mask it as well (`userinterface.py:775`, `agent_tools.py:1131`). A debug yaml from **before v8.55.0** can still carry live credentials, so the "read it locally, never quote a raw block" rule below stands regardless of version.
- The masker itself (`mask_secret_args()` / `is_secret_key()`, `utils.py:141-159`) matches `_key`/`password`/`secret`/`token` substrings on top-level keys. Narrower gaps flagged in GH#4757/#4770 (credential-like keys with none of those substrings) and GH#4768 (the MCP `get_apps` tool returning `args` unmasked) have since been closed — confirmed fixed on `main`.
- `debug_history_enable` defaults **True** and captures a snapshot roughly every 3 hours **independently of `switch.predbat_debug_enable`** (`predbat.py:807-820`) — a user who has never turned debug on still has credentials sitting in the rotating `predbat_debug_history.tgz` buffer.
- `annual.py:60`'s `scrub_secrets()` is a properly recursive masker; its docstring's claim to mirror `create_debug_yaml()`'s behaviour is itself wrong (GH#4770) — they mask differently, and `create_debug_yaml()` is the leakier of the two.
- **Write-time log redaction is on `main` now** (PR #5053, GH#4770): log lines are masked as they are written, and an invalidation hook (`_invalidate_log_secret_pattern()`) re-derives the pattern when args or secrets load, including from `auto_config()`. GH#5106 — `resolve_arg_re()` logging the matched entity id unmasked — was real only on the then-open PR branch and was closed during that merge (`bd79397a` masks that line; later review rounds closed the pattern-cache race). PR #5171 has since moved the whole cache into a `LogRedaction` mixin (`log_secrets.py`), which `Hass` mixes in — its `threading.Lock` is a shared class attribute rather than something lazily built, so two first-use threads cannot acquire separate locks. Two traps from that investigation survive it: an issue's cited line numbers may resolve against a PR branch rather than main — check which tree the numbers resolve against before reading code; and `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<n>/comments` has come back permission-denied on the runner — fall back to the JSON fields `gh pr view --json` offers.

## Check configuration before code

"The plan is wrong" has repeatedly turned out to be settings rather than a defect:

- **GH#4222** (odd freeze-export windows) — traced to the reporter's own `combine_export_slots: True` plus a high `metric_min_improvement_export`, not a code regression. A stale local test file also produced a false kernel regression partway through that investigation.
- **GH#4478** (exports scheduled late) — there *was* a real ordering bug (`optimise_swap_export` ran before the later plan passes, fixed in PR #4513), but the user-visible difference was dominated by `best_soc_keep: 4.0` in their config. The commented-out swap call left at the old position in `optimise_all_windows()` can make a reader think the pass is dead - it is not: the live call runs deliberately **last**, after the plan and solar passes that add the near-term exports the swap exists to push back. Read `optimise_all_windows()` end to end before claiming any pass "runs during planning"; symbol presence in `plan.py` is not evidence either way.
- **GH#4840** ("random" exports at peak rate on a flat 12p export tariff) — `metric_battery_value_scaling: 0.9` against the 1.0 default. `compute_metric()` credits leftover SoC at `battery_value_rate()`, which is the *replacement* cost of a stored kWh (cheapest future import grossed up by charge losses). Exporting is profitable to the planner exactly when `export × inverter_loss × battery_loss_discharge` beats that credit, so the protective margin is only `(export realised) / (replacement cost)` — on 10.54p import against 12p export that is `11.174 / 11.319`, i.e. **1.28%**. Any scaling below ~0.987 flips every export decision on a near-parity tariff, and the trade genuinely loses ~0.2p/kWh. Two things made this hard to see: the reporter had also turned off `metric_cloud_enable` (default **True**), so the planner assumed perfectly smooth PV and predicted *zero* grid import for 48h — restoring that default alone also removed the export; and the modelled 48h cost looks ~52p *better* with the export, because the plan simply ends with 4.7kWh less in the battery. Note the slider's step was 0.1 until the PR for #4840 reduced it to 0.01, so the smallest possible nudge below the default used to overshoot the flip point by 7.8x - and 0.02 was not enough either, since 0.98 is still below 0.9872.

- **"Loss-making charge-to-export" reports (GH#5069, second variant of the #4840 margin analysis) — check the reporter's arithmetic first.** Two terms the planner's metric counts that a naive `export × round-trip-efficiency vs import price` comparison omits: (1) the end-of-record SoC credit — `battery_value_rate()` credits stored energy at the replacement import rate, so discharging ~0.93 kWh stored forfeits ~15p of credit and arbitrage that looks −0.2p direct is ~+25p worse to the planner; and (2) house displacement — a multi-hour charge ending at a peak band usually serves house load at the peak import rate, which *is* profitable, and export happens only for surplus. `metric_min_improvement_export` (default 0.1p) is the existing minimum-margin gate on exports. If a report survives both corrections, ask for the debug yaml + plan from a recurrence before believing an optimiser bug.

- **GH#3683 (non-GBP tariffs): the plan metric is computed in the tariff's own minor currency unit and every `metric_min_improvement*` gate is compared against it directly, with no conversion.** All five definitions carry `"unit": "p"` with pence-scaled defaults (`metric_min_improvement_plan` 2.0, `metric_min_improvement` 0.0, `metric_min_improvement_export` 0.1, `metric_min_improvement_swap` -0.25, `metric_min_improvement_export_freeze` 0.1 — `config.py`), so on a øre or cents tariff each default acts the minor-unit ratio too small (measured ~8x on DKK øre) and the plan re-adopts a marginally-better plan almost every cycle. The GH#3683 reporter had raised import/export thresholds (9/10 øre) but left `metric_min_improvement_plan` at 0.0 and `..._export_freeze` at 0.1 øre — effectively no retention gate at all — which fully explained the churn without the load model in play (their `load_ml_enable` + `calculate_inday_adjustment` were on as well: a separate, unrelated cause). Workaround is config-side: multiply the pence defaults by the minor-unit ratio (~16 øre stands in for the 2.0p plan gate on DKK; verified by reading `config.py` and the reporter's dump on 2026-10-19). Docs lag: customisation.md says "in pence" for these thresholds plus `combine_rate_threshold` and `metric_battery_cycle` and never says "your tariff's minor unit", and `metric_min_improvement_plan` is not in customisation.md at all (grep-verified) — the ticket stays open for that. Family: #4840 (same knob, GBP side), #5261 (cent-scale tariffs through the same gates), #5608 (currency symbol display only).

- **A money-losing-plan report: the reporter's own log discriminates retention from naive arithmetic, without touching the metric code (GH#5616, log-pair-grep-verified 2026-10-18).** Grep `Previous plan best metric is ... and new plan best metric is ...` (plan.py) and diff the pairs against the reporter's `metric_min_improvement_plan`: real deltas sitting under the bar = **retention** — the plan was right when made, in-day load divergence moved its break-even, and each recompute's improvement didn't clear the adoption gate ("why didn't it stop the losing plan" is answered the same way: the loss was visible in the plan's own prediction while the bar kept failing). Plausible-but-naive arithmetic instead = the horizon metric deliberately pricing the later refill (#4840/#5069/#5509/#5566 above and below). The #5616 instance: a fresh optimise found a better plan than the executing one, but every evening recompute's improvement (+2.1–7.4p) sat under the reporter's 5.0p gate except the one that executed. Replay-probe traps for this class are in the Traps section (GH#5616). The retention grep's second outcome shape — **a *negative* delta — is the lock-in state (GH#5686, log-pair-verified 2026-10-22, v9.4.2 reporter log + code read on 0eaafc6b):** a negative `Previous plan best metric is ... and new plan best metric is ...` pair repeated every recompute means the gate is irrelevant — lowering `metric_min_improvement_plan` cannot help anyone in that state. Mechanism: `should_replace_plan()`/`plan_scoring_pair()` (plan.py) re-score the incumbent under current rates, so retention is apples-to-apples but structurally one-sided — the fresh optimiser's candidate windows are rebuilt from *current* rates, so freeze windows planned into since-cancelled dispatch stamps cannot be re-derived, and a retained plan's windows are dropped only when they expire (nothing re-checks them against the rates they were priced from — the GH#4736 mechanism); the incumbent outscores everything the fresh optimiser can propose and self-heals only as the stale windows age out of the horizon. Amplifier to check in the dump: `metric_battery_cycle` — the #5686 reporter's 10.0 (default 0.0) is ≈ the logged +40p penalty the fresh plan pays for its discharge-and-refill round trip (arithmetic on their logged metric pairs; flagged to the maintainer as an estimate in the posted triage comment). One-shot workaround: any UI control event sets `plan_valid = False` so the next recompute skips the comparison (e.g. toggle `switch.predbat_active`).

- **GH#5633 (closed as dup of the still-open #3376; the same complaint as #861/#924 under the old knob name) — raising `metric_min_improvement_export` cannot remove an export the levels pass seeded (verified by a live replay on main d17f0e00, 2026-10-19):** the per-window margin gate lives only inside `optimise_export()`; the write-backs don't honour it — `optimise_detailed_pass()` adopts on `n_best_metric < best_metric` alone, and `keep_window_change_if_improved()` reverts a gate rejection whenever the export still scores better (removing a profitable-but-below-threshold export is itself a metric regression, and the revert deliberately protects an in-progress export from mid-peak cancellation, #4402) — while `optimise_levels_pass()` seeds export windows by price threshold + whole-plan metric and never reads the knob, so a threshold of 100 still left the 22:00–00:00 export planned at a worst-scored metric. The docs' "Export only if there is a profit" preset (configuration-guide.md) and inverter-setup.md's "set it high to prevent forced battery export" hold only for the detailed passes. Family: #5509/#5566/#5579 (the profitable-by-design arbitrage family).

- **Three different "keep" mechanisms** get conflated in reports, and only two of them can hold a battery up. `best_soc_keep` is *soft*: purely a cost-model penalty added when predicted `soc <= best_soc_keep` (the `metric_keep +=` line in `prediction.py`), scaled by `best_soc_keep_weight` and ramped in over the first four hours of the horizon. It never creates a charge window and never raises the reserve, so an automation setting it to 3 kWh cannot pin a full 8.64 kWh battery at 100% (GH#4961). `manual_soc_keep` (the web-UI `manual_soc` override) and weather-alert keeps are *hard-ish*: both merge into `all_active_keep`, which forces `keep_minute_scaling` to at least 10.0 - a 10x-import-rate penalty that effectively forbids discharging below the keep percent. That 10x penalty is weighted by the **import rate at the deficit minute** (`prediction.py`'s `metric_keep +=` line, mirrored in the C kernel), so a floor whose deficit falls inside a 0p import window scores zero and is inert there (GH#5162) — while the `manual_soc_max` ceiling is penalised by the *export* rate, so it keeps working when import is free. The plan table's ✍ "manual SOC target" marker is drawn from `manual_soc_keep` whether or not the plan met the target (`output.py`), so it records targets the soft keep never enforced; and an overnight 100% window that *looks* like the floor working can be a `manual_charge` slot instead, which force-sets `charge_limit = soc_max` on matching windows (`plan.py`) — check the `select_event`/`Rate override requested` lines before crediting the floor. Floor and ceiling live in separate maps: `all_active_keep` (floors, max-combined) vs `all_active_keep_max` (ceilings, min-combined) (`fetch.py`). `set_reserve_enable` with a high `reserve` is the only true hard floor. Related, because it comes up in the same reports: `set_discharge_during_charge` **defaults True** (`config.py`), and even when it is on, `prediction.py` still pins the discharge rate to `battery_rate_min` while SoC sits within 1% of the window's charge limit. That at-target hold is what `HoldChrg` means, and it is not `FrzChrg` - `is_freeze_charge()` requires the limit's percent to equal `reserve_percent`. GH#3200 (replay-verified on main, 2026-10-23) pinned the alert keep down further: `prediction.py` turns an active-alert minute into nothing but a keep **floor** at keep% × soc_max — no path raises the reserve and nothing stops discharge above the level — so "the alert held my battery at 100%" is really the optimiser's cheapest defence of that floor. With `set_discharge_during_charge: false` that defence is a daytime charge window whose target sits at the reserve value (freezing at whatever SoC the battery has — the freeze signature); with ddC true (the default) the same plan targets ≈ the keep level, as the docs promise. The alert `keep` is set per-alert in apps.yaml (`alerts:` config, default 100 when omitted, `alertfeed.py`) and a user's global `best_soc_keep` does not feed it. Probe note from the same replay: `discharge_window_best` no longer exists as an instance attribute — a replay probe reads discharge plans from `export_window_best`/`export_limits_best` (window `target` vs the packed post-clip level differ; see the replay section).

There is no *charge-start* hysteresis anywhere (GH#2513, enhancement — nothing in `config.py`, only predheat has hysteresis settings): the execute-side hold band is additionally gated on `inv_target_soc_percent < 100.0` (`execute.py`), so with a 100% target an inverter sitting at 99% misses the hold gate entirely and the plain `Charging` branch re-commands the window every 5-minute cycle while the BMS tapers. `battery_charge_power_curve` is not a way out of that, though it is *not* model-only: `execute.py` passes it to `find_charge_rate()` (`utils.py`) and that function's first return value becomes the `charge_rate` written by `adjust_charge_rate()`. It only *selects* the written rate when `set_charge_low_power` is on — with low power off `find_charge_rate()` returns `max_rate` unchanged and the curve shapes only the second return value, which `execute.py` logs as `real` and never writes. Even in low power mode the rate search floors at `max(400W, battery_rate_min × MINUTE_WATT)`, so the 0.0 taper above ~97% that `get_charge_rate_curve_cached()` honours in the *model* (through the `battery_rate_min` default of 0) never becomes a zero write, and nothing on that path stops the window being re-commanded. #2628 is the generic deadband request, so design any fix with it. Whether the ±1% band itself still produces GH#5083's charge/hold thrash on current main is untested — the gate exists in the same shape.

So pull the non-default config list out of the replay first and look at `combine_export_slots`, `metric_min_improvement_export`, `best_soc_keep`, `metric_battery_value_scaling`, `metric_battery_value_export_scaling`, `metric_cloud_enable` and the `load_scaling*` family before concluding "code bug". Saying "this is configuration" is a legitimate triage outcome — that is what the `configuration` label is for.

A cheap way to settle these: replay the debug file once per suspect setting, changing one at a time, and compare the resulting force-export windows rather than the headline cost. The cost column moves for uninteresting reasons (a plan that ends with an emptier battery always looks cheaper), so **compare the plan's decisions, not its metric** — and check a suspect setting is actually non-default before building a theory on it (`best_soc_keep: 0.0` and `metric_battery_value_export_scaling: 0.8` both *are* the defaults, and have each cost a session's time).

Two `get_arg()` boolean traps affect every component boolean arg, and either alone can look like a feature silently not running (both pre-existing and generic, surfaced reviewing PR #4997):

- **An explicit empty value defeats the default.** `key:` with nothing after it is YAML null; `self.args.get(arg, default)` only applies the default when the key is *absent* (`userinterface.py:287`), and `validate_is_boolean()` (`predbat.py:1446`) passes `None` silently — so the component stores `None` and `not value` treats it as off. A default-True flag with `key:` in the yaml is off. **Narrowed 2026-09-24 (PR #5188):** for component args declared in `COMPONENT_LIST`, `Components.initialize()` now resolves a YAML null back to the registry default (`components.py`, comment cites GH#5186), so this bullet no longer reaches component registry args — it survives for the larger population of direct `get_arg()` callers. The obvious global fix (a None arm in `get_arg`'s bool branch, which unlike its int/float branches has none) was deliberately left as a follow-up: `impact()` on `get_arg` rates CRITICAL, and since `None` and `False` are both falsy only bool-True defaults would change. The quoted-string bullet below is unaffected either way — `get_arg` returns a real `False` for it, so no resolver can distinguish it from an explicit opt-out.
- **A quoted truthy string coerces to False.** `'1'` or `'y'` passes validation (anything `bool()`-able) but the coercion list `['on','true','yes','enabled','enable','connected']` (`userinterface.py:319`) turns it into `False`. Unquoted `1` parses as an int and stays truthy — the trap is specifically quoted strings.

Bool defaults are safe from the int-default truncation pattern (`userinterface.py:307` excludes bool from `int(float())`) — that one (GH#4925, **fixed in PR #4938, merged 2026-10-08, unreleased at the time of writing** — keep the mechanism for logs on v9.3.6-) bites component args with float defaults like `solcast_poll_hours: 4.8`. Check both paths first on any "flag silently off" report.

## Test-order pollution (GH#5079)

**Fixed on main by PR #5102 (merged 2026-09-15) - keep the mechanism for older logs.** The registry loop now builds a fresh `create_predbat()` per test (~22ms each), so a test that mutates shared state cannot reach the next registry test, and `main()`'s own instance is reserved for the debug-file replay, random-scenario and profiling paths, which still share one instance. Before that, all tests shared one `PredBat`/HA fixture, so a test that mutated shared state and restored it incompletely (or unconditionally to a hardcoded default rather than whatever was actually there) could make an unrelated *later* test fail - and only when that specific ordering happened to occur, so a clean `./run_all` proved nothing about the next run. Every instance was spotted by manual review, not by the suite itself, and the entries below stay as the record of the class.

`coverage/run_shuffle --hunt --quick` still fuzzes the test order and bisects any failure down to the minimal (culprit, victim) pair with the exact `--test`/`--test` repro; see "Finding test order dependencies" in `docs/developing.md` for the other modes (`--bisect` a known-failing shuffle-seed, `--campaign N` for a frequency table across many rounds). The known-pairs list it used to record to (`tools/shuffle_fuzz_known_pairs.txt`) is gone — with per-test isolation the catalogue became moot. Historically `load_forecast_history` and `kernel_static_cache` were the two most repeated culprits, each implicated against multiple different victims, while victims clustered around `model`/`model_kernel`/`kernel_parity` because tight numeric prediction assertions are the most sensitive canary for *any* leaked state — that clustering was about test sensitivity, not where the leak originates.

Residue a "fails in the suite, passes alone" report should still be checked against: hand-rolled snapshot/restore of `args` in `test_inverter.py` and `test_agent_tools.py`, and the restore-to-default shape (the #5061 pattern) `soc_kwh_history = {}` at entry and cleanup in `test_calculate_yesterday.py` — currently latent (nothing else populates it), but fragile by construction. Throwaway-instance precedents (`run_debug_cases`' per-case instances and the `*_isolated` runners in `unit_test.py`) remain the pattern for anything that replays a full dump.

Two more shapes from the GH#5169/PR #5171 round (code-read plus one observed red run): pollution through **module-level and class-level** state can still cross the per-test instances — `test_secrets.py`'s race test replaces `collect_log_secret_values` in another module's namespace (restoring it in a `finally`), and the log-redaction pattern lock is a class attribute on `LogRedaction` — so `run_shuffle` remains the detector for that class even though the instance half is fixed. And a test that **raises aborts the whole run**: the registry loop's per-test call is a bare `test_failed = func(my_predbat)` with no try/except (`unit_test.py`), so an exception stops later tests in the same module and every later module — observed during #5169's red run, where a test importing the module under test raised `ModuleNotFoundError` and the tests registered after it never ran. Put a new module's test before siblings that import it, and catch the expected `ImportError`/`AttributeError` inside the test so it reports a readable ERROR line instead of aborting. Related flow trap: `coverage/run_pre_commit` is `python3 -m pre_commit run --all-files && ./run_all --quick`, so when the markdownlint hook fails (in triage flows, on the uncommitted GitNexus edits to `.claude/skills/` and `CLAUDE.md`), the quick suite silently never runs — a log that ends at cspell with no test output is **not** a green suite; run `./run_all --quick` separately from `coverage/` when the suite result is what you are claiming.

**A found pair does not by itself say whether the fix belongs in the culprit's test cleanup or in the production code the victim exercises.** The first pair found (`calculate_yesterday` → `prediction_batch`, crashing on `self.car_charging_battery_size[car_n]` in `prediction.py:768` with no bounds check against `self.num_cars`) initially looked like it might be a real latent gap, since a genuine divergence between those two in production would crash the same way — but `calculate_yesterday()` turned out not to touch `car_charging_battery_size` at all, so that one traced back to test-fixture state, not a live code path. Check both before writing a fix.

Unknown apps.yaml keys are **silently ignored** — `validate_config()` (`predbat.py:1459`) iterates over `APPS_SCHEMA` checking required-ness and type and never the reverse, so a typo'd or obsolete key produces no diagnostic at all. The nearest thing to a net is `unmatched_args` (`userinterface.py:1209`), which only captures args whose `re:` regex matched nothing (GH#4999 is the enhancement request for a warning). If that warning ever gets implemented, the allowlist cannot be `APPS_SCHEMA` alone: component yaml blocks (`octopus:`, `web:`, …) are validated via `COMPONENT_LIST` (`components.py:56`), Predbat injects derived args at runtime via `set_arg()` (so the check must read the startup snapshot `args_from_apps_yaml`, not live `self.args`), and dict-valued schema entries such as `predheat` have un-enumerated inner keys.

Related, sensor-typed config items **reject fixed numeric scalars** (GH#2172, probe-verified): 13 `APPS_SCHEMA` items (`car_charging_battery_size`, `pv_forecast_today`, `car_charging_soc`, ...) are typed `{"type": "sensor"}`, and the `sensor` branch of `validate_config()` wraps a scalar into a list only when it is a *string*, so `77.4` or `100` falls through to the generic `not of type 'sensor'` error while a one-element list (`- 77.4`) passes. Two strings to search on: with `entries` resolving to 1 it is `not of type 'sensor'`; above 1 the pre-dispatch length check fires first and reports `Invalid type, expected list`. Impact is status-only (`arg_errors` zeroes the `config_valid` sensor; the value itself reads fine). Do not fix it by loosening the validator — the obvious wrap-any-scalar patch was rejected on the issue because several sensor-typed items (`pv_forecast_today`, `car_charging_soc`) should never hold a fixed value; the maintainer's direction was to fix the documentation to the list form, and that has now landed for `car_charging_battery_size` (PR #5024, v9.0.2, docs only — the validator still rejects the scalar form, and `docs/car-charging.md` now says to use the list). Start here on any "validator says X should be a sensor but the docs show a fixed value" report.

## The shared clock is rewound for ~0.8s every hour

`calculate_yesterday()` winds `self.midnight_utc` back a day **and zeroes `minutes_now`** on the shared `PredBat` instance while it re-simulates yesterday — the fake is the `# Fake to yesterday state` block, the restore the `# Save state` block, both inside `calculate_yesterday()` (re-anchor on those comments rather than line numbers; the refs have drifted several times). That is roughly 0.8 seconds, once an hour, on an object that other threads read live — components run on genuine OS threads, the web server answers `/api/plan_data` every 5 seconds, and HA select changes arrive on the websocket thread. There is no lock anywhere in the codebase.

This has now produced three independent bugs, in **two different flavours** — off-thread readers *and* code inside the fake reading its own faked clock — so treat "reads the shared clock" as a class rather than a one-off:

- **Manual overrides ran a day late and never came back (GH#4900, fixed in PR #4906).** Manual charge/export/freeze/demand slots and manual rate overrides are stored as relative `"%a %H:%M"` labels and re-decoded, re-rendered and written back on every read. The decode read `midnight_utc` and the render read `midnight`; a caller landing inside the rewind decoded the labels a day late and **persisted** the result. The same borrowed clock also broke the 48-hour horizon check, silently dropping any slot more than 24 hours out. The fix is local: `manual_times()`/`manual_rates()` now take their origin from `manual_time_origin()`, derived from `now_utc`, which nothing fakes — the rewind itself is unchanged.
- **PV forecast buckets landed a day late (GH#4804).** `SolarAPI.publish_pv_stats()` reads the same attribute live (`solcast.py:696`). The reporter's log showed `today` at 0 with every later day matching the previous cycle's one-day-earlier value. Not separately fixed, and confirmed by reading rather than reproduced.
- **History car slots shifted (GH#5004, fixed in PR #5025, v9.0.2 - keep the mechanism for older logs).** First instance of the **same-thread** flavour: `calculate_yesterday()` itself reads its own faked attribute inconsistently across ~350 lines. It builds `yesterday_load_step` with `base_offset = 24*60 + real minutes_now` *before* faking `minutes_now = 0`, then calls `yesterday_reconstruct_car_slots()`, whose per-minute scan derived `minute_previous = self.minutes_now + 24*60 - minute` — which under the fake is `1440 - m`, so car energy recorded at yesterday clock T was attributed to axis minute T − minutes_now, and any session before minutes_now vanished entirely (`get_from_incrementing()` wraps a negative index by +1440 and reads the *previous* day instead of nothing, `fetch.py:757`). The fix passes the real minutes_now into `yesterday_reconstruct_car_slots()` as a parameter (`output.py`); it touches only that formula and its call site, so the octopus-half consumers below survive it. Affects the History display and yesterday savings only; the live plan path is indexed correctly. The old e2e test actively encoded the bug — it built its sensor fixture from the same faked `1440 - minute` formula, so it passed only because fixture and code shared the error; and its `step_data_history` mock swallows `base_offset` into `**kwargs` and returns a flat 0.1 kWh dict on every axis, so the load-side assertions are axis-invariant and a partial regression that fixes only one of the two clocks stays green — while every test in the file pins `car_energy_reported_load=True`, so the flag-off (charger outside the CT clamp) no-subtraction path has zero coverage. The deeper fix already exists in-repo: a yesterday-axis origin object built like `manual_time_origin()` (`userinterface.py:1484`) would retire the faked attribute; note `publish_html_plan`'s internal alignment arithmetic is load-bearing on the fake, so removing it without threading an origin would shift the whole History render — the *reverse* of GH#5004. PR #5048 (`2499e865`, 2026-09-12) then extended the fix to **today-so-far**: `yesterday_reconstruct_car_slots()` now receives `end_record + minutes_now` so the reconstruction reaches as far into today as `yesterday_load_step` itself does, and clamps its final bucket to `end_record` because `end_record` is not generally a multiple of `plan_interval_minutes` and the overhang reads negative minutes that `get_from_incrementing()` silently wraps into yesterday's data.
- **Three faked-clock consumers survive the sensor-half fix in the octopus half of the same function** (pre-existing on main; PR #5025's diff touches only the energy-sensor formula and its call site; all bounded by the `car_energy_reported_load` load-cap clamp; code-read, not reproduced live): (1) `decode_octopus_slot()` caps slot ends at `forecast_minutes + self.minutes_now` (`octopus.py:2939`), which inside the fake is `end_record + 0`, so a dispatch that ran *today* before now decodes to yesterday-axis minutes past 1440, the cap collapses `end` below `start`, `kwh` scales negative and the `kwh > 0` filter silently drops it — the today-so-far segment of the widened History axis gets neither a car slot nor its load subtraction (the car-energy-sensor half of that gap was closed by PR #5048; the Octopus-dispatch half, this one, is still live); (2) the `dispatch_billed_off_peak()` completed-dispatch exemption from PR #4957 keys on `end_minutes <= self.minutes_now`, which can never fire on the yesterday axis, so a retroactively relabelled AWAY dispatch vanishes from yesterday's reconstruction (GH#4946 replayed); (3) the `kwh == 0 and start <= self.minutes_now < end` active-slot synthesis branch (`octopus.py:3146`) fires for any zero-kWh dispatch straddling yesterday midnight — bounded by the load cap and requiring an unverified data condition (an explicitly zeroed chargeKwh), so suspected, not confirmed.

So a "shifted by exactly one day" or "the value reverted / went stale for no reason" report is worth checking against this before anything else. Any new code that reads `midnight_utc`/`minutes_now` from outside `update_pred()`'s own thread needs `manual_time_origin()`'s treatment, not the shared attribute — and **anything inside the fake that indexes a history series must be handed the real `minutes_now`**, because those series are keyed from the real now.

## Per-integration notes

Grep for the named symbol rather than trusting a line number.

| Area | What past debugging found | Targeted test |
|------|---------------------------|---------------|
| Fox (`fox.py`) | The cloud API returns errno 42015/44096 for settings a given device does not support (`FOX_SETTINGS_UNSUPPORTED_ERRNO`); those are marked unavailable and never polled or written again. Entity type matters — WorkMode is a select, ExportLimit a number. Two later capacity/schedule traps; the first still live on main, with the fix drafted: `publish_data()` sums every capacity-carrying `batteryList` entry unconditionally (the `battery_capacity += battery.get("capacity", 0)` loop in `publish_data()`, fox.py), and an AIO ESS returns one physical pack as four `bmu` entries all carrying the inverter's own `batterySN`, so `soc_max` comes out at 4x the correct `batteryDesignCapacity` and `soc_kw` with it (GH#4919, read out of the reporter's own API response; a second reporter reproduced 40.96 kWh on a Fox **EVO 10-5-H** comment on #4919 — duplicate-serial payloads are not AIO-ESS-only, that model's payload unexamined). **Draft PR #5629 (unmerged at writing) moves the sum into `FoxAPI.battery_capacity_wh(detail)`:** no serial repeating among capacity-carrying entries returns the plain sum as before; a serial repeat returns `batteryDesignCapacity` × 1000 (which may arrive as a number or a numeric string); a repeat with an unusable design capacity counts each serial once. Until it merges keep every pre-fix reading on current logs — including "four `bmu` entries with the same serial means soc_max is 4x too big"; once it merges cut this to "fixed in #5629" and keep the signature for older logs. The discovery observer now publishes `battery_capacity_entries`/`battery_capacity_serials` ratings alongside (verified on main 2026-10-19) — neither `len(batteryList)` nor the entry count alone can tell the bug from a healthy four-module stack, but four entries against one serial is the GH#4919 signature. Not verified: whether `batteryDesignCapacity` is the whole-system total on a multi-pack stack — that is why the PR uses it only when a serial repeats. `fox_automatic: true` re-`set_arg`s `soc_max` every cycle and `soc_max` is not in `CONFIG_API_OVERRIDE`, so apps.yaml cannot override it - the escape hatch is `fox_automatic: false`. With the Mode Scheduler deleted the scheduler `enable` flag reads 0 and `compute_schedule()` (`fox.py:1080`) derives the displayed charge window from the legacy `forceChargeTime` read, which Predbat cannot clear: `set_battery_charging_time()` (`fox.py:1009`), the only writer of that endpoint, has zero production callers - re-verified on main, where it is referenced only by `test_fox_api.py` (GH#4939). Note this is the *residual* half of that issue: the blocker the reporter actually hit was HA event routing filtering on the literal string `predbat` rather than the configured entity prefix, **fixed in PR #4962**. Unlike the self-referential HA verify trap below, a Fox `didn't complete got X` is a genuine failure - the read-back polls entities the component republishes from Fox Cloud. A separate trap on the **HA/modbus path** (inverter type `FoxESS`, not `FoxCloud`): the stock template binds `reserve:` to `number.foxess_min_soc_on_grid` (`templates/fox.yaml`), and that file's own mode table ties both Freeze charging and Hold charging to that entity. A reporter had `reserve:` bound to `number.foxess_inverter_min_soc` instead - a name that appears nowhere in `templates/` or `docs/` - so Predbat never read or wrote `min_soc_on_grid`; when the inverter stranded it at 100 (observed sitting there six days) Predbat could neither see it nor clear it, and `min_soc_on_grid` at 100% stops the battery discharging while grid-connected. `FoxESS` is `has_idle_time: False` (`config.py`), so the `idle time is ...` line is bookkeeping only and never programs a demand period - discharge outside a charge window depends entirely on the inverter's own settings, which is what makes a wrong reserve binding silent (GH#4961). FoxCloud freeze export (GH#5015, **fixed in PR #5038**, v9.0.2 - keep the mechanism for pre-v9.0.2 logs): `support_feedin_first: True` makes `prediction.py`'s freeze branch model freeze export as genuine Feed-in-First — load exports up to the limit, only the surplus beyond it charges the battery — but the cloud path used to never select the `Feedin` work mode: `apply_battery_schedule()` (`fox.py`) only emitted `SelfUse`/`ForceCharge`/`ForceDischarge` groups and `adjust_inverter_mode()` hardcodes `SelfUse` for Fox (`inverter.py`), so the inverter sat in SelfUse where surplus PV charges the battery *before* exporting and the plan's freeze-export revenue didn't match the hardware. #5038 makes `apply_battery_schedule()` select `Feedin` as the baseline work mode whenever freeze export is requested; note the cloud API's verify read is slow there — a write can log success, read back pre-write 3s later and read back the new mode after ~18s (recorded in a `fox.py` comment), so a quick verify read is not evidence of failure. `support_feedin_first` therefore means two different things depending on connection method: the modelling is correct on the FoxESS HA/modbus path, where `discharge_freeze_service` genuinely selects Feed-in First (#4207, fixed by #4425), while #4582 extended the flag to the cloud defs with no equivalent execution path. Log check: a freeze-export day shows hundreds of full-day `SelfUse` groups in `Fox: New schedule` lines and never a `ForceDischarge` or `Feedin` group — and `ForceDischarge` appearing in a `Fetch scheduler V1 returned` enum list is **not** evidence a discharge window was written. Workaround: turn `set_export_freeze` off so the planner stops selecting freeze-export slots. #4182 is the neighbouring open issue. **The device MaxSoc setting is published but never bound (GH#5395, code-read on main 2026-10-04):** `number.<prefix>_fox_<sn>_setting_maxsoc` is user-writable with a working write-back to Fox Cloud, but `automatic_config()` binds only `soc_max`/`reserve`/`battery_min_soc`/`export_limit`, there is no per-inverter max-SoC arg in `inverter.py`, and the planner's only upper ceiling is the global `best_soc_max` in kWh — so "the plan models full capacity while the inverter stops at the device's Max SoC" is a real gap; a tree-wide grep for `maxsoc` landing only in the component (plus its test) is the tell. The writer hardcodes `maxSoc: 100` on baseline/filler groups and sends `max(soc, reserve)` on charge groups, while schedule reads derive the published entity with **max across groups** — the reporter's device read back 80 against writes of 100, implying the device applies Max SoC on that write path somewhere (**unverified**). Family: #4832 (GE Cloud floor-side sibling), #2128 (the same ask as a user input number), #3837 (FoxESS modbus: the plan charge target is never written to the device max_soc entity); do not confuse with `manual_soc_max` (a time-of-day manual percent override, PR #4841). **GH#3398 — fixed in PR #5623 (merged 2026-10-24):** the ForceCharge branch of `apply_battery_schedule()` used to send `"fdSoc": 100` with the charge target only in `maxSoc`, while the read side in `compute_schedule()` keys the charge target off `maxSoc` and the discharge target off `fdSoc` — so a pre-#5623 "Fox charges past the planned ForceCharge target" report starts at that write (reporter-sourced, no live Fox API here), and pre-#5623 logs show `fdSoc: 100` on ForceCharge groups — that value is the bug, not a user setting. The fix sends `max(soc, reserve)` in all three charge-branch fields and deliberately leaves the read side alone. The other `"fdSoc": 100` literals in `compute_schedule()` (unused-slot placeholders and the legacy `forceChargeTime` slots) are read-side only and never written, so grepping them up during a fix PR is not a second bug. **A hold/freeze can drain-block through the shipped template's `charge_freeze_service` → "Back-up" mapping (GH#5704, verified 2026-10-23 against main 333c358c read + the reporter's v9.4.3 log/dump; the mapping itself re-verified in `templates/fox.yaml` on 2026-10-26):** the hold-above-target pass (`execute.py`'s `Hold charging as SoC ... is above target SoC` branch) sets reserve = target+1, disables the charge window and marks the inverter charging, and the charge-immediate block then calls `adjust_charge_immediate(..., freeze=True)` in both branches (`execute.py`) — including when `charge_limit_best[0]` is not a planner freeze — so `charge_freeze_service` runs; on FoxESS that selects `select.foxess_work_mode = "Back-up"`, a mode in which the battery supplies no house load at all, defeating the hold's reserve-based drain (the reporter's battery sat unchanged for an hour while the plan predicted a drain to 1.82 kWh; alerts are not required — a pre-alert 03:00 Back-up write was in the log; log greps: `charge_freeze_service` and `Back-up` preceding the complaint). Config workaround: removing `charge_freeze_service` makes the call fall back to `charge_stop_service` (Self Use). Suspected, not fixed: passing `freeze=True` from the charge-immediate block only for genuine planner freezes — #3200 (the alert keep rendered as a freeze window) is the complementary half of that fix. Review-convention (d) in the Traps below is the PR-review mirror of this trap. | `fox_api`, `fox_oauth` |
| Solis (`solis.py`) | `SOLIS_CID_STORAGE_MODE = 636` is Modbus 43110, a bit mask. On firmware "4B and above" (what `is_tou_v2_mode()` detects: CID 6798 reads 43605) the timed charge/discharge enable moved to the per-slot registers — `SOLIS_CID_CHARGE_ENABLE_BASE`/`..._DISCHARGE_ENABLE_BASE`, Modbus 43707, six slots not three — and every mode value carrying TOU bit 1 (3/35/43/51/98) was dropped; 35 became 33, 98 became 96. Such an inverter answers a CID 636 write with code 0 and reads back without bit 1, so a log full of CID 636 verification warnings is a refused bit, not a failed write (GH#4707). `set_storage_mode_if_needed()` decides the bit from `is_tou_v2_mode()`: never asked for on V2, always asked for and retried on V1, where bit 1 still *is* the timed charge/discharge enable. Do not try to learn it from the read-back instead — GH#4710 did, and GH#4774 showed why that cannot work: on six inverters the same write of 179 verifies minutes before and minutes after the one that reads back 177, so a single post-write read is not evidence of a firmware property. Latching it was also silent rather than noisy: the verify read refreshes `cached_values`, so after a stripped write the cache held the stripped value, the suppressed computed value matched it, and `set_storage_mode_if_needed()` stopped writing CID 636 at all for the whole 8-hour verdict — across an overnight charge window. GH#4239's "only retained while a window is configured" is not the whole story — it was refused with slot 1 enabled and its window in force. `read_and_write_cid()` re-reads once after `verify_settle_seconds` before calling a mismatch a failure, because the immediate verify read is taken about half a second after the write. On the SolisCloud select path specifically, **PR #5247 (merged 2026-09-26) makes a discovered inverter's slot-time writes run their handler immediately** so Predbat's read-back sees them in the same cycle — pre-#5247 the select callback only queued the event (GH#4875) and the queue drained at the top of `run()` up to a minute later, so the write verified against the stale value and logged a spurious `didn't complete` once per moved slot time (pass 1, V1 and the slot SoC/current writes keep their every-minute retry). Do not "stop the failed verify poisoning the cache" — `write_cid()` caches the value it *requested* and the post-write `read_cid()` deliberately overwrites it with what the inverter actually reports, so the cache mirrors the inverter rather than Predbat's intent; skipping that update would leave the cache agreeing with the write that just failed, and change detection would then never retry it. The two control paths choose the storage mode differently and it matters: V1 picks from `in_charge_slot`/`in_discharge_slot`, which are clock tests, so CID 636 changes value and gets written the moment a window opens; V2 picks from `slot1_active`, which is only "slot 1 has a window configured", so the mode is written when the slot is *programmed* and nothing at all happens at the window boundary. On the GH#4774 night the last CID 636 write was 22 minutes before the window opened, and the comparison night that worked had one near the window - so `claim_window_mode_assertion()` now asserts the mode once per window on both paths. Slot registers are polled hourly, plus every 5 minutes while `is_inside_active_window()` is true, so drift during the window that matters is caught in the same cycle it happens. GH#4774 also left a useful negative result: that inverter grid-charged from 20% to 91% overnight with bit 1 clear throughout, confirming the per-slot enables alone drive timed charging on V2 — so a "charge window never started" report on V2 firmware is not a CID 636 problem and needs looking at elsewhere. Separately, an inverter reporting `batteryType 'No Battery'` stays in `self.inverter_sn` for polling; `is_battery_inverter()` keeps control writes off it, which was most of the warning volume in GH#4707. That explanation doesn't cover every CID 636 report, though: a later GH#4707 comment showed zero `setting storage mode to` idle-mode log lines, and both V1 mode-decision idle paths always log that line — so a report with neither line cannot be coming from `write_time_windows_if_changed()` at all. The only remaining write path is the HA select handler, `set_storage_mode_value()` at `solis.py:2574`. Check which write path actually ran before assuming this entry's explanation applies. Separately, on inverters using `H M` time format (`GS_fb00`, most cloud subtypes) `adjust_force_export()` used to re-commit a stable export window every cycle — `is_hm_format` alone made `changed_start_end` true, so `press_and_poll_button()` fired on every cycle of an unchanged export window (GH#4709, confirmed live on main at the time) — and PR #4713 fixed the twin idle-cycle press where the times being managed came back as `None` and never compared equal to what the inverter still reported (GH#4712, #2328). **Both merged together in PR #4711 (`bc853a0e`, 2026-09-12, post-v9.0.2)**: `adjust_force_export()` re-commits only on a real schedule change through the commit-once ledger (`last_committed`/`commit_pending`, `commit_needed()`/`record_commit()` in `inverter.py` — the attribute this entry used to name, `last_export_schedule_committed`, is gone), and a commit is only recorded when the writes *and* the button press both succeeded; **the guard only became real when PR #5126 (merged 2026-09-26, unreleased at the time of writing) stopped rebuilding Inverter objects every cycle** — pre-#5126 the rebuild reset the latch to empty each cycle, so the guard was decorative and the every-cycle export commit (the #2328 ~288 TOU-register-write-batches/day signature) stayed live; keep that reading for pre-#5126 logs, where counting `Successfully pressed button` lines settles the actual volume. (The every-cycle `H M` time-entity rewrite remains, deliberately — #1529 write reliability); `press_and_poll_button()` takes a `side` argument so a split-button config no longer presses the unrelated side's button (which is what cleared `timed_charge_current` alongside the intended one); and `adjust_inverter_mode()` now sleeps 30s after a real window change as a GivTCP settle workaround, so expect a 30s pause per real window change on those types (the sleep is unconditional on any just-written export window — the v8.37.x `rest_data` GivTCP-REST exemption is gone since #4864 — GH#4180; open PR #5647 adds a narrow eco-toggle-only exemption — a cycle in which the eco toggle itself cannot be written (missing, a fixed value or a non-switch binding) settles nothing — narrow this on merge). Keep the mechanism for pre-merge logs, where the signature is a button press logged every 5 minutes inside a window. Away from the CID work, `automatic_config()` had an asymmetric arg-binding defect, **fixed in PR #5308 (merged 2026-09-29, v9.3.3) - keep the pre-#5308 signature:** it bound `battery_rate_max` - which fed *both* the charge and the discharge cap - to the per-device `max_charge_power` entity, while the `max_discharge_power` entity it also creates was bound to nothing; `inverter.py`'s `min(inverter_limit_discharge, battery_rate_max_raw)` then capped discharge at the charge limit on an asymmetric inverter, and `inverter_limit_discharge` could not lift it because the raw value was the binding constraint (GH#4940, confirmed against the reporter's debug yaml). The fix: Solis publishes a `battery_rate_max` sensor holding the **larger** of the two per-direction limits and binds `battery_rate_max` to it - auto-discovery still beats a manual apps.yaml value for that binding (`component_base.py:96-110`), so an apps.yaml `battery_rate_max` does not opt out - and binds `inverter_limit_charge`/`inverter_limit_discharge` to the two per-direction limits with `overwrite=False`, so an apps.yaml `inverter_limit_*` (an AC rating, a DNO cap) still wins. Separately, on Solis Cloud/TOU-V2 the per-slot current write is clamped by `write_time_windows()` (`solis.py`, `new_current = min(slot_data['discharge_current'], cap)`). **The cap's source was rewritten in PR #5314 (merged 2026-09-30, v9.3.3) - keep the pre-#5314 signature:** the cap used to be the *minimum* `sysCommand.max` metadata across all six slot CIDs from `cached_infos` — read here at the time as deliberate, but the metadata is actually a UI definition for a *list of model codes* (its `productModel`), and the cloud hands back definitions for other models: an S5-EH1P5K-L (model 3104, 5kW, registers reading 100A) was given 60A for its slot currents from the models-3101/3102 definition and 62.5A for slot 1 charge from models 3111/3140/3145's, holding exports to ~3.6kW (GH#5068). `slot_current_limits()` now ignores the metadata: the cap starts from the battery-side limit register itself (CID 7224 charge / 7226 discharge, read as-is - not multiplied by pack count - and `SOLIS_SLOT_CURRENT_DEFAULT_AMPS` when never read), and once `ensure_slot_current_probed()` has measured what the slots really accept a **measured** ceiling replaces the rated estimate (PR #5309's probe, runnable as `python3 apps/predbat/solis.py --probe-ceiling` without `--write`; it starts from the register and the sweep ends with a half-amp step, so a 62.5A ceiling is found exactly rather than rounded down, with candidates written as the inverter shows them). The verify-echo traps survive the rewrite: the number entity is republished from the local `charge_discharge_time_windows` cache, so the write verify reads back the *clamped* value converted at the **live** battery voltage (60A × ~51.6V ≈ 3094W, not 48V) and logs `didn't complete got 3094.0` every cycle — while a "Wrote N successfully now N" line can also be self-referential (cached == clamped skips the CID write entirely). Grep `CID 5967 ... is set to` to see what actually went to the inverter. The planner models `battery_rate_max_discharge` from apps.yaml/entity max and knows nothing of the clamp, so the plan is systematically optimistic while it binds; on a current build set `inverter_limit_discharge` to the measured cap × pack voltage, pre-#5314 it was 60A × pack voltage. #4220 is the same 60A clamp from the user's angle. Separately it built every arg list - PV, load and grid included - from the battery-filtered `devices` list (`solis.py:1456`, narrowed by `cd6e7c79`), dropping a PV-only inverter's generation from `pv_today`/`pv_power`; the PV half was fixed in PR #4923, load/grid deliberately left battery-only because those registers can overlap on a shared-CT install. **A string inverter that declares its no-battery state in *neither* place was still enrolled as a battery inverter (GH#5279, fixed in PR #5281, merged 2026-09-28 — keep the pre-#5281 signature):** `_reports_no_battery()` was deliberately narrow (absence of the fields = "unknown", never dropped), and the captured string model (product `0106`) reports `batteryType '0'` — the code batteryList uses for "No Battery" on the alternative firmware, not a name — with an empty `batteryList` and zero battery readings, while `batteryHealthSoh: 0` parses as `0.0` rather than `None`, so `automatic_config()` counted it as a battery and bound control args to it (the #4707 refused-write stream). Signature: `Configuring Predbat for N inverter(s) with batteries` with **no** `Skipping inverter … reports no battery` line and **no** `Including N inverter(s) with no battery in the PV totals` line, plus `Set arg soc_percent = [two entries]`. #5281's detection requires all of: no battery type named (absent, empty or the code `0`), no batteryList entry, `batteryNum == 0` **and** `batteryJump.batteryCount == 0`, and `batteryVoltage` **and** `batteryCapacitySoc` both present and 0 — an unread detail, a named pack reporting zeros, or a detail missing the `batteryNum`/`batteryJump` keys still counts as a battery (that omission is exactly the second #5279-family unit's shape: Solis Cloud reports the string inverter's `batteryType` alternating `'0'`/`'0.0'` on **alternate polls**, so match battery codes by value — `_reads_zero()`, `solis.py` — not by string), and a named pack is decided before the readings are looked at; a PV-only inverter now gets `inverterDetail` only (startup TOU read and hourly register reads skipped, publish stops at the detail sensors, event handlers ignore it) and takes its place in the PV totals. The #4923 PV-args split can now apply to such an inverter, which is exactly why it could not before: it never left the battery list. Pre-#5281 workaround remains `solis_inverter_sn` pinned to the battery inverter — which silently removes all PV generation from the plan, so on a current version the fix is the upgrade. **Grid-tied machines by model number (PR #5764, merged 9985960b 2026-10-26):** `SOLIS_GRID_TIED_MACHINE_PATTERN` (`^S\d+-GR\dP`, case-insensitive — S6-GR1P6K, S5-GR3P10K) reads a machine Solis names grid-tied as PV-only when top-level `batteryVoltage` and `batteryCapacitySoc` are both exactly zero, with **no** pack-count corroboration; a named pack still decides first and live or absent readings keep the inverter enrolled. The `machine` field is top-level in `inverterDetail`, get_state-polled every cycle and allow-listed into the `describe_inverter_detail()` summary log line, so it is visible in a reporter's log even on a version predating the fix. One more shape worth remembering: the entity-event queue used to drain at the top of `run()`, before the `first` block created the `ClientSession` and discovered inverters, so an event queued during startup executed against `session=None` with an empty `inverter_sn` - and was popped before execution, making it a silently lost write (fixed in `9fe1f7e0`). Three September fixes and one still-live trap. **PR #5089 (merged 2026-09-14)** closed the SolisCloud API-allowance family (GH#5087/#5091): `B0115` ("Datalogger offline or disconnected") and `R0000` ("Daily API request allowance exhausted") are now classified in `SOLIS_API_CODES` and excluded from retry (`SOLIS_API_CODES_NO_RETRY`) - retrying cannot change the answer and every attempt still spends one of the 200 daily requests - with a per-inverter datalogger-offline cooldown (`datalogger_offline()`) so one offline datalogger backs off alone rather than the fleet; and `automatic_config()` is no longer first-cycle-only: `run()` retries it every cycle until it returns True (`automatic_config_done`), so an API outage at startup no longer leaves `load_today` unset until a Predbat restart (the ValueError-at-fetch symptom this produced has its own row in the symptom table). Keep the mechanism for pre-#5089 logs: B0115 was treated as rate limiting (10s sleep + retry, the `ad26f95a` "Quick rate limit hack") and any R-series code fell through to `Unknown code` with the full retry ladder - an offline datalogger drained the 200/day allowance and R0000 arrived hours later. `automatic_config()` makes no API calls of its own - it reads only `self.inverter_details` - and returns False when details are still missing, which is the retryable case. **PR #5095 (merged 2026-09-15)** fixed the amp↔watt conversion voltage (GH#5090): `get_nominal_voltage()` now prefers `solis_nominal_voltage` from apps.yaml (the only stated physical property), then a live reading above `SOLIS_HV_BATTERY_VOLTAGE` (HV packs keep the live voltage as they have since #4493), then BMS charge voltage classification (16S at/above 55V charge voltage, 15S below - settings rather than measurements, so the result holds across polls), then the 48V fallback; the live voltage had swung 10-14% over a month on every sampled system, dragging `battery_rate_max`, the write tolerance computed from it and the read-back of every rate setpoint along. No workaround existed pre-#5095 - `solis_nominal_voltage` fed only the capacity path (`get_capacity_voltage()`), so telling a user to set it fixed capacity but not the rate paths. Diagnostic tell for pre-#5095 logs: failed-write readbacks at a constant ratio of the target (e.g. exactly 66/70) are consistent with write-time vs read-time voltage difference alone. **Still live (GH#5093):** `SolisAPI.run()` folds per-inverter failures into one `poll_success` bool - discovery failure, any inverter's detail fetch, TOU window reads, the in-window re-read - and `ComponentBase.start()` gates startup on that return: False → `api_started` never set, and the startup burst (discovery, details, `startup_reset_registers()`, the infrequent poll) re-runs on a doubling backoff from 60s to a 128-minute cap, so a multi-inverter fleet is held hostage to the least reachable device and every retry re-spends quota. Per-inverter success booleans already exist at every `poll_success` site, so the fix is contained to `run()`; the interim workaround is listing only working SNs in `solis_inverter_sn`. PR #5185 (merged 2026-09-23) also backs off `startup_reset_registers()` per-inverter with a persistent B0600 pause, and a refused startup register read no longer aborts startup — the try/except now sitting at the `run()` call site covers both triggers, the #5177 B0600 refusal and the SolisCloud timeout (GH#5202, closed as its dup: the traceback matched main line-for-line, the same `SolisAPIError` escaping the same call). Three Modbus-vs-component bindings and a mode-domain split (GH#5127/#5128/#5129, triaged 2026-09-17). On `charge_time_format: "H M"` types (`GS_fb00`, most cloud subtypes) `adjust_charge_window()` rewrites the charge start/end time entities on every 5-minute cycle of a programmed window (the format check in its write condition, inverter.py) - deliberately, the same #1529 write-reliability workaround the export side has - so "Predbat hammers my Solis with writes" is this first, not SoC-hold micromanagement: `adjust_battery_target()` is change-gated (logs `already at target` and writes nothing when equal) and the hold path's `disable_charge_window()` writes nothing unless the enable switch was on. The Modbus-path types (`GS`, `GS_fb00`, `SX4`, Sofar) have **no Predbat component at all** - every control arg is bound only by the hand-edited template; `create_missing_arg()` only ever *dummies* absent capabilities (a dummy `charge_limit` 100 when `has_target_soc` is False), so no INVERTER_DEF default binds a real entity, and a GS_fb00 user who misses the template's `charge_limit` uncomment gets a silent 100.0 target plus `No entity_id for charge_limit to write N` on every target change while the inverter keeps charging to its own default (GH#5129 - the signature is the repeated `No entity_id` line, not a startup error). On the Solis Cloud component the opposite applies: `automatic_config()` binds `charge_limit` with `set_arg_auto(..., overwrite=True)` (solis.py), so an apps.yaml `charge_limit:` pointing at the inverter's own timed-charge-SoC entity is replaced and logged `auto-discovery wins for this setting` - the first thing to grep a reporter's log for when #2328's guidance "didn't help" (GH#5127). Separately, Solis has **two mode-switch control paths whose mode domains do not overlap** (GH#5128): the HA `solax_modbus` integration (`inverter_type: "GS"`) writes a mode *name* from `SOLAX_SOLIS_MODES`/`SOLAX_SOLIS_MODES_NEW` through `alt_charge_discharge_enable()` and only ever computes 33/35 - the 64/96/98 "Feed-in priority" entries are dead in that table - while the SolisCloud API path above does use feed-in priority in its idle paths; so a "why doesn't Predbat use feed-in priority" report is GH#5128's gap on modbus and already-covered on the API component. Trap for any fix there: 33/35 mean different things in the two tables (`SOLAX_SOLIS_MODES` vs `_NEW`, the `solax_modbus_new` switch), so writes must go through the table-derived name, never a bare number; the reusable pattern is `support_feedin_first` (PR #5038, Fox). Check `fox.py`/`gecloud.py` for the same fold-everything-into-one-bool shape - GE Cloud still runs its own auto-config under `if first:` only, with no retry, so its self-heal after an outage is the pre-#5089-era slow one. **GH#5187, fixed in PR #5189 (merged 2026-09-24) - keep the mechanism for pre-#5189 logs.** Slot currents are now capped at the inverter's rated power (from the `inverter_size` sensor, logged `Capping slot currents on ... at the most the inverter can deliver at its rated power`); pre-#5189 the bound was only CID 7226 × count and the per-slot `sysCommand.max` metadata, so a 3.6 kW AC inverter reading 100 A at CID 7226 refused every 100 A slot-current write and left the slot enabled at 0 A, while the planner side never saw the AC rating - pre-#5308 auto-config bound `battery_rate_max` to `max_charge_power` (GH#4940) and set no `inverter_limit_discharge`; since PR #5308 the component binds `inverter_limit_discharge` to `max_discharge_power` and an apps.yaml value still wins (`overwrite=False`), so `inverter_limit_discharge: 3600` remains the plan-side workaround where the modelling matters. Implausible recovery SoC (CID 7229) no longer defeats the #4706 clamp: 0/1/≤over-discharge/>100 now falls back to `over_discharge_soc + 1` with a log line; pre-#5189 a 65521 read passed the guard and made Predbat attempt to write the recovery register itself down every cycle. **Still live:** the write-skip on a never-read slot current CID — `cached_values.get(cid, cap)` defaults to the cap, so a register the poll has never returned reads as "no change" and **no write is attempted at all** (`solis.py`, both charge and discharge paths); if a fixture shows no write of the slot CID, check whether the fixture seeded it in `cached_values` before assuming a bug. Test notes from #5177: `test_solis.py`'s first-cycle tests mock `startup_reset_registers` via instance attribute (`del api.startup_reset_registers` restores the real method); a canned `api.session` set before `run(0, True)` reaches the real request path; and do not patch `asyncio.sleep` to speed up a retried error — `_with_retry` budgets with `time.monotonic()`, so a no-op sleep just burns the real ~30 s budget across ~8 attempts. The hold lever on Solis modbus is window-scoped (GH#5152, code-verified): the only discharge-suppression write is `set_current_from_power()`'s `timed_{charge,discharge}_current` (re-asserted on every rate adjustment, #4415); the standing `battery_discharge_current` registers are never driven anywhere in the repo, and GS makes the timed write the *only* lever (`has_reserve_soc: False`, `has_timed_pause: False`) — so a hold's 0A binds only inside the timed slot, and a "hold/freeze doesn't stop discharge" report on Solis-modbus points at GH#5152 before any plan logic: the execute-side hold gates fire correctly, the lever they pull is what is window-scoped. (The "only binds during the slot" firmware semantics is read off the register names in the solax_modbus integration, not yet live-log confirmed.) Same firmware on the **solax_modbus** path: `inverter_type: GS` with the integration's plain "Solis" plugin on V2/FB00 firmware shows as `Setting Solis Energy Control Switch to 35 Self-Use from 33 Self-Use - No Timed Charge/Discharge` every cycle, the write "succeeds", and the next read is 33 again — a `Control interference: N change(s) in the last 24h, sustained on [...energy_storage_control_switch]` count in the hundreds. Charging still worked (SoC 28→100% overnight) but exports never ran: the discharge slot's times were written but its per-slot enable (43707) stayed 0, which the `solis.py` V2 time-window decode showed as `discharge_enable: 0`. Not a code bug — the user needs the solax_modbus "Solis FB00" plugin (per-slot enable switches, no mode 35) and `inverter_type: GS_fb00` via `templates/ginlong_solis_fb00.yaml`; confirmed fixed on a live system on 26 Sep 2026. Adding Solis Cloud credentials *without* `solis_automatic: True` on such a system leaves inverter 0 bound as GS while `solis.py` still runs its slot/mode logic with no plan — it logs `no active slot`, clears discharge slot 1 and sets 'Self-Use - No Timed Charge/Discharge', fighting the modbus path. **GH#5190 (verified on main): PEP 515 underscores defeat every numeric-parse guard.** Python ≥3.6 `int()`/`float()` accept `_` digit separators, so a junk register string such as `"1234567890_00"` (datalogger off the remote-control platform; polls fail with `B0063 "Lack of iot platform three elements"`) parses as `123456789000` with no exception — `parse_cid_int()`, `cid_value_matches()` and the raw `int(...)`/`float(...)` coercion all accept it, so every `try/except ValueError` fallback silently passes. Consequences seen in the reporter's config: `reserve`/`battery_min_soc` auto-bound to the junk percent (inverter.py raises `set_reserve_min` to it), CID 636 never converging (every-minute write + failed-verify repeat), and no `B0063` handling anywhere in `solis.py`. The same gap is in the core `get_arg()` float coercion (`userinterface.py`), so it generalises past Solis: start there on any "value reads as an absurdly huge number / the fallback never fires" report. Fix shape per the issue: one strict-parse helper (plain decimal only), range checks, publish `None` for unparseable reads, never write CID 636 from an unreadable current mode. Holds and freeze (PR #5265): `reserve` is now bound to the **Battery Reserve SOC** (CID 157, `reserve_soc`) and the row declares `has_reserve_soc` True - `battery_min_soc` stays the over-discharge SOC (CID 158). Solis only treats the Battery Reserve SOC as a discharge floor while CID 636 bit 4 (`SOLIS_BIT_BACKUP_MODE`) is on, so `set_storage_mode_if_needed()` always sets that bit: `set_reserve_enable` only decides whether Predbat *changes* the reserve, and with it off `inverter.py` plans against the live reserve, so the inverter must enforce it too (gating the bit on `set_reserve_enable` left the plan flooring at a register the inverter ignored). Reserve writes are shown in the cache and entity at once by `show_soc_limit_now()` and written from the event queue - non-slot events wait for the next `run()`, far beyond Predbat's 2s write verify. Hold detection has a 1% hysteresis (`SOLIS_HOLD_HYSTERESIS`) so the mode does not flip across the target; a hold outside a slot needs no extra grid-charging logic because outside slots the component already writes a No-Grid-Charging mode. Inside an open charge slot with the battery at or above the slot's SOC (`is_holding_in_charge_slot()` - a freeze charge, or a charge that reached its target) it writes `Self-Use - No Grid Charging` (`ENUM_SELF_USE_HOLD`: 3 on V1 where bit 1 is the slot enable, 1 on V2) ahead of the 0A -> Feed-in rule, which on V1 used to select Feed-in priority - exporting PV - during a freeze charge. **`Warn: Inverter N battery_scaling read as 0.0 ... retaining last known value` repeating every cycle (GH#5368, code-verified on main + `triage_test.sh inverter`):** `solis_automatic` binds `battery_scaling` to the per-inverter `_battery_soh` sensors (`solis.py`), which publish Solis Cloud `batteryHealthSoh`/100 **as-is, including 0** (deliberate, the #4494 decision); the `refresh_config()` guard (`inverter.py`, from PR #4500) retains the last valid value (default 1.0), so **the plan is unaffected** and the report is about the repeat — a warning whose guard comment assumed a transient outage but never sees a valid read again, an enhancement (one-time/backoff per inverter), not a planning bug. apps.yaml `battery_scaling` is **not** a workaround: `set_arg_auto(..., overwrite=True)` (`component_base.py`) replaces an explicit apps.yaml value with the discovered sensor binding, so the only config-level opt-out is `solis_automatic: false`.
