Product-code trap, probe-verified in that run: a fixed product can **renew under the same code** (COSY-FIX-12M-25-09-24 still publishing current-window rates at the old prices), so never conclude "a 2025-dated product can't hold this month's rates" without the API check. `self.mpan` is the **import** MPAN only — `async_find_tariffs()` sets it once from the first active import agreement's `meterPoint` and never overwrites it; an account with export has import and export as separate agreements with different MPANs, and the export one is retained nowhere (PR #4972 review). The saving-session/free-electricity GraphQL queries use `self.mpan` deliberately because those campaigns are import-side; any feature that needs to identify a *meter* cannot take `self.mpan` (PR #4972, merged 2026-09-19, adds `self.tariffs[direction]["mpan"]`). Symptom: two things that should describe different supply points coming out identical — that is this, not a deduplication bug. **GH#5144** (fixed in PR #5145): the REST tariff endpoints return overlapping `DIRECT_DEBIT`/`NON_DIRECT_DEBIT` rows for the same validity window and `minute_data()` writes each row over its range, so whichever row came last in the response won — and the order is not stable across periods, so the displayed rate could flip between variants from one period to the next ("always the higher rate" was an artefact of that ordering, not a property of the bug). `filter_payment_method()` (`utils.py`) now keeps one variant at the parse point on the component, day/night, annual and minute-data paths — preferring `DIRECT_DEBIT`, keeping rows with no `payment_method` (Agile) untouched, and leaving single-variant tariffs unchanged; null `payment_method` is the common case, so any future filter must keep nulls. **The daily cheap-slot cap is derived per account but enforced per car (GH#5215, semantics unresolved):** `get_octopus_slot_max()` (`octopus.py`) takes no `car_n` — it resolves the cap from the *account's* import tariff code via `has_six_hour_cap()` (matches `IOG-SMB` → 12) or a single apps.yaml integer — while both enforcement counters are locals (`slots_per_day` in `rate_add_io_slots()` and in `load_octopus_slots()`), and both callers run once per car, so each car draws a fresh budget: up to `octopus_slot_max × num_cars` cheap half-hours priced per day. Do **not** assume the docs' per-car sentence is simply wrong: the derivation is account-level but Octopus's own blog says "*Your car gets up to 6 hours of off-peak charging per day*" (quoted in GH#4830, the multi-car confusion thread), so the code is internally inconsistent and the intended semantics is genuinely open. Exposure is invisible outside IOG-SMB — uncapped tariffs default `octopus_slot_max` to 48 — so only capped multi-car installs can see the doubling; existing tests encode the per-car behaviour, and `octopus_slot_max` is read without `index=` so per-car configuration does not exist either way. **The IOG slot-confirmation strip removes the house's cheap rate too when a car's dispatch is cancelled (GH#5335, with the #5317 family - code chain on main 57ec7bf1):** with `octopus_intelligent_dynamic` on (default), `dynamic_load_car_check()` (`plan.py`) cancels every slot of a car that sits inside a started dispatch but is not seen charging after the confirmation grace (log: `car 0 is in a dispatch but not charging, cancelling its slots`), and `dynamic_load_car_strip_feed_rates()` (`octopus.py`) re-prices the house's view of those minutes back to `rate_max_base` when the cheapness came from the integration feed, **exempting the fixed IOG band** (`OCTOPUS_NIGHT_RATE_WINDOWS["iog"]` = 23:30–05:30) — so a house battery that had planned into the dispatch loses its cheap stamp as well, and PV10 re-prices the minutes at `rate_max` outright (prediction.py's `dispatch_gone` line, `minute > 30`). Version tell: the strip is v9.3.2+ code — a reporter's deliberate mid-log downgrade left the A/B in one `predbat.log`: v9.3.3 logged `Octopus Intelligent: removed the dispatch rate from 355 minutes of cars [0] which are not charging` while v9.3.1 kept the 6.57p dispatch rate (`Charging target 2%-100%`); workaround (semantics read, not live-tested): `octopus_intelligent_dynamic` off = no confirmation and no strip, ≈9.3.1 behaviour at the cost of #5229's low-load protection. Check the car side first, though: Predbat must *see* the car charging to confirm the slot, and a `plug_status` string (`eco`/`boost`) that never equals `car_charging_now_response: charging` leaves the car "not charging" through a real charge — check the sensor's own history before blaming the planner. Since PR #5725 (merged 2026-10-24) the confirm evidence is **"any car charging"** — `dynamic_load_any_car_charging_now()` (plan.py) — so on a multi-car site a car's charging-now sighting confirms a half hour even when the dispatch under test belongs to a different car (tests in `test_dynamic_load_car.py` pin it). **#5316 is the mid-dispatch variant, fixed in PR #5319 (merged 2026-10-03) — keep the pre-#5319 signature:** a car that stops part-way through a running dispatch half hour used to have the rest of that half hour re-priced at the day rate; the strip paths (`rate_add_io_slots()`, `dynamic_load_car_strip_feed_rates()`) now start from `dynamic_load_car_strip_from()` (`plan.py`) — the end of the half hour the car was last seen charging in, which is billed off-peak in full by Octopus — and the confirmation is never cleared, only outlived. Review-round refinements ride along: a sensor reading confirms a half hour only from 2 minutes into it, and the grace clock is not kept across a restart (saved state is judged on the skewed clock). The bounce symptom itself — the battery flipping Charge↔Demand while a car charges in bursts — is the supervision working as coded (GH#5390, log-verified): a non-"charging" reading counts after `DYNAMIC_LOAD_CAR_START_MINUTES` (3 min) inside the dispatch, the cheap dispatch rate is stripped from the car's still-future slots (house plan re-prices those minutes) and it resumes on the next "charging" reading — ground-truth with the hourly `Last hour: ... car X kWh` log lines against Octopus's own per-slot amounts before calling it a bug. **The low-load test path (no `car_charging_now` sensor).** A cancel via the load test needs two consecutive low 5-minute windows - the first sets `dynamic_load_car_since`, the next satisfies `timed_at − since ≥ DYNAMIC_LOAD_CAR_LOAD_MINUTES` (5 min) - and the evidence guard requires the whole trailing window to lie inside the dispatch, so the dispatch's own ramp window is eligible and starts the clock: one lagged/under-read window beyond the ramp cancels (GH#5461, verified by `git log -L` on plan.py plus the reporter's log). **A genuine cancel was fixed in PR #5462 (v9.3.7):** the live `load_power` total must now also be under the cut-off - with `load_power` unset the 5-minute figure alone decides; live load that is not low or has no reading makes a low window no evidence either way (it neither cancels nor resumes) - and the cut-off factor dropped 0.9 → 0.85 (`DYNAMIC_LOAD_CAR_LOW_FACTOR`, `const.py`) for a load sensor that under-reads while the battery grid-charges. Pre-#5462 signature: a 4.80kW 5-minute read against a 5.4kW (0.9 × 6.0) cut-off at 22:40 while the inverter reported 7410W of live car charging. The confirm/cancel band is asymmetric by design: the cheap-rate confirmation needs load at the full `car_charging_threshold` while the low/cancel classification uses the 0.85 factor of it, so the in-between band keeps the slot trusted but never confirms it. `car_charging_threshold`'s history-strip heuristic is skipped when a real `car_charging_energy` sensor is configured (fetch.py, GH#4717), so "lowering car_charging_threshold corrupts the load forecast" only holds without that sensor. **The completed-dispatch cheap stamp lives only as long as the integration's dispatch feed carries the session (GH#5413, reporter log + yaml, 2026-10-05):** `rate_add_io_slots()` stamps completed dispatches deliberately (`dispatch_billed_off_peak()` exempts `end_minutes <= minutes_now`, and the fetch merges `completed_dispatches` unconditionally), but the integration withdrawing the dispatches when a session closes removes the stamp retroactively (`io_adjusted` reads empty, `rate_import`/`rate_max` all back at the day rate hours later) and the plan-History re-render derives past minutes from the **current** rate tables (`history_to_future_rates(self.rate_import, ...)`, and `today_cost()` re-prices today-so-far), so the flip is the display of the un-stamped arrays - no persistence of applied rates exists (open GH#2785, PR #3340 parked); the narrower fix is persisting the stamped/completed minutes. A tell from the same log worth keeping: those midday dispatches were visible only from their own start minute, so a "cheap slot Octopus never offered" question on an *ad-hoc* dispatch is different from the overnight band, which is visible hours ahead. **A second in-family trigger — elapsed half hours of a live dispatch priced at base until the completed feed lists them (GH#5520, code-read on main 40b9ce28, 2026-10-12):** nothing promotes `flexPlannedDispatches` into `completed_dispatches` (#4114/#4115, deliberate — phantom cheap slots), `trim_started_dispatch()` (octopus.py) advances an in-progress dispatch's start to now each cycle and a cached planned dispatch fully ended is dropped outright, and the merge into `octopus_slots` takes completed (supplier feed only) + planned (fetch.py), so for the observed 15–60 min between a half hour ending and `completedDispatches` listing it those minutes sit in **neither** list — `today_cost()` re-prices them at base mid-day, then falls back (`dispatch_billed_off_peak()` bills ended dispatches off-peak regardless of location once the feed confirms). **Fixed in PR #5533 (merged 2026-10-14, v9.4.0) — keep the mechanism for pre-#5533 logs:** `octopus_held_update()`/`octopus_held_dispatches()` (`octopus.py`) now remember the half hour a planned dispatch was *seen running* in and re-inject it as a finished dispatch until a completed dispatch overlaps it or `OCTOPUS_DISPATCH_HOLD_MINUTES` (90) expires — a dispatch still to start is never held, neither is a cancelled one; the held record used to be memory-only — held half hours are now saved to Storage when they change and restored at startup (PR #5581, 2026-10-17), so a restart inside the hold no longer loses them. The GH#5520 row in the symptom table below carries the log tell and the test (`octopus_dispatch_hold`). **Poll-trim mutates the stored boundaries of a running dispatch (GH#5702, triaged 2026-10-23, still live — trim re-verified on main ef1432be):** `trim_started_dispatch()` (octopus.py) advances a running planned dispatch's `start` to the poll time and scales `charge_in_kwh` to the remainder, and the mutation travels: it is published as the `binary_sensor.<prefix>_intelligent_dispatch` attributes, read back by fetch.py and merged into the per-car `octopus_slots`, so `decode_octopus_slot(raw=True)` returns the boundaries only *modulo the last poll* — the last <5 minutes of a long dispatch read as a fresh short dispatch and re-price at the day rate (`dispatch_too_short()`), the tail minutes leave the cap accounting (`take_cap_minutes()`), `octopus_planned_running()` rejects the tail so the held half hour is not refreshed, and `octopus_held_dispatches()` leaves the held half hour out while a planned entry still reaches it; it self-heals the cycle after the dispatch ends. Dynamic-load confirmation is trim-invariant (the confirm key is the `round_out_to_period()` half hour), so a `car_charging_now` sighting in the half hour rescues the tail — the visible effect needs the half hour unconfirmed (no `car_charging_now` sensor, or the car genuinely stopped). The HA-integration path (`octopus_intelligent_slot` fed by the BottlecapDave integration) does not trim — only the built-in component does. Test-module gap: `test_octopus_dispatch_hold.py` never runs a cycle on the last <5-minute tail of a long dispatch. **The same trim starves the post-dispatch dynamic-load baseline (GH#5762, triaged 2026-10-26, reproduced exactly with a triage-probe on the reporter's logged floors):** `dynamic_load()` (`plan.py`) subtracts car energy only via `in_car_slot()` over `car_charging_slots`, rebuilt every cycle from the trimmed dispatches, so a running dispatch's trim-tracked start covers only 1–2 of the 5 lookback minutes, and on the first cycle after the dispatch ends `load_octopus_slots()` drops the ended slot (`end > minutes_now`) so nothing is subtracted at all — car-scale load sits in the baseline and the plan drains the battery until the next 5-minute recompute. Symptom line is `Dynamic load adjust is setting load minimum` on the plan's "Home" load right after an Intelligent dispatch ends. Impact is bounded: with a `car_charging_energy` sensor `max(sensor, planned)` covers it, and the full-rate fill fires only when a `car_charging_now` entity is configured — so it needs a site with neither. The existing dynamic-load tests cover only car slots with full lookback coverage; a repro must build the slot list exactly as the trim leaves it (start = poll time, kwh scaled to remainder). #5702 above is the tail side of the same trim; #5602's "car energy lands in house load" is the yesterday-reconstruction path — a third mechanism again. **Account-dependent (GH#5544, 2026-10-13):** on some accounts `completedDispatches` stays `[]` all day, clean status-200s included, so that 15-60 min #5520 gap is unbounded there; and because planned slots are deliberately never promoted and per-device `completed_dispatches` is the only retained record (cached in `async_get_intelligent_devices()`, merged back each cycle by `fetch_previous_dispatch()`, pruned >5 days), a finished slot the feed no longer lists re-prices permanently for the day and is baked into yesterday (`calculate_yesterday()` reads the recorded `predbat.cost_today` sensor history, not re-pricing). A parsing trap in the same family: that account's planned entries carry a negative raw kwh figure, which `decode_octopus_slot()` abs()es (`octopus.py:3398`) - exactly what keeps them past the past-slot `kwh <= 0` skip in `rate_add_io_slots()`; do not "fix" the abs(). The current half hour stays covered because `rate_add_io_slots()` round-outs the trimmed start (`round_out_to_period`, octopus.py) — so the step is at past half-hour boundaries, not at the running one. The built-in component auto-wires only `octopus_intelligent_slot`: there is no second planned-slot sensor to cover the gap the way `octopus_intelligent_planned_slot` does on the Ohme/external path (PR #5443's shape). Log-comment trap: the `Elapsed minutes keep theirs - they record what the tariff charged` comment inside `rate_add_io_slots()` is written for the dynamic-load *cancellation* path and holds for the half hour in progress only — a reporter quoting it correctly does not make it mean elapsed minutes in general stay cheap; a future #5520 fix is pricing-only, never planned→completed resurrection (+ precedent: #5443 covering the end→feed-confirmation gap; #4917's trust gate overlaps the "keep cheap only where the car was seen charging" idea). `test_rate_add_io_slots` passed at triage but covers cap/eligibility/io_adjusted only — no trimmed-or-dropped-dispatch scenario. `calculate_yesterday()` rebuilds the previous day from the *recorded* `predbat.cost_today` sensor history, not by re-pricing, so any confirmation arriving after midnight can never correct a finished day's totals. **Octopus Intelligent devices freeze in place once the account leaves an Intelligent tariff (GH#5412, probe-verified on main 2026-10-05, still live):** `async_update_intelligent_devices()` returns before polling when the import tariffCode is not Intelligent, so the live-set pruning never runs off-IOG and everything built while on the Intelligent tariff persists - `automatic_config()` wires from `self.intelligent_devices` tariff-blind, the storage cache round-trips the devices, `fetch_sensor_data_car_planning()` keeps taking the Intelligent branch and rebuilding `self.octopus_slots` from the frozen entities' dispatch attributes every cycle, and `rate_add_io_slots()` keeps stamping completed dispatches (with the ~96h look-back) - the Ohme half flips `octopus_intelligent_wanted()` false on the new tariff but `charger_slots_wanted()` refuses while `octopus_intelligent_slot` still points at the frozen Octopus entity, so the #5401 charger-schedule mode stays blocked until the wiring is cleared by hand. #5405's owner re-wire (`car_slots_released_to_us()`) treats the not-refreshed device list as a live constraint on purpose - it refuses to re-wire off an Intelligent tariff, which is the same freeze acknowledged, not removed. **A related clobber to read before any "my manual octopus_intelligent_slot keeps reverting" report:** with `octopus_automatic: true`, `set_arg()` overwrites unconditionally and `automatic_config()` re-wires whenever the device dict is non-empty, so a user's own apps.yaml entity is replaced on the first run after every restart; since #5405 only the *empty-set clearing* respects foreign wiring (it checks the wiring is still what Octopus itself last wrote) - "the user is not affected" holds only under `octopus_automatic: false`. **Weekend Happy Hour / Power Up booking is not implemented (GH#5404, enhancement, 2026-10-05):** Predbat reads only the booked/allocated side (the free-session `events` attribute and `joined_events` with `event_type` WEEKEND_HAPPY_HOUR → `octopus_free_slots`); `available_events` is consumed only by the Power Down auto-join loop (`join_octoplus_power_down_session_event` plus the deprecated saving-session fallback, #4593) and WHH entries never reach it - they are skipped at the source (cannot be joined through the API - Octopus allocates them or the user books on the website) and Power Up entries ride in `available_events` at 0 octopoints, skipped by the min-octopoints guard; no WHH join service exists anywhere. If a join half is ever built, the saving-session auto-join block is the template and Power Up needs a slot *choice* among 2-3 candidates, with an explicit rule for weekend slots released Thursday that partly sit beyond the 48h horizon; the `events` attribute carries both available (code set) and auto-joined (`code` null) entries and the free-session path zero-rates them all regardless of booking state. **Free electricity has exactly one source, and past events are plan-inert (GH#5614, verified on main 063549db, 2026-10-18 — start `async_get_flexibility_events()` (octopus.py) on any "free_electricity (or saving_session) sensor stale/off" report):** the `customerFlexibilityCampaignEvents(campaignSlug="free_electricity", last: 50)` query (octopus.py) stores to `self.free_electricity_events` and the sensor's `events` attribute and on/off state mirror that list verbatim — the attribute has **no** end>now filter (the available-events half of saving sessions does filter, `get_saving_session_data()`), so a frozen attribute means a frozen upstream list, not a Predbat filter. **Saving sessions has a designed legacy fallback, free electricity does not:** "No saving session events from flexibility API, falling back to legacy query" is the designed path (no-MPAN or empty flexibility answer) and is itself diagnostic — the campaign endpoint is not populated for that account. The count line = API content: "Found N … via flexibility API" logs the decoded edges for `last: 50`, so N < 50 means the API holds no more than N events for that campaign+supply point; in #5614 the count (15, all 2025) proved the campaign data itself was frozen at 2025-10-25 while the legacy feed and the Octopus integration showed 2026 sessions — staleness upstream, not a Predbat filter. `totalCount`/`pageInfo` are decoded but pagination is never followed, so a campaign exceeding 50 events truncates silently (which end survives depends on the API's edge ordering — unverified). Past events are plan-inert: `load_free_slot()` bounds every slot (`start_minutes >= 0`, end clipped, and import slots apply `min(rate, rate_dict[minute])` so a stale event can never *raise* an import price, octopus.py), so a frozen historical list just means the plan misses current sessions. `self.mpan` (first active import meter's MPAN) is the query's supply point; a supply-point mismatch is the alternative explanation for 2026 events assumed missing (unverified lead). The API's response body is already in the user's log — `async_graphql_query()`'s log gate records it in full when it changes — read those lines before asking for new evidence (a long-frozen response's body may have rotated out of an older log). Poll cadences (octopus.py, verified): account/tariff 30 min, flexibility events (both slugs) 10 min, dispatch sensors 2 min — don't diagnose "never updated" from sub-10-minute gaps. Version-field trap: the issue template's "1.7.1" was the HA addon packaging version, not the Predbat release — place a reporter by matching main's exact log-wording instead. Companion #5615 (saving_session sensor on for a joined Happy Hour) is the other published-sensor problem in this area — separate feeds, separate paths. **The vehicle-catalogue lookups match by make+model *name*, last match wins (GH#5438, code-read on v9.3.6):** `async_get_intelligent_devices()` matches account devices against the global GraphQL catalogue (`intelligent_catalogue_query`) by name only — both the charge-point and vehicle loops assign on every match and keep the last, so when a model name lists several variants (zappi "(all models)" is listed at 7.4 and 22.0 — three-phase variants share a name) the last list entry wins and the order is the API's, not Predbat's. The device dict (`charge_point_power_in_kw` / `vehicle_battery_size_in_kwh`) spreads into the component's own `intelligent_dispatch` entity attributes, `automatic_config()` wires `octopus_intelligent_slot` to that entity, and `fetch_sensor_data_car_planning()` reads them back — battery size **replaced outright** when truthy (recalculating the car SoC), and rate via `max(rate, configured)` (the "Take the max as Octopus over reports" comment is an #2855-era line predating the catalogue) — so when the catalogue figure is too high, both the rate and the battery size are unreachable from apps.yaml while the component runs. Symptom: `Car 0 rate from Octopus is 22.0kW and configured rate 7.4` repeating every cycle; on an IOG-wired car `plan_car_charging()` is skipped entirely, so the bad rate skews charging-now modelling and battery-size-derived limits rather than the slot list. Cosmetic but reliable marker for this path: the catalogue returns `powerInKw`/`batterySize` as **strings**, `_clean_number()` refuses strings, so the coordinator drops both car `ratings` entries every poll with `value does not fit the container's type - dropped`. Test gap: `test_octopus_catalogue_cache.py`/`test_octopus_intelligent_devices.py` fixtures have a single variant per name, so ambiguity is never exercised. **Fixed in PR #5439 (b88cbd8d, 2026-10-12):** `catalogue_value()` (`octopus.py`) now chooses deliberately between disagreeing matches — `min` for a charge point's power (the most every variant can do), `max` for a vehicle's battery size — instead of last-match-wins, warns once per make+model+field (`catalogue_ambiguity_logged`), and the component hands on the **lowest** of differing power figures (`fetch.py`'s "Car N rate from Octopus" path notes the choice). **A partial GraphQL response wipes the planned dispatches for a poll cycle (GH#5492, maintained-filed with a full log, verified on main d6985b6d 2026-10-11 by code read):** `async_get_intelligent_devices()` runs the per-device dispatch query with `ignore_errors=True`, and `async_graphql_query()` returns `body["data"]` whenever the body has one - the auth-retry and full-error bail live in front of that return, so a 200-with-`errors` "partial" response comes back *as data* and its `errors` array never reaches the caller. Octopus's KT-CT-4340 blip ("Unable to fetch planned dispatches") is exactly that shape: `flexPlannedDispatches: null` inside truthy data, which `dispatch_result.get("flexPlannedDispatches") or []` turns into a real `[]` that is parsed and stored over the cache - so the car's slots + cheap dispatch rate vanish for one ~2-min poll cycle, `intelligent_dispatch_replan_check()` sees a signature change and requests a replan (two extra replans per blip; the reporter measured 7/230 dispatch polls failing in a day), and with no `car_charging_now` sensor the battery hold releases while the car charges. PR #5250's fallback ("reusing the last known planned dispatches") covers only the fully-failed query (`dispatch_result is None`) and already prunes ended dispatches and re-trims the in-progress one, so the fix is to route the null/absent value into that same branch - the value being `null` rather than `[]` is the practical failure signal, because the `errors` array is not visible to the caller. **Fixed in PR #5493 (0bca948b, 2026-10-12):** `flexPlannedDispatches is None` now routes into that same keep-last-known branch (prunes ended dispatches, re-trims the in-progress one, warns `Dispatch fetch failed for intelligent device ..., reusing the last known planned dispatches`) — keep the pre-#5493 signature (one-vanishing-planned-poll, silent) for older logs. Completed dispatches are unaffected either way (the parse merges into the cached list seeded per device). **Kraken contrast:** Kraken's `async_graphql_query` treats any `errors` in the body as a full failure and returns None regardless of `data`, so its dispatch cache survives partial responses - the check for any future octopus-vs-kraken discrepancy question. The partial-response shape is covered in `test_octopus_intelligent_devices.py` since #5493 (the KT-CT-4340 null case). **The same null-is-not-empty shape hits other `ignore_errors=True` Octopus queries (PR #5750, 2026-10-25, seen in a live log and a production log, pinned by tests):** the devices list can answer `{"devices": null}` with KT-CT-4360 ("Device status could not be fetched") on one device's `status`. `status` is non-null in the schema, so one device's error nulls the whole list, and the identical query worked 3 s earlier. `savingSessions.account` can come back `null` with OE-0003 ("Temporary service error"), which dropped a joined Weekend Happy Hour mid-session (GH#5745). In each case `null` means that part failed, and the real "none" is `[]` (an account with nothing joined has `joinedEvents: []`). Pre-#5750 signatures, for older logs: a failed devices list is silent and the dispatches go stale until the list answers again; OE-0003 shows as `User has not joined Octopus saving sessions campaign` followed by `Octopus free slots changed`; and any single 504 on `get-flexibility-free-electricity` dropped the free electricity events. From #5750 each logs a `Warn:` line saying it is keeping the last known data or refreshing the known devices. **Still exposed after #5750 (review follow-up; code shape re-verified on main 2026-10-29):** `events` or `joinedEvents` nulled beside an error on `octoplus_saving_session_query` still flows unguarded through `get_saving_session_data()` (`octopus.py`) — `.get("events", [])` returns `None` when the key exists with a null value and the per-event iteration raises TypeError — unverified whether Octopus actually nulls these two, the same likelihood class as the `account` null that was only discovered by happening. **Pre-#5750 poisoning nuance (GH#5745):** the wipe was sticky — the caller assignments were unconditional, so after one partial-error wipe later plain failures returned the emptied cache and the post-incident log looked healthy while the day's remaining free windows were already gone; a 504 in the log *after* an OE-0003 wipe was the poisoning mechanism, not recovery. Separately, the SmartFlex device `provider` field is deprecated (2026-10-15, removal on or after 2027-04-15) — as of 2026-10-25 **neither** query asks for it: the Octopus one via PR #5750 and the Kraken one via the #5754 follow-up (`KRAKEN_DEVICES_QUERY` now fills the stored provider from make, per its comment). Log-reading note since PR #5428 (0dbcce38, 2026-10-12): repeated GraphQL requests/responses (and skipped Happy Hours) are logged **briefly** between full logs (`RepeatLogGate`, `REPEAT_FULL_LOG_SECONDS`, octopus.py) — "no response lines in a window" can be the gate summarising rather than the poll stopping; a failure logs the request in full. **Meterless export (GH#5595, fixed in PR #5597, merged 2026-10-18) — keep the pre-#5597 signature for older logs:** `async_find_tariffs()` used to gate on `(isActiveMeter and isActiveAgreement)` with `isActiveMeter` set only inside the meters loop, so an active export agreement on a meterPoint with `meters: []` skipped the whole block including the #3505 OUTGOING/EXPORT fallback and export rates vanished silently (log tell: `Adding import tariff` but never `Adding export tariff`). #5597 adds `isUnmeteredExport = not meters and isActiveAgreement and isExportTariffCode` (octopus.py) alongside the old gate, warns that Octopus may not be metering/paying export until the meter is linked, and its tests cover the shape (Tests 8-10, `test_octopus_url.py`); rates themselves need no deviceID (`fetch_tariffs()` works off productCode/tariffCode alone). Trade-off the fix accepted (reporter risk note): Predbat may plan export before Octopus meters it — a future report of unpaid export while the meter is unlinked is the accepted trade-off, not a new bug. Test-name trap: `test_async_find_tariffs` (test_octopus_url.py) tests `async_find_tariffs()` (account-data discovery — the #5595 surface), `test_octopus_fetch_tariffs` (test_octopus_misc.py) tests `fetch_tariffs()` (the rates fetching half) — the name overlap has cost time. **A withdrawn dispatch leaves the feed-published discount in place (GH#5663, log+yaml verified on main 2026-10-20):** every discount-removal path keys on the car's **current** dispatch list — `rate_add_io_slots()` stamps only slots that exist, and `dynamic_load_car_strip_feed_rates()` builds its minutes from `self.octopus_slots[car_n]` (plus the `octopus_intelligent_consider_full`-gated `octopus_surplus`) — so once Octopus withdrew a dispatch (with no completed record) the feed's cheap price survives everywhere: the feed rows carry both the price and the `is_intelligent_adjusted` mark, the strip has nothing to key on, and a zero strip never logs (the gate is `if stripped and stripped != ...`). Signature: the `removed the dispatch rate` lines fall silent exactly when the charge begins. Diagnose in the debug yaml: `rate_import_no_io` still cheap at the phantom minutes with no matching dispatch in `octopus_slots`, and `io_adjusted` marked with no backing dispatch entry. Predbat's `today_cost()` re-uses the same stale table, so it cannot settle the true billed rate — ask the reporter to confirm from their half-hourly usage data; and under the window billing rule (`dispatch_billing_window()`) the feed discount *is* the billing truth for the whole planned window and must not be stripped. Family: #5012 (ignore_unplugged survivorship), #5080, the #4516/#4946/#5316 trust/stamp family. **Since PR #5657 (merged 2026-10-20) a short dispatch is cheap only where the car was seen charging:** a dispatch under `OCTOPUS_SLOT_MIN_MINUTES` (5) is off-peak only in a half hour `dynamic_load_car_confirmed_periods` recorded (`dispatch_too_short()`), and the confirmed-set records a half hour only from `DYNAMIC_LOAD_CAR_CONFIRM_MINUTES` (2) minutes into it (`minute % 30 >= 2` in `dynamic_load_car_target()`), so a blip starting on or just after a half-hour boundary is never confirmed even though Octopus bills the half hour off-peak — the report shape is "a 14:00/00:00 one-minute dispatch's half hour shows the day rate although the car charged in it; a :13 one works" — and the 5-minute load average cannot rescue a one-minute draw (a 7 kW minute averages to 1.4 kW, under the confirm threshold). The new `{int: set(datetime)}` state serialises as `!!set` in the debug dump without a `DEBUG_EXCLUDE_LIST` entry (checked against the #5569 unhashable-key class). **A slow-charging car reads as "not charging" through the load-test path, and Predbat cancels real dispatches (GH#5706, triaged 2026-10-23 against 333c358c, all-default config verified from their dump):** with no `car_charging_now` sensor the load test needs load ≥ `dynamic_load_car_low_kw()` = `DYNAMIC_LOAD_CAR_LOW_FACTOR` 0.85 × `car_charging_threshold` for the whole trailing `DYNAMIC_LOAD_CAR_LOAD_MINUTES` (5 min) window — a 1-2.4 kW smart charge under the threshold's 4 kW config-UI minimum (3.4 kW cutoff) reads "low" the whole time and cancels (the live-load-must-also-be-low #5462 guard covers telemetry lag, not genuinely slow cars — tests 60-67 in `test_dynamic_load_car.py` codify low+low→cancel and pass on main). Unit trap: `car_charging_threshold` is held in **kWh per minute** (`car_charging_threshold_kw()`, plan.py) — a dump's `car_charging_threshold: 0.1` is the 6.0 kW default, not a misconfiguration. Lead for a fix: #5318 (use `car_charging_energy` as dispatch evidence); workarounds: a real `car_charging_now` entity (the sensor path bypasses the load test) or `octopus_intelligent_dynamic` off. And the plan-table car kWh `?` marker is deliberate — `car_charge_slot_kwh_cancelled()`/`kwh_cancelled` (output.py): it marks a slot *Predbat itself cancelled*, so have the reporter find the `Octopus Intelligent: car N ...` lines before treating it as slot uncertainty (the cancelled kWh sit in `slot["kwh_cancelled"]`). **Day/night DST is fixed (PR #5655, merged 2026-10-20) — keep the mechanism for older logs:** the recurring late-October/March `octopus_url`::`day_night_rates` failure (`Expected 16 rate entries (8 days), got 15`) was a product bug, not a test-only one — `async_get_day_night_rates()` built each day's anchor with `local_midnight + timedelta(days=…)` and re-normalised the pytz-localised result, so on the GMT day after the autumn change `26 Oct 00:00+01:00` normalised to `25 Oct 23:00` and `replace(hour=0)` pulled the anchor back a day, duplicating a night date and collapsing the 16 windows to 15 (the dropped-window day-gap check then skips because the final pair overlaps). The fix builds local windows naive and localises each end (`_localise_wall_clock()`), keeping 16 contiguous windows; the same bug was what ran `octopus_url` red at every PR for ~6 days each autumn/spring (it clears by itself after the change). pytz technique for any future DST work here: a localised pytz tzinfo is a specific-offset instance that **still carries the zone's full transition table**, so `tz.localize()` on a datetime holding a specific-offset tzinfo still honours DST rules (raises Ambiguous/NonExistent, and `is_dst=True` on an ambiguous wall time returns the earliest-by-UTC occurrence) — do not "simplify" it to `replace(tzinfo=…)`, which is the wrong-era-offset bug for pytz zones (only zoneinfo-style zones want `replace()`). **Since PR #5639 (merged 2026-10-24) the cap is enforced on one shared per-account budget in clock time** (`octopus_account_slots()`, octopus.py — re-verified on main), flipping the GH#5215 enforcement: `octopus_account_slots()` builds the day budget from *every* configured car's slots in both `rate_add_io_slots()` and `load_octopus_slots()` (rebuilt from scratch on each call, order-matched so the two grant the same cheap minutes), deliberately leaving the plan side counting a cancelled car's (`dynamic_load_car_strip_from()`) and surplus minutes while the rates side withholds them — so a multi-car "car plan shows a slot where the rates aren't cheap (or vice versa)" report has a cross-car cap cause in addition to the per-car period-stamping one; keep the pre-#5639 per-car reading for older logs (the 6-hour cap's per-account semantics remains an Octopus-unconfirmed assumption — if per-car, the shared-budget switch counts a shared minute once per car in both counters). **Third-party free-hours feed shapes (GH#5707, triaged 2026-10-24, configuration):** the two free-hours args and the saving-session arg read different attributes — `octopus_free_session`/`octopus_free_electricity` read `attribute="events"`, `octopus_saving_session` reads `joined_events`/`available_events` — so pointing `octopus_saving_session` at a free-hours feed (the third-party stevekirtley EDF integration publishes free sessions under `events`, deliberately shaped so Octopus tooling works unchanged) silently finds nothing: log shows `Regular expression argument octopus_saving_session matched ...` but the dump's `octopus_free_slots`/`octopus_saving_slots` stay empty, and external (non-Predbat) entity states are not in the dump — read the integration's source. One-pass log checks that localise the class: `Octopus: free events code ...` (decoded) → `Setting Octopus free session in range ...` (rate array written); collect-sans-apply ⇒ the horizon gate (fixed since #4931); neither ⇒ shape mismatch. Native free-hours support on the Kraken/EDF component arrived with PR #5730 (2026-10-25): EDF Flextras booked free hours (only `SELECTED` slots count as booked) published as a free electricity sensor wired to `octopus_free_electricity` — EDF-only, E.ON Next has no Flextras; Octopus-shaped saving-session support on Kraken remains enhancement #5291. **A saving-session state without slots is dead by construction (verified 2026-10-27):** in `get_saving_session_data()` the "In saving session that's not reported" phantom-slot block and the `Octopus Saving session is active!` log sit nested **inside** the `if joined_events:` block — `not joined_events` can never be true under it, so `octopus_saving_session` alone (state on, no events) can never produce a slot or log "active"; plan-inert, and a cleanup PR would fold them to the sibling level the comment assumes. | `octopus_*`, `saving_session*` |
| Kraken / EDF (`kraken.py`) | EDF Kraken answers a day/night-structured tariff with **HTTP 400** on `standard-unit-rates/` (`{"detail": "This tariff has day and night rates, not standard."}`) while `day-unit-rates`/`night-unit-rates` return 200 and standing charges 200 — where Octopus four-rate products (GH#4921) answer the same-shaped URL **200 with empty results**. So the "empty results, then data empty" signature belongs to `octopus.py` and a hard 400 to `kraken.py`; do not carry the expectation across (GH#5166, probed against the live API). A nonexistent product still 404s, so 404 and 400 are both live "REST can't serve this tariff" signals on EDF — probe the exact URL from the reporter's log before assuming either. **Fixed in PR #5167** (`async_fetch_rates()`): the GraphQL `applicableRates` fallback now gates on `KRAKEN_REST_RATES_UNAVAILABLE_STATUSES` = (400, 404, 410), per direction (404 covers both a private product and one retired from the REST API; the authenticated retry stays 404/410 because auth cannot change a 400), and only counts a failure when the fallback comes back empty without counting one — `async_graphql_query()` counts its own failures, so a caller that also counts scores 2 per down cycle; snapshot the counter around the call. The standing-charge fallback stays deliberately 404/410-only (`kraken.py` — "400 is a rates-endpoint-only answer"). Two traps from the fix's review: `_fetch_rates_rest()` returns `(None, None)` on a network error, so `err is None` does **not** mean success — success is `(results, None)`; the merged code stashes the public attempt's status and restores it when the authenticated retry hits a network error (pre-fix symptom: a private-product tariff's rates vanish for one cycle with no fallback log line, while an identical 404 one cycle later recovers). The day/night endpoints' rows carry the same `payment_method` variant overlap as GH#5144 — now **probe-confirmed on EDF too (GH#5502, triaged 2026-10-12)**: `async_fetch_standing_charges()` (`kraken.py`) reads `results[0].value_inc_vat` of the public `/standing-charges/` response with no payment-method filter, and live EDF returned overlapping current-period rows with `NON_DIRECT_DEBIT 61.186` at index 0 against `DIRECT_DEBIT 52.317` — so the published standing charge is the non-Direct-Debit figure (the reported 61.186p vs the EDF-confirmed 52.317p), while the unit rate survived only because the DIRECT row happened to sort later and `minute_data()`'s last-write-wins merge keeps it; flip the row order and the unit rate breaks the same way. `filter_payment_method()` (`utils.py`, PR #5145) replays correct on the live EDF rows unchanged — it is the reusable fix — and the GraphQL fallback `async_fetch_standing_charges_graphql()` reads `applicable_charges[0]` with its query requesting no payment-method field (whether the account-scoped endpoint can return overlapping methods was not probed). Suspect this first on any E.ON standing-charge-too-high report; not probed on E.ON's base URL. **Fixed in PR #5568 (4845a227, 2026-10-17):** the REST standing-charge response is now run through `filter_payment_method()` — keeping the DIRECT_DEBIT row, leaving no-`payment_method` rows (Agile) untouched and single-variant tariffs unchanged — so the wrong-variant pick is gone on current main. Also benign: `Warn: Kraken: Auth not available for find-tariffs` repeating on early restarts before the first `Tariff discovered` line is setup-phase, not a second bug. **SmartFlex dispatches ride the Octopus rule machinery with no provider distinction anywhere in the path (GH#5521, code-read on main 40b9ce28, 2026-10-12):** `_publish_dispatch_sensors()` (`kraken.py`) publishes `octopus_intelligent_slot` from SmartFlex planned/completed dispatches "exactly like Octopus IOG", so every Octopus rule applies to Kraken dispatches — `dispatch_billed_off_peak()`'s location test for in-force ones, `trim_started_dispatch()` advancing an in-progress start to now (the GH#5520 elapsed-half-hour mechanism), the finished-`kwh<=0` drop, and the not-charging cancel + strip under `octopus_intelligent_dynamic`. `has_six_hour_cap()` (`octopus.py`) still keys on "IOG-SMB" only, so a provider's own dispatch cap is not modelled. The `kraken_provider` apps.yaml arg itself only feeds the component's constructor (the registry's `"config": "kraken_provider"`, `components.py`) — the instance attribute `self.provider` stayed invisible to Predbat's plan-side rules until PR #5581 read it through `self.components.get_component("kraken").provider` inside `dispatch_billing_window()` (octopus.py). Kraken dispatches refresh on a 2-minute cycle (`KRAKEN_DISPATCH_REFRESH_MINUTES`), so an "observed in force" record is never more than ~2 min stale. Any E.ON/EDF "cost or plan looks wrong around EV smart charging" report starts here. **Fixed in PR #5581 (3ef4eccb, 2026-10-17):** new apps.yaml setting `intelligent_dispatch_billing` (`dispatch_billing_window()` in `octopus.py`) — `auto` (default) takes the window rule when the Kraken component runs on provider eon; under the window rule every half hour a planned dispatch was in force is billed off-peak whether the car charged in it or not (a zero-kWh completed entry no longer takes back a held half hour), slot confirmation never cancels (plan.py's `dynamic_load_car_active()` gained `and not self.dispatch_billing_window()`), `octopus_intelligent_consider_full` no longer withholds surplus dispatch minutes, and the held half hours are saved to Storage and restored at startup. **The rate sensors' published attributes are unbounded (GH#5534, enhancement, 2026-10-13):** `_publish_rate_sensors()` (`kraken.py`) writes the whole `self.import_rates`/`export_rates` list into the `rates` attribute, state = `len(...)`, no time window, and on a fixed multi-month tariff the REST fetch returns the entire active contract period (the 3-page REST cap is the only limit; `_normalize_rate_timestamps()` only normalises), which then **stops growing at the cap (GH#5572 correction, 2026-10-16):** `run()` reassigns `self.import_rates` on every refresh, so once capped the in-memory list, the 7-day storage cache and the sensor hold the newest ~page_size×3 rows and no more — the post-cap harm is the capped warning on every refresh (`KRAKEN_RATES_REFRESH_MINUTES` = 30) plus 3 wasted page requests, not contract-life growth (the reported 255 rows / 37.8 KB arrived through ≤3 capped pages). **Fixed in PR #5571 (61108741, 2026-10-17):** the published `rates` attribute is now window-trimmed by `_published_rates()`/`_rates_in_window()` to roughly −(forecast_days+1)d→+(forecast_days+2)d around `midnight_utc` — the full list stays only in memory/storage and the state is the number of periods published — so the recorder glob below is the pre-#5571 workaround. The FAQ's recorder section covers only the `predbat`/`predheat` domains plus named entities - provider-component entities follow `sensor.predbat_<component>_<account>_<suffix>` (`get_entity_name()`) and none are covered, so any future `State attributes ... exceed maximum size` warning on one of them maps here (the Octopus component's own rate sensor, stamped −24h→+48h, can also exceed 16 KB at fine plan intervals - code-read only, do not assert it from a log alone). `recorder: exclude: entity_globs: [sensor.predbat_kraken_*, binary_sensor.predbat_kraken_*]` is the workaround; exclusion wins over include in HA's recorder filters. Predbat itself is unaffected (it reads these entities live via `get_state_wrapper()`), and a publish-side window is safe for the consumer only while it keeps roughly ±(forecast_days+1) days: `fetch_octopus_rates()` → `minute_data()` bounds writes to that band around midnight_utc (`utils.py:1414`) rather than dropping past rows, so the Octopus rate sensor's −24h→+48h stamp (`octopus.py:2166`) is the in-repo precedent shape. Do not recommend the global `recorder: exclude: attributes: [rates]` variant - it strips the attribute from Predbat's own recorded-by-design sensors too. **Kraken REST facts (GH#5572, 2026-10-16):** rate rows arrive **newest-first** (confirmed from a real reporter paste in GH#5534) — a capped/truncated fetch keeps every future rate and silently drops the *oldest* history; plan-safe, because the sole consumer `fetch_octopus_rates()` → `minute_data(..., backwards=False)` bounds every write to a band around `midnight_utc` (`utils.py`), so an "X rate missing from the plan" report on Kraken should not start at the page cap. The REST fetch's page size is never set by Predbat (`build_rates_url()` returns the bare `standard-unit-rates` URL — `KRAKEN_RATES_PAGE_SIZE` serves only the GraphQL fallback), so the cap is 3 × whatever page size the provider defaults to; derive it from a real response or a live probe, never assume 100. **GH#5573 (standing charge, remainder carved from PR #5568 — which fixed only the payment-method half — 2026-10-16; still live on current main, re-verified 2026-10-18):** both standing-charge fetchers pick a bare first row — REST `results[0].get("value_inc_vat")` (first page only) and GraphQL `applicable_charges[0]` from a −1d→+2d window — with **no `valid_from`/`valid_to` coverage check** either way; the Octopus component's `_get_rate_for_time()` (`octopus.py`) is the in-tree fix pattern (skips future-`valid_from` rows, latest `valid_from` wins). Wrong standing charge is cost-reporting only — it enters the prediction metric only for the final saved plan (`prediction.py`) — so priority_low; REST and GraphQL standing-charge row ordering is unverified (do not assume newest-first from the unit-rate fact), and `./run_all --test kraken` passes with nothing pinning covering-row selection (GH#5584's point 3 is the same gap — fix together rather than opening a new ticket). **Fixed in PR #5737 (`_standing_charge_covering()`, kraken.py, merged 2026-10-24):** a row applies when `valid_from <= now < valid_to`, a null or unparseable bound is open, and the latest covering `valid_from` wins (non-dict rows ignored) — same rule as `OctopusAPI._get_rate_for_time()`; keep the bare-first-row mechanism (REST first page, GraphQL `applicable_charges[0]`) for pre-#5737 logs. The earlier in-flight covering pick, PR #5636, was still open alongside it at the 2026-10-26 flush — check its state before opening a duplicate. The contract any validity-window helper depends on is unchanged: `str2time()` returns tz-aware values only — the naive-string `ValueError` that `strptime_aware()` (`utils.py`, from PR #5653) now guarantees on every Python version — so a validity-window helper must compare against `datetime.now(timezone.utc)`, because comparing a naive `datetime.now()` against an aware stamp raises `TypeError` outside the parse `try`, and the skip-the-bad-row handler never fires. **Kraken auth/liveness (GH#5580/#5582 — the silent half fixed in PR #5583, c028cd4f, 2026-10-17; re-verified on main 2026-10-18):** `oauth_failed` latches on a genuine credential rejection and clears only on component re-init; a CDN/WAF block on the mint sets `token_mint_edge_blocked` and backs off instead — the latch is never set by rate limiting (GH#4850). Since #5583 `is_alive()` is `api_started and not oauth_failed` and `health_message()` names the login (E.ON Next began rejecting the email/password mint with HTTP 400 around 16 Oct 2026; the E.ON message points at `kraken_auth_method: api_key`), so a mid-session token death now surfaces in predbat.status. Previously the mid-session latch was invisible to every health signal: `update_success_timestamp()` ran on cache-only cycles (`had_success or self.current_tariff`, including republishing the tariff sensor from cache), so the 60-minute is-alive staleness gate never expired, `oauth_failed` fed only the account_status sensor and run() returned True mid-session. The fresh-restart path was always visible — run() returns False while `first` when the mint is refused or no tariff is known (`if first and (self.oauth_failed or not self.current_tariff)`) — that is the +10-min `wait_api_started` block, see the Component-health row. Log-reading signatures: a repeating `Warn: Kraken: Auth not available for <context>` with no mint attempt in the same window is the latch short-circuiting every refresh, not "auth works, token not needed"; `Token request failed with HTTP 400` appears only at start/re-init (the latch suppresses the rest); a 403 carrying a CDN-block body is rate limiting (backoff, no latch); and the failed mint's GraphQL error body is logged redacted (`summarise_token_errors()`, since #5583 — pre-#5583 never logged, so a reporter quoting a 400 body could not be confirmed from the log). KT-CT-1124 (expired JWT) is in `KRAKEN_AUTH_ERROR_CODES` now (it was missing, so an expired-JWT error used to self-heal only via the next refresh cycle) — read it as "refreshed and retried in-cycle" on current main. The public REST rate fetch is unauthenticated and kept working hourly through the GH#5580 E.ON auth outage — an "E.ON stopped working" report with continued `Fetched ... rate periods` lines is auth-side only; check for rate-fetch lines before believing the whole API is down. **Failure accounting (GH#5584):** `failures_total` is diagnostic-only — an `account_status` attribute, not a liveness input; the count-once guard for an expected-status GraphQL fallback is the rates path (`failures_before` snapshot, `kraken.py`); `async_fetch_standing_charges()` counts the REST error even on an expected 404/410 before falling back, so "standing-charge failure counts climbing every cycle on a TOU tariff" maps here (counts-once fixed in PR #5641, merged 2026-10-24 — keep the pre-#5641 climbing-count signature for older logs). Also **fixed in PR #5641 (merged 2026-10-24):** a 200 whose JSON body is not an object — an array, null or a bare string, which would have raised `AttributeError` at `data.get("results", ...)` outside the `(aiohttp.ClientError, asyncio.TimeoutError)` except clause — is now treated as a failed fetch with `Warn: Kraken: Invalid response fetching rates: expected a JSON object, got <type>` (the in-tree precedent for the class is #5619's non-object-200-on-GivTCP failed read); keep the pre-#5641 AttributeError signature for older logs. **EDF Flextras booked free hours (PR #5730, merged 2026-10-27, a4f2f408) — symptom → module for any "EDF plan wrong around their free hours" report:** endpoint `GET https://www.edfenergy.com/support/energyhub/api/weekend-saver/v1/{account_id}/{property_id}/ui/hours` with `Authorization: <Kraken access token>` (no Bearer prefix) and a `Accept: application/json` request; the property id comes from a root-level Kraken GraphQL `properties(accountNumber:)` query cached in the component's storage blob, and the fetch refreshes every 30 min (`KRAKEN_FLEXTRAS_REFRESH_MINUTES`) with every attempt stamped, so an off-Flextras account costs one request per period. Payload: `components[] → type == "BOOKED_HOURS_LIST" → props.items[].rawTimeSlots[] {date, startTime, endTime, status}`; times are **UK wall-clock despite being labelled UTC** (converted with `ZoneInfo("Europe/London")`), and only `status == "SELECTED"` (or no status) counts as a booking on main (`parse_flextras_booked_hours()`, kraken.py — the review's "presence == booked" concern was fixed before merge, so a non-SELECTED slot logs a Warn naming it; check that filter first on a "Predbat plans a handed-back hour as free" report). Future hours the screen stops listing are dropped — the screen is authoritative, customers move/hand back hours until the Thursday before — while an already-started session is kept until the day after it ends (`keep_after = midnight_utc − 1 day`; `ComponentBase.midnight_utc` is the **local** midnight, so the timezone-frame trap above applies) so today's history is not re-priced, and an active hour survives an empty/failed screen read because it is kept from `flextras_sessions`. Wire-out rides the existing free-slot path (`octopus_free_electricity` arg; the events attribute carries `{start, end, code: "flextras_…", rate: 0}` handed to `load_free_slot()`, which clamps to the forecast horizon): Predbat's free-session 3-day lookback does **not** apply on this feed. Replay CLI: `python3 kraken.py --flextras [--property-id X]` with `--config apps.yaml` prints property id, HTTP status, raw screen and parsed hours. Platform fact reached while reviewing it: the predbat addon builds all five architectures from `ghcr.io/hassio-addons/ubuntu-base:10.0.5`, so the system `/usr/share/zoneinfo` database is present and runtime `ZoneInfo` needs **no `tzdata` in requirements.txt** (the Kraken component is the app's first runtime ZoneInfo user; the standalone Dockerfile was not inspected — re-check before raising "addon lacks tzdata"). | `kraken` |
| Kraken VAT basis (`kraken.py`) | The two rate sources are on different VAT bases. REST `standard-unit-rates/` carries `value_inc_vat`; GraphQL `applicableRates` has a single undocumented `value` (live introspection on EDF, E.ON and Octopus shows `ApplicableRate` = `value`/`validFrom`/`validTo`, no descriptions) which the BottlecapDave-derived EDF integration (`stevekirtley/HomeAssistant-EDFEnergy`, `api_client/agreement_tariffs.py`) treats as **exc-VAT**. That is that integration's assertion — nobody has yet posted a raw `applicableRates` value beside a bill, so a "rates are 5% out on the fallback path" report is the chance to confirm it. Predbat labelled the value inc-VAT until the fix that scales it by the import agreement's `standingCharge / preVatStandingCharge` (both on the `TariffType` interface on EDF and E.ON). Dead end to skip: the Octopus public GraphQL reference says nothing on VAT for this query. GB domestic electricity is zero-rated 1 Oct 2026 - 31 Mar 2027, so inc and exc are equal in that window and a wrong basis cannot show; the GraphQL `applicableStandingCharges` fallback is still assumed inc-VAT and is unverified either way. |
| Axle (`axle.py`) | Export sessions have to boost the import rate as well as the export rate - introduced deliberately by PR #4520 (first release v8.48.2, mirrored on Octopus saving sessions), documented in docs/energy-rates.md, and upheld by the maintainer on #5060. Two reports of the same design now exist (#5060 closed as dup-of-#4277 with the by-design answer given; #5175 triaged as enhancement, no regression - `tools/triage_test.sh axle` asserts the dual boost), and **no config knob disables just the import side** (`axle_pence_per_kwh` scales both directions; config.py has only `axle_api_key`/`axle_pence_per_kwh`/`axle_automatic`/`axle_control`). The "textual plans / colour coding skewed" half of such reports belongs to the #5050 threshold row (boosts before `rate_scan()`), not here. State is published unconditionally from `run()` so a fetch failure does not freeze the sensor at a stale value. `load_axle_slot()` has no lower time bound - it checks only `start_minutes < forecast_minutes + minutes_now`, not the `start_minutes >= 0` guard `load_free_slot()` has (octopus.py) - so a closed Axle event writes dead keys at negative minutes through `rate_dict.get(minute, 0)` (GH#5036, replay-verified against the reporter's dump: the +100 boosts sit only at the two closed events' actual UTC start/end times). The dead keys are inert in the plan - `rate_minmax()` and the window scans start at `minutes_now` - so "a dead event projected forward as free export" is not what the code does. The comment at octopus.py:2859 saying `load_saving_slot()` and `load_axle_slot()` "both bound themselves that way" is half-wrong: axle bounds only its end, and `load_saving_slot()` also lacks the start guard but is harmless there because its inner loop writes only `if minute in rate_dict`. Two September reports extend this row. **GH#5060** (closed as a duplicate of #4277, the same defect reported a year earlier and never root-caused — #4277 covers Octopus saving sessions too): event boosts are applied *before* user rate overrides on both sides — `load_axle_slot(..., export=True)` runs ahead of `basic_rates(rates_export_override, ...)` (import mirrors it: `load_saving_slot`/`load_axle_slot` then the import override) — so a full-horizon `rates_export_override` rewrites the event minutes and re-marks them `user`. That reporter had no `rates_export` key in apps.yaml at all, so the override was their only export source, which is why the import-side boost survived while the export-side +100 did not. Workaround (maintainer's suggestion on #4277, checked viable against the code): move the fixed schedule from `rates_export_override` into `rates_export` — it becomes the base tariff via `basic_rates` before the boosts run, so the event stacks on top and nothing downstream rewrites it (assuming manual export rates are empty, since `apply_manual_rates` also runs after the boosts). Forensic technique: in a debug yaml, the `rate_*_replicated` dict discriminates the two failure modes in one step — mark `user` through the event window ⇒ the boost ran and a later override clobbered it; the `saving` marks missing while the boost is missing ⇒ the boost call never ran (or, per GH#5036 above, wrote dead keys). Compare against `rate_*_base` for the tariff's own price; same idea as the GH#5012 base/no_io/adjusted comparison in the Octopus row. **GH#5050** (shared with the Octopus row): the same boosts run before `rate_scan()`, so the automatic low-rate threshold classifies the whole day low — see the symptom-table row on `set_rate_thresholds()`. | `axle` |
| History fetch / memory (`ha.py`) | History is fetched in `HISTORY_CHUNK_DAYS`-sized chunks with boundary dedup — records landing exactly on a chunk start inside a data gap corrupted smoothing before that was fixed. The largest memory peak in a run is ML load-predictor training (`load_predictor.py`), not the plan. The same chunking is the REST-call amplifier that can trip the fatal API-error counter at startup — see the "Too many API errors" symptom row. | `history_chunking` |
| Minute data (`utils.py`) | The gap-fill in `minute_data()` writes `newest_state` into every minute beyond the data, and `newest_state` is the value of the sample *closest to now* — which for a **forward** series (`backwards=False`) is the **earliest** sample's value, not the last. A consumer that converts a cumulative series into per-minute deltas *after* the fill therefore manufactures a phantom positive delta at the minute right after the last sample whenever the series ends lower than it started (internal reset: `6.0 first … 2.0 last → +4.0 kWh`), and when several such series are summed before delta conversion a shorter-horizon sensor's reset drop can mask another sensor's genuine same-minute delta. On any "forecast/demand series is wrong near the horizon" report, check whether the path calls `minute_data()` with `backwards=False` and then takes deltas — convert deltas per-sensor before summing, or fill the tail with the last sample, at the call site (PR #4756 review, verified by reading the fill loop). | `minute_data` |
| Charge/discharge curve (`inverter.py`) | The curve is evaluated per target minute; tapering near ~93% SOC is expected behaviour, not a fault. An `IndexError: list index out of range` at `predbat_status_data[0]` entering curve detection right after a history outage (often preceded by the `Empty history passed to minute_data` warning) was a typo'd guard — `charge_rate_data` checked twice, `predbat_status_data` never — fixed in PR #2814 and first released in v8.26.2; the four-way guard is correct on main. Any new find_charge_curve IndexError report against ≥ v8.26.2 is a stale-version report (GH#2689). **A different, still-live crash in the same function (GH#5219, probe-verified on main 2026-09-24):** `find_charge_curve()` computes `average_power / max_power` with **no zero guard on the divisor** — `max_power = int(battery_rate_max_charge * MINUTE_WATT)` (discharge side mirrored) — while the guard that exists covers the *numerator* (`total_power == 0` break). Symptom: `ZeroDivisionError: float division by zero` repeating every cycle and blocking all planning until user intervention. Feed: `inverter_limit_charge`/`_discharge` bound to **live BMS rate sensors** (Sunsynk SK on the kellerza integration; user-set but sanctioned — `execute.py`'s comment acknowledges sourcing the limits from live sensors), and the trigger is a feedback loop: Predbat writes 0 W to `charge_rate` when a freeze-export window starts, the integration mirrors that into the rate sensor, and the next cycle's config re-read (`refresh_config()` re-reads live config every cycle since the #5126 object-persistence change; pre-#5126 the per-cycle `__init__` rebuild did the same) recomputes `battery_rate_max_charge = min(0, ...)` → division crashes → `update_time_loop` catches → no plan → the rate is never restored → repeats (21 crashes in 40 min observed). **A guard only in `find_charge_curve()` is not enough:** `adjust_battery_target_multi()` (`execute.py`) divides `inverter.battery_rate_max_charge / self.battery_rate_max_charge`, where the base rate is the sum of per-inverter rates, so a zeroed single inverter can crash there too. Do not merge this with the #2689 IndexError. Version trap from the same issue: the reporter's quoted traceback line (1284) resolved to v9.0.3 because their log showed a restart *downgrading mid-incident* (v9.1.0 → v9.0.3) — check which version actually ran at each timestamp before quoting line numbers. *Suspected, not verified:* a BMS that tapers its reported max charge current to 0 near full SoC hits the same crash with no Predbat write involved (the same guard covers it). **A single bad SoC sample could stamp one low value across 1-86% of the learned curve (GH#5259, fixed in PR #5592, 2026-10-18 — keep the mechanism for logs on v9.4.3-):** the curve walk-back from each charge starting point stored one averaged `charge_curve` value across every SoC between readings and broke only on status/rate/power with no SoC sanity check, so a stray 0% reading (a GivTCP failed read, GH#1889) or an interior history gap read as 0 (`soc_percent` is built with `soc_kwh.get(minute, 0)`, and `minute_data(backwards=True, smoothing=False)` fills only the span *between* consecutive updates, so a gap reads `calc_percent_limit(0, soc_max)`) covered `1..previous_point-1` in one hit. The poison window was narrow rather than anywhere in the charge — the walk stops at the *first* sample below the starting point, so a glitch only poisoned when it landed exactly there (~14 such slots per full-rate charge), which is why days of intermittent sensor failures could pass first. #5592 adds `CHARGE_CURVE_MAX_SOC_STEP` (10 percentage points): a missing or implausible SoC jump between consecutive readings breaks the walk, and `test_find_charge_curve.py` pins both vectors (the stray 0% and the missing minute). Lifecycle facts that survive the fix: the learner runs only while `battery_charge_power_curve` is empty and only `reset()` empties it, so a curve learned by an unguarded version persists until restart, and `get_curve_value()` defaults missing points to 1.0, so the flat-low fill changes predicted charge power. **The learner is gated to inverter 0 at the caller — per-inverter curves are a model rework, not a flag (GH#5442, code-read on v9.3.6):** in `execute.py` both the charge and discharge branches start `if id == 0 and (not self.computed_charge_curve or ...) and not self.battery_charge_power_curve`, a gate as old as the feature itself (#683/#690, Feb 2024 — design lineage, not a regression). `find_charge_curve()` itself is fully per-inverter (reads `soc_percent`/`soc_kw`/rates/`battery_power` at `index=self.id`, logging `Inverter N Looking for ... curve with sensors ...`), so per-inverter sensor args being configured ≠ per-inverter learning happening — the log discriminator: `Inverter 1 Looking for` lines present means the gate is NOT the problem (that is the history-missing branch, `Cannot find ...`); absent for inverter 1 while present for inverter 0 means the gate is. Only inverter 0's history is searched, so a full-rate cycle that only exists on inverter N>0 can never be found. The learned curve is a **single fleet-wide value**: the config item is read once via `self.args.get("battery_charge_power_curve")` in `fetch.py` (cannot be configured per inverter), stored once on the PredBat attribute, fed into the prediction as one list, and looked up at **combined fleet SoC%** in the single-combined-battery simulation — so per-inverter curves need the plan model to simulate one battery per inverter (a rework; the design-consistent alternative is aggregating history across inverters; do not frame "per-inverter curves" as small). Auto-mode signature: the learner re-runs every cycle while `auto` is on and no curve is found (the `Stop retrying every cycle` comment marks the *non-auto* branch as the one that stops), so a repeating `Cannot find battery discharge curve (no full rate discharge cycle to empty found in history)` every 5 minutes is the normal auto-mode signature, not a fault loop. Debug-yaml trap: `battery_charge_power_curve: auto` appearing twice (under `args:` and under `args_from_apps_yaml:`) is two copies of the same *global* arg, not per-inverter sections — the per-inverter config is the list args next to them. **The sibling ask — deliverability of a plan on a mixed-sizes fleet — is the same single-battery prediction model (GH#5508, closed as dup of #830, 2026-10-12):** `prediction.py` simulates one combined battery (the discharge floor `battery_to_min = max(soc - reserve_expected, 0)` runs on *fleet* SoC against the *fleet* reserve with the fleet curve, prediction.py) while `execute.py`'s published fleet totals are plain sums of the per-inverter values — so both charge-side under-booking (a faster inverter fills first and drops the aggregate rate) and the discharge mirror (one member hits its own reserve and the export window is over-booked) are model gaps, not executor bugs. Dup target #830 (the maintainer's "meaningful improvement needs modelling the inverters separately"), umbrella #4856 (F1/F2/F3/F11), per-inverter curves #5442 above; ranked fix direction: per-inverter SoC in the prediction > proportional-share approximation. Class-check before calling dup: `battery_to_min` on fleet SoC plus the `execute.py` fleet sums are the two greps — if a per-inverter prediction rework has landed, new reports are bugs against that rework rather than duplicates of #830. Dup-close routing (GH#5628, closed as dup of #4856, 2026-10-19): a "the plan books more charge/discharge power or energy than the fleet can actually deliver" report dup-closes to **#4856** with **#830** named as the original report, linking the live precedent already in that thread (a discharge case where members' reserves differ on purpose, peter1rhodes 2026-10-12). Re-checkable proof point before each close: the fleet aggregates are still plain `+=` sums over `self.inverters` in `execute.py` (`soc_max`/`soc_kw`/`reserve`/`battery_rate_max_charge`/`battery_rate_max_discharge`) — once any #4856 strand merges, re-grep those before treating new reports as duplicate-closes. **Battery temperature curve settings are apps.yaml-only keys (GH#5630, verified by grep on main, 2026-10-19):** `battery_temperature_charge_curve`/`battery_temperature_discharge_curve` are **not** in `CONFIG_ITEMS` — grepping `config.py` alone falsely suggests they don't exist (that detour cost real time on #5630); they are plain apps.yaml keys read and validated at `fetch.py`'s `validate_curve()`, and the prediction applies the temperature caps on top of the SoC power curves through `get_charge_rate_curve_cached()`/`get_discharge_rate_curve_cached()` (mirrored in the kernel). By contrast `battery_charge_power_curve`/`battery_discharge_power_curve` *are* CONFIG_ITEMS. **The missing-history branch's `Sensor history data lengths: ... 0, 0, 0, 0` line is not diagnostic (GH#4009 — fixed in PR #5646, merged 2026-10-24, with the coupled `docs/faq.md`/inverter-setup edit riding along; keep the mechanism for logs on v9.4.3-):** the lengths line in `find_charge_curve()`'s missing-history `Warn: ... Cannot find battery ... curve (missing history) ...` branch (inverter.py) reads the **parsed** lists (`soc_kwh`/`charge_rate`/`battery_power`/`predbat_status`), which are initialised empty and only filled in the all-history-present branch — so the line prints all zeros **whatever entity is actually missing**, and usually only one does; don't hand "all four lack history" to a reporter off it. Related, and a version tell: the different wording `Warn: Cannot find battery ... curve (no full rate ... found in history)` with **no inverter id** is the pre-v8.40.0 shape; on current main that same case (history present, no full-rate cycle found) is the `Info: ... this is normal in cost-optimised operation` line (inverter.py), downgraded by `6784a3bb`. Post-fix, the warning names the setting and the entity that has no history and the companion line reads `Sensor history record counts: ...` with real per-entity counts — and a rewording needs the coupled FAQ edit (docs/faq.md's "Info: Cannot find battery charge curve" section builds its GivEnergy-REST guidance around the quoted message — the #5646 PR shipped it together with the code change); the parsed-list locals (`soc_kwh = []` etc.) become dead initialisers once the lengths line goes, cleaned up in the same PR. | `find_charge_curve`, `battery_curve_keys` |
| Inverter time string (`inverter.py`) | `Warn: Inverter N unable to read inverter time string X using formats [...]` is a format-coverage gap in `Inverter.__init__`'s parse chain, not a broken sensor. **Fixed in PR #5154 (merged 2026-09-19)** — keep the mechanism for older logs: the chain used to be exactly three formats (`TIME_FORMAT` T-with-tz, `TIME_FORMAT_OCTOPUS` space-with-tz, plus the per-type `clock_time_format`), so the very common space-no-tz shape (`2025-06-10 15:44:42`) failed all three on `SA` (whose own format is T-no-tz) and on the `%H:%M:%S`-only types (GE/GEC/GEE/GWMQTT). #5154 turned the nested try/except into one loop over `[TIME_FORMAT, TIME_FORMAT_OCTOPUS, TIME_FORMAT_SOLIS]` with the type's own format appended as a fallback, and localizes naive parses to the configured timezone. Consequences of a failed parse: `inverter_time` stays None so clock-skew detection never runs for that inverter, the warn repeats every 5-minute cycle, and `auto_restart("Unable to read inverter time")` fires each cycle — only logging when `auto_restart` is unconfigured but restarting inverter control when it is. Not every instance is a format gap: GH#3599 logs the same line with the string literally `unknown` — on current main the unavailability guard (`""`/`unavailable`/`unknown`/`none` → no reading, skew skipped without restart) handles that, so read the actual string in the warn before assuming which failure it is. Test notes: `test_inverter_time_handling` covered unavailable and T-with-tz only — space-no-tz was the uncovered variant; and a *stale-dated* inverter time string parses fine and then trips `check_clock_skew` → `auto_restart`, which **raises** in the harness (the fixture's `auto_restart` stub raises rather than setting a flag), so probe with a near-`now_utc` string in the format under test. **A false skew warning on a cloud/REST integration can measure data age, not clock skew (GH#5562, triaged 2026-10-16):** Predbat reads the *published* inverter-time sensor, which on those paths advances only when the upstream poll does (`gecloud.py` publishes the API `time` field, the gateway its own) — so `check_clock_skew()`'s difference is data age, and a slow or dropped poll reads as a drifting inverter clock (the reporter's cadence/drop statistics in #5562 are production measurements, attributed not reproduced). Two implementation points for any freshness logic: HA's `last_changed` is the value-change stamp with an in-repo precedent (`control_ledger.py`'s `generation_from_state()`), reachable only via `get_state_wrapper(entity, raw=True)` — `get_state(..., attribute="all")` falls through to the default because `get_state()` looks the attribute up inside the attributes dict (`ha.py`); and in `db_primary` mode `set_state` → `db_manager` stamps every publish with write time, so there Predbat-published entities' `last_changed` is poll age, never data age. | `inverter` |
| LuxPower (no code — `templates/luxpower.yaml` only) | Predbat has **no LuxPower code at all** — no `INVERTER_DEF` entry and no component: grepping `luxpower`/`LuxPower` across `apps/predbat` matches docs, tests and the repo-root template only — support is 100% template/config, the user maps Predbat arg names onto whichever HA integration's entities they run. The stock template targets LuxPython (`lux_*` entities) while `lxp_*` entities come from the wills-b luxpower **cloud** integration (same device registers behind the web portal) — start any LuxPower report on "what did the user map, and which integration publishes it" (GH#5661, verified on main 2026-10-20). **Every target-SoC write is floored at the reserve:** `adjust_battery_target()` does `soc = int(max(soc, self.reserve_percent))` (inverter.py, "SoC has no decimal places and clamp in min"), and several `execute.py` reset paths deliberately write target 0% (e.g. the export-window reset), so the zero arrives at the reserve floor — `set_reserve_min` (default 4) for the stock `has_reserve_soc: False` shape (`refresh_config()` sets `reserve_percent = reserve_min`; `battery_min_soc` ≥ it raises one-way). LuxPower hardware floors differ **per register** (the reporter's portal screenshot showed the LuxPowerTek web app itself rejecting "Stop AC Charge SOC(%)" = 4 with "Operation failed, error code: 3"), so a perfectly-configured setup can still fail every SOC-limit write; the fix is honouring the control entity's own min/max — open umbrella GH#4831, whose table already lists `charge_limit` ← `adjust_battery_target()` — and the workaround is `set_reserve_min` at or above the device's smallest register minimum. Readback note: `got 100.0` in the warning is the number entity holding its previous value, not the plan's charge control failing — plan charge control goes through `charge_stop_service`/the AC-charge enable switch, so don't overstate harm beyond the recurring `Completed run status ... with Errors reported` (`had_errors` from `write_and_poll_value()`). Symptom pointer: a `Trying to write N to X didn't complete got M` on a `number.*`/percent arg maps to `write_and_poll_value()` (`inverter.py`) with the value's origin in the `adjust_*` caller (`execute.py`/`inverter.py`) — grepping "write to charge_limit failed" finds nothing, the strings are generic. | `inverter` |
| Anker SOLIX X1 (PR #5677 — open at writing, 2026-10-24; `templates/anker_x1.yaml` template family, no code) | Custom inverter type `AX1` over GE defaults, whose `inverter:` block sets `support_charge_freeze`/`support_discharge_freeze` false and drives everything through one HA script (`script.predbat_anker_x1_control`) writing a signed battery setpoint in VPP work mode — so freeze/hold reports on an X1 should look at the script's hold→auto (Self-consumption) conversion, not the planner (execute.py's capability gates apply as recorded above). Integration facts verified against afewyards/anker-x1-ha (2026-10-21): 0.7.0's RestoreEntity daily sensors (`grid_bought_energy`, `grid_fed_in_energy`, ...) reset at midnight and survive an HA restart, while the register-backed `grid_bought`/`grid_fed_in` counters are **lifetime totals on firmware 1.0.19.16 but midnight-resetting on 1.0.16.1** — and Predbat power control needs ≥0.7.0 (setpoint bounds moved from live limit registers, which collapse in VPP mode, to nameplate registers 10124/10126), so do not take an older-version report at face value. The X1 reports **no house-load energy**: `load_today` comes from package template sensors balancing PV+battery+meter, so "load history wrong today" should first ask whether Home Assistant was restarted mid-day. |
| Standalone / Docker (non-HA) | GH#4601: a callback returning `None` instead of `True` broke the Octopus saving-session fallback in standalone mode. Anything that works under HA but not standalone is worth checking along the `ha.py` websocket and `userinterface.py` callback paths. | `trigger_callback_success_signal` |
| Holiday mode (`fetch.py`) | GH#4732: under `days_previous_auto` (the default) holiday mode is handled entirely inside `compute_load_forecast_history()`, not by the `days_previous = [1]` branch, which is only reachable with `days_previous_auto: False`. Days whose holiday state does not match the *forecast day's* are now excluded outright rather than halved, and `get_holiday_minutes()` must span `num_days + 1` to cover the `minutes_now` overhang. A slot with no matching history falls back to `holiday_load_scaling` (default 0.7), which is what makes holiday mode act on day one - a normalised weighted mean cannot otherwise express "all of my data is wrong". **All of that is bypassed while Load ML owns the load forecast (GH#5523, code-read on main 40b9ce28, 2026-10-12):** with `load_ml_enable` + `load_ml_source` both set and a non-empty ML forecast, `load_forecast_only` is set (`fetch.py`) and the entire holiday-aware weighted-bucket forecast is skipped (`not self.load_forecast_only` in `fetch_sensor_data()`); the ML forecast is ingested at face value (`scale=1.0`) and nothing in `load_ml_component.py` mentions holiday at all — training pulls raw `load_today` history unscaled, so holiday days enter the rolling training window with no exclusion.
