| Predbat charged the car at peak rate, or planned a small house-battery top-up inside a peak window, with car charging enabled | Non-smart car planning is price-blind by design: **`car_charging_plan_smart` defaulted false until PR #5251 (GH#5237, merged 2026-09-26, unreleased at the time of writing) flipped the default to true** — `car_charging_plan_max_price` defaults 0 (never filters) either way, so pre-#5251 the car filled from the earliest low-rate-scan window onward regardless of price, and a plug-in mid-peak charged at peak (GH#2716, log-confirmed; smart mode is what re-sorts by price and honours the max-price skip, `plan.py`). On a #5251+ build a peak-rate car charge is no longer the default path — check the version before citing this explanation. Two more non-bug shapes from the same report: a saving-session reward is stamped onto the **import** rate too, which can push that hour above the low-rate scan threshold (`Rate thresholds ... import = rate_max - 0.5`), so the hour vanishes from `low_rates` and the car slot list shows a gap there — do not read that gap as a planning bug; and the in-progress peak-window house-battery top-up is the optimiser's keep response (`car_charging_from_battery: true` + `best_soc_keep` > 0 + the end-of-record SoC credit at replacement cost), same family as the "Hold charging target" row above — the high-price gate only skips windows whose limit is still 0, so a deliberately-selected window is exempt by design. Traps: `best_soc_keep` can be time-varying (automation or user), so the incident log and a later debug dump can disagree (4.0 vs 5.0 observed); and the `Calculate Best options` log line carries the plan-relevant config (`best_soc_keep`, `metric_battery_value_scaling`, ...) so you often don't need the yaml. | `car_charging` |
| Battery sits idle ("Hold for car") while the car charges from the grid on PV-then-grid — "why isn't the battery covering the house during the car's slot?" | Expected, not a bug: the hold (`execute.py`'s carHolding block) fires on `set_charge_window` with `car_charging_from_battery` off whenever a car is charging now or inside a planned slot, and it only stops the *battery feeding the car* — the car's grid charging and the charger's start/stop are outside its scope (GH#4572). Since PR #5470 (post-v9.3.8) the hold defers its writes on an inverter that is itself `Charging` (non-timed-pause path) - log line `Inverter N is charging, so the hold for car N leaves the discharge rate and reserve alone until charging stops` - because a charging battery cannot feed the car and some GivEnergy hybrids refuse to grid-charge at discharge rate 0 with the SoC on the reserve; the pre-#5470 signature is a battery sitting at reserve for the whole cheap window while the status read `Charging, Hold for car` (GH#5419/#5467). "Charge the EV only when X" has no native knob at all: no EV-SoC gate, and the price knobs shape which slots are planned, not the charger — see the car-charging row above and open PR #3791 for the tooling direction. |
| "Why does Predbat discharge to the house during cheap hours?" / low-rate hold requests | A structural model gap, not an optimiser mistake (GH#5122): any minute outside a selected charge AND export window falls into the `ECO Mode` branch of `predict()` (`prediction.py`), where the battery is modelled supplying whatever house load PV cannot cover — there is **no modelled action meaning "import from grid instead of discharging the battery"** outside a selected window. The only zero-discharge-outside-window modelled states are EV slots (`car_charging_from_battery: false`) and iBoost (`iboost_prevent_discharge`); all freeze/hold config is scoped to selected windows or manual periods. Also: a charge window covering a cheap gap saves only the round-trip loss on the cycled kWh — often ~2p, right at the default `metric_min_improvement_plan` gate of 2.0p — so a marginal window can legitimately fail to clear it (analysis, not verified against a real plan). | none |
| "Predbat reports a GivEnergy error but I use Solis / Fox / GE-Cloud" | `inverter_type` was never set. It defaults to `GE` (`inverter.py`'s `get_arg("inverter_type", "GE")`, warning to the log only) and component auto-config is what actually writes it — roughly a dozen components (`solis.py`, `gecloud.py`, `fox.py`, `givtcp.py`, `sigenergy.py`, `gateway.py`, `teslemetry.py`, `deye.py`, `sunsynk.py`, `alphaess.py`, `solax.py`, `enphase.py`) — but auto-config only runs after discovery succeeds, while the component is registered as active the moment it is instantiated. So an outage or credential failure at startup leaves a non-GivEnergy inverter driven with GivEnergy capability flags, and every source read failure surfaces as `unable to read ... window - GivEnergy returned no data, check the GivEnergy credentials`, because `inverter_source_name()` prefers `INVERTER_DEF[inverter_type]["name"]` and `GE` is named "GivEnergy". Confirm from the debug yaml (no `inverter_type` key) plus the log line `Warn: ... inverter_type is not set in apps.yaml`, and treat any GE-specific behaviour in that log as assumed rather than configured. Self-heals on comms recovery; a manual `inverter_type` avoids it. The warning now names the assumed type and the component that should have set it — PR #4992 merged (`bdeb4e05`) after GH#4990, so on a current version the message reads `assuming <name> (<type>)` rather than a bare "assuming GE" (GH#4990). |
| "Charging and exporting in the same slot" in the plan html | Two stacked artefacts, sequential in time - not simultaneous charge and export (GH#4987, replayed from the reporter's debug yaml). A **hold-charge** window whose limit equals current SoC (`HoldChrg→`, a no-op that only blocks discharge) is clipped to the minutes before an export window opens, and the 30-minute html row aggregates the two - `output.py` deliberately splits the State cell in two for exactly this case ("charging and exporting in the same slot, split the state into two"). The hold window itself is cycle-dependent: the same dump replayed later produced identical export windows and no charge window at all, so suppressing a charge window whose limit equals current SoC would be cosmetic, not a fix. When the slots back onto an Octopus saving session, note the session bonus is stamped onto the **import** rate as well as export (`load_saving_slot()` runs on both rate tables), which inflates the modelled cost of serving house load during the session - a baseline-free approximation, same class as GH#4893/#2090 (the metric impact is suspected, not quantified). |
| `Best export window @ 0p` on a non-zero export tariff | Stale inverter-programmed discharge window read back from the inverter, not a rate: `update_status()` pre-fills `export_window` from the inverter's programmed discharge start/end times with `window["average"] = 0` (`Rates are not known yet`, `inverter.py`) and repeats it +24h for every forecast day; with `calculate_best_export: false` the planner uses those read-back windows verbatim as `export_window_best` (GH#5036, replay-verified). Grep the replay's config tail for `calculate_best_export`/`set_export_window` first. Related disambiguation: `Wrote N to discharge_rate` plus a reserve write right after a charge window closes is the routine restore/reset path (`execute.py`), not an export command. |
| Inverter setting not applied | `execute.py` into the component's reconcile loop; check whether `read_only` is set. |
| `Note: Inverter does not support charge/discharge freeze - disabled` although the user set that flag — or "the `has_service_api`/`has_rest_api` flag is on/off and Predbat behaves the same" | Two INVERTER_DEF flag families that do not do what their names suggest. The capability flags are per-inverter-type table values, not top-level config: `INVERTER_DEF[type]["support_charge_freeze"]` is copied to `inv_support_charge_freeze` in `Inverter.__init__` and `execute.py` force-disables the feature from those, so a top-level `support_charge_freeze:` in apps.yaml is read by nothing — it must go inside the `inverter:` block, which is merged into `INVERTER_DEF[type]` before the flags are read (the pattern `templates/luxpower.yaml`/`fronius.yaml`/`huawei.yaml` already use; docs/inverter-setup.md documents it — GH#1406, Sofar `SF`/`SFMB` ship both flags False; a custom `inverter_type` is seeded from a copy of the `GE` definition before the user's `inverter:` block overrides it key-by-key, so anything the block leaves out inherits GivEnergy's flags — GH#5066). Residual on #1406: a `templates/sofar.yaml` shipping the `inverter:` override itself (plus commented `charge_freeze_service`/`discharge_freeze_service` examples) is the open fix idea; whether Sofar's `battery_save` mode meets Predbat's charge-freeze contract is an unverified hardware question. Opposite direction: `has_service_api` is not an `INVERTER_DEF` key at all and `has_rest_api` is set on every definition but never read anywhere — REST is enabled by the `givtcp_rest` args and the service path by the presence of the `*_service` keys in apps.yaml (`call_service_template()` returns False when the key is absent); only `has_mqtt_api` is actually consumed (GH#5066). Two service-path facts a "can Predbat call integration X's service?" question needs (GH#5203, where the bot first wrongly answered "no mechanism" and had to post a correction): the six `*_service` config items (`charge_start_service` … `discharge_freeze_service`, `config.py`) are free-form service routes on the immediate-control path (`adjust_charge_immediate`/`adjust_export_immediate` through `call_service_template()`), where a dict-form entry names any HA service plus arbitrary data keys and `{placeholder}` values are `.format()`-resolved from the call's data (`device_id`, `target_soc`, `power`, the start/end times); identical calls are deduplicated by hash unless `repeat: True`/`always: True` (the GH#4876 interplay), and shipped templates now carry these keys across two dozen inverter templates. Window programming stays entity-based — no service hook — and there is no `set_charge_window`/`set_discharge_window` config item to stop window programming entirely. Upstream note from the same issue: Predbat's supported Solis Modbus path (wills106 solax-modbus + `templates/ginlong_solis*.yaml`) exposes TOU slots + commit button only, hultenvp/solis-sensor exposes no dispatch either, while the dedicated HACS integration **Pho3niX90/solis_modbus** does expose remote dispatch (`solis_modbus.solis_dispatch`/`_stop`/`_schedule`, capability-gated on register 34502 = 0xAA55) — reachable from Predbat today through the `*_service` keys, so Solis "remote dispatch" is a template/config question, not a Predbat code gap. |
| `Warn: Inverter N write_and_poll_switch: No entity_id for scheduled_charge_enable/scheduled_discharge_enable to write`, or `write_and_poll_option: No entity_id for inverter_mode to write`, while the plan works fine and the only casualty is `Completed run status <mode> with Errors reported` red | Presence flags, not a write failure. `check_write_entity()` (`inverter.py`) logs the warning and records `had_errors` - that red banner is its only consequence - for any control whose bound arg is not an entity id, while plan/execute continue unaffected, which is why reporters describe working control under a red banner (GH#5539). A custom inverter type is seeded from a copy of `GE` in `refresh_config()` before the `inverter:` block overrides key-by-key, so its *presence* flags are GE's unless overridden: `has_charge_enable_time: True` (GE's own `has_discharge_enable_time` is False) and `has_ge_inverter_mode: true`. Flag on with no bound entity means every pass warns - enable writes from `disable_charge_window()` and `adjust_force_export()` (and, since PR #5543, the between-windows enable-off write from execute.py); `inverter_mode` under a GE-mode type warns via `write_and_poll_option()`. Flag off instead creates Predbat-owned dummy entities in `refresh_config()` for the unbound controls - silent no-op writes, and enable state is simulated from window start/end equality; the window-only semantics are tested by `test_disable_charge_window` (window reset to midnight via `adjust_charge_window()`). First check in any future one-brand report of this warning: the reporter's `inverter:` block flags, not a code change. Distinct from the #1406 entry above (capability flags, `support_charge_freeze` family). Unverified, kept as suspicion: that the flags-false route fully preserves a known-working window-only setup - the disable paths change what gets written (window zeroing, window-derived enable state). |
| Reserve keeps ratcheting up to a high value and never comes back | `battery_min_soc` floors `set_reserve_min` **one-way**: `Inverter.__init__` bumps the reserve min up to it and nothing ever lowers it back — the log line is `Increasing set_reserve_min from X% to battery_min_soc of Y%` (`inverter.py`). If Y tracks the battery's live SoC or flaps within minutes, the user's `battery_min_soc` is bound to a dynamic entity (the stock Solar Assistant Growatt SPH template ships one) and the ratchet pins the reserve at the highest value ever read — 97% observed, leaving no export headroom on an 11.5 kWh battery (GH#4013). If `battery_min_soc` is unset it defaults to `set_reserve_min` and nothing ratchets, which is why "removed it and it works" reports check out. `battery_scaling_auto` only rescales `soc_max`, never reserve — a red herring in the same log. Broader reading for "reserve stuck at N%": **the first knob to check is `set_reserve_min` (default 4), because `battery_min_soc` is a raise-only floor *on* it, not an independent floor** — with `battery_min_soc` scalar `0` set and `set_reserve_enable` on (`has_reserve_soc` types), `reserve_percent` is the `set_reserve_min` default of 4 while `reserve_percent_current` correctly reads 0, so the observed "ignored `battery_min_soc`, stuck at 4%" is the other knob, not the scalar being dropped (GH#5232, probe-verified: `get_arg(..., index=0)` returns scalars fine — `resolve_arg()` extracts-by-index only for *lists*, so scalars are silently accepted). The resolution for "I want a 0% floor" is `set_reserve_min: 0`. **A display-only sibling (GH#5400, code-verified 2026-10-05):** the device's own register floor (`reserve_device_bounds()`, read from the reserve entity's min/max attributes - PR #4956's modelling fix reaches only `reserve_percent`) never raises the *shown* `set_reserve_min`, which is raised one-way from `battery_min_soc` alone in `Inverter.__init__` - so a component publishing an entity `min` without binding `battery_min_soc` (GE Cloud publishes entity min/max from the register's static validation rule and binds no arg; by contrast GivTCP publishes GE's assumed 4% floor lowered-only by the user's `battery_min_soc`) leaves the log's `Reserve min: 4%` line below the floor actually in force. Cosmetic: the clamp corrects `reserve_percent` itself, `self.reserve_min` feeds just the log - and feeding `set_reserve_min` from this static register metadata is the lower-risk direction for that failure than the dynamic-entity ratchet above, so it is a maintainer call, not a defect report answer. **A third shape answers "Predbat always sets the reserve to X%, no matter what `set_reserve_min` says, but the plan shows my value" (GH#5660, log+yaml verified on main 2026-10-20):** where X = (plan charge-window target + 1) that is the **hold-on-reserve** path — in the log every such `Current Reserve is ... and new target is X%` write is immediately preceded by `Hold charging as SoC ... is above target SoC`, and `execute.py`'s hold branch re-asserts `adjust_reserve(min(target + 1, 100, reserve_max))` every 5-minute cycle while a charge window is active (deliberate since #4164; `set_reserve_hold` defaults true). `set_reserve_min` is an input to the plan's floor only, never to the hold target — check for `Current Reserve is 4% ...` writes outside holds before concluding the setting is ignored. Two compounding effects verified in the same log: both `set_reserve_min` and `set_reserve_enable` carry `reset_inverter: true`, so every change drops the reserve to the device floor (4% on GE) before the next cycle re-asserts the hold ("I set X, it snapped back"); and the register occasionally reads the floor value with no Predbat write (ledger `we_set/now_reads` divergence events) — something outside Predbat touches it a few times a day, so do not attribute external reserve drift to Predbat without ledger evidence. The plan panel shows the plan's floor; the hold reserve is execute-time and not displayed — the display gap is the mirror of GH#5400 above. |
| Raised the reserve to keep the battery full (e.g. `set_reserve_min` to 100%) and the battery then never charges - `Filtered charge windows []` in the plan log, battery drains onto grid | The charge-candidate scan needs SoC levels strictly *above* the reserve to enumerate: `while loop_soc > self.reserve` (`plan.py:2696`) starts from `soc_max` (`plan.py:2513`, capped by `best_soc_max` at `plan.py:2556`), so a reserve at or above soc max means the scan never runs, and with freeze off the reserve-equal candidate is removed as well (`plan.py:2725`), leaving off-only. Downstream then makes it look worse than it is: the sim floors SoC at the reserve whenever actual SoC is below it (`prediction.py:1323`, kernel `prediction_kernel.cpp:1296`), so freeze-at-reserve candidates score model-dead and `prune_dead_plan_slots()` (call site `plan.py:2183`) drops them; the plan log line is `plan.py:2263`. Two traps: any charge limit **at** the reserve percent is treated as a freeze by the kernel (`prediction_kernel.cpp:823`, `charge_limit_n = max(soc, reserve)`), so a fix that caps only `self.reserve` does not unfreeze a 100% limit - `reserve_percent` has to be capped with it; and `discard_unused_charge_slots()` keeps any `limit > 0` (`plan.py:3467`) without comparing it to the reserve, so the emptying happens upstream - don't chase that filter. Reachability: the UI caps `set_reserve_min` at 100 (`config.py`) and Fox has no `reserve_device_bounds()` override, so nothing clamps the reserve below soc max; the execute-side tell is `Current reserve is X%, already at target` (`inverter.py:2121`). Same collapse from the other bound: `best_soc_max` below the reserve (GH#4766); a high reserve on the export side is just no export headroom (GH#4013, row above). `best_soc_min` is the one floor that does raise a charge target above the current SoC (`plan.py:2693` raises it above the reserve, appended as a candidate at `plan.py:2721`) - both the user workaround and the in-tree precedent for planning toward a floor. |
| Both `Validation ... is not a list, but requires N entries based on num_inverters` **and** `Return bad int value [<whole list>]` repeat each cycle, "Read-Only with Errors" | A per-inverter arg whose reader fetches without `index=`: the schema's `entries: num_inverters` requirement and the reader's list-less read disagree, so *only the scalar form works* while the validator demands the list form. The list form fails `get_arg()`'s int/float coercion and silently falls back to the default **and** sets `record_status(had_errors=True)` every cycle. Instance: `inverter_reserve_max` — schema (`config.py`) demands `entries`, reader (`inverter.py:504`) reads `get_arg("inverter_reserve_max", 100)` with no index (unchanged since `d43f16a9`, Jun 2024, predating the schema), so the list form silently leaves the written-reserve cap (`inverter.py`, only use) at 100; the documented scalar form (docs/apps-yaml.md, faq) reads fine but the validator flags it, because the `entries` check runs before the integer type-check normalises a bare int to a one-element list and `continue`s past it — a fix has to decide together whether a scalar means "one value for all inverters" or the docs/validator change (GH#5369, probe-verified via `tools/triage_test.sh`). Fix shape: `index=self.id`, like the sibling `inverter_battery_rate_min` (`inverter.py`, the `battery_rate_min` read). **The two contracts, and the fix in flight (PR #5651, open at writing, reviewed 2026-10-19 — sibling PR #5644 was closed as its duplicate on 2026-10-20):** every `entries: num_inverters` arg is read through two independent contracts — `validate_config()`'s `entries` list check and the `get_arg(..., index=...)` reader — so a red banner and a misread value travel separately in one report. Reader traps: a **list** value read with `index=None` hits the int/float coercion tail and falls back to the default with `had_errors` (the "Return bad int value [98, 98]" line — the half the validation banner hides); a **scalar** read with a truthy index warns `item X is incorrectly setup` for inverters 1+ on every cycle (index 0 is exempt — the guard is `if index`); manual-API overrides store index 0 when written without one, so a no-index override matches only inverter 0; an out-of-range index on a list returns `None` and the default applies *quietly* (no `record_status`), so the status sensor alone does not reveal it. #5651's shape: a `single_value_ok` spec flag on the `APPS_SCHEMA` item plus the reader deciding `index=self.id if isinstance(self.base.args.get(name), list) else None` — complete for `inverter_reserve_max` only because it has exactly one consumer. Same ticket one arg over, still open: `inverter_battery_rate_min` — the docs section (docs/apps-yaml.md) shows the scalar form while the validator rejects it for 2+ inverters. |
| Plan shows a flat hold at the reserve but the battery drains well past it — on Sigenergy down to the inverter's own 0% cut-off | For `has_reserve_soc: False` types (stock `SIG`; the GE Cloud flavour, #4832, is closed - the reserve-register modelling landed as PR #4956, merged 2026-09-06, fixing #4953) the planned floor is **model-only**: `reserve_percent = reserve_min` (`inverter.py`, deliberate since commit `5caa9363` — use reserve_min as the planning floor for non-managed inverters) feeds only the plan's prediction, `execute.py` logs `Note: Inverter does not support reserve - disabling reserve functions` every cycle and never writes one, and the template's `reserve:` mapping is inert because for `has_reserve_soc: False` types the `Inverter.__init__` dummy block swaps `args["reserve"]` for a predbat dummy entity. The only device-visible stops are (1) the execute export-hold poll (`Export Hold (Demand mode) as export is now at/below target...`) when SoC reads at/below the target — a 5-minute poll that can land a hair **past** the floor — and (2) once the mode returns to eco/self-consumption, house load drains the battery to the inverter's own cut-off (SIG default 0%), which is the bulk of the drift (GH#5358, plan/log-verified 2026-10-02). Log tells: the "does not support reserve" disable line every cycle = `has_reserve_soc` False in effect (which also rules out the GH#4728 override shape that makes reserve writes destructive); `Export Hold (Demand mode) ... current SoC XkWh and target YkWh` = the poll stop at the model floor. Workaround: raise the device's own cut-off (`number.sigen_plant_ess_discharge_cut_off_state_of_charge`) so the inverter holds the floor itself — caveat: the canned docs-template automations re-pin the entity (edit the Freeze-Charging-excepted branch), and SIG's confirmed firmware bug (import-to-charge when SoC reads below the cut-off, docs/inverter-setup.md) only bites at the boundary. See also the reserve-ratchet row above for the `set_reserve_min`/`battery_min_soc` knob family. |
| First cheap window charges to 0% / the charge target lands later than the cheap band | A metric tie in the charge-window SoC scan: the running-best adoption is non-strict `<=` (`plan.py`, `(metric + min_improvement_scaled) <= best_metric_first and metric <= best_metric`) and `min_improvement_scaled` gates only against the first (full-charge) candidate, so an exact tie lets the later, lower-SoC candidate replace the fuller one. Ties are realistic because `metric_battery_cycle` defaults 0, making charging 1 kWh cost ≈ its stored-energy credit (GH#5069). Neither knob fixes it: `metric_min_improvement` doesn't cover the running best, and raising `metric_battery_cycle` raises the stored-energy credit too. |
| Overnight charge stops at "just enough": the battery is planned to hit the reserve exactly as the next cheap window opens, with no clipping risk that would justify charging less | `prune_dead_plan_slots()` (plan.py, PR #4491) trialled each slot on the **nominal-only** metric and kept the removal whenever nominal did not get worse. That stripped any charge slot that hedges the pv10/load10 scenario, even though it charges real energy. Replay-verified on a 2026-10-24 dump at ae4e9c43 (flat 7.6p 23:30–06:30, 36.94p otherwise, 0p export, 11.52 kWh battery). The optimiser chose 05:30–06:30 at 50%, blended metric 436.15p. The prune dropped the 05:30 slot (nominal 439.74→437.36) and the blended plan fell to 442.51p, so the live plan charged 30 min to 34% and ran down to the 10% reserve at Mon 00:00. #4491 assumed a replan re-creates the slot if the pessimistic case happens, but a slot whose window closes before the risk arrives cannot be re-created. The same holds for an export that makes room for pv90 solar. **Fixed in PR #5721 (merged 2026-10-24):** a removal that leaves nominal unchanged is still pruned on nominal alone, which keeps the #4453 phantoms out. A removal that improves nominal must also not worsen the blended metric; the debug log shows `Prune kept hedge ... slot` when it doesn't. Separately, "charge to 100%" is not the metric optimum even without the prune: with export at 0p, `battery_value_rate()` scales the stored-kWh credit by `metric_battery_value_export_scaling` (0.8), giving 6.53p against an 8.16p purchase cost, so each kWh left at end_record costs ~1.6p. The blended optimum on this dump is ~49%, flat to ~75% at scaling 1.0. Replay needed `--redo` (`KeyError: 0` otherwise). |
| Overnight charge starts later than the cheap window and stops short of full, though the window is long enough to charge more | Still `prune_dead_plan_slots()`, through the branch #5721 kept: a removal that left the nominal metric unchanged was pruned as dead "whatever the pv10/pv90 branches say". A charge slot can be nominal-neutral and still real. It charges in its own window, but the optimiser's tie-valued limit on the last slots (stored energy is credited at the price paid plus losses) cuts the same energy off the end of the night. In pv10, `charge_scaling10` (0.8) slows charging so the battery never reaches that limit, and the slot is the hedge. Replay-verified on the maintainer's 2026-10-24 20:00 dump (v9.5.0, GivEnergy 19.05 kWh at 2.6 kW, IOG 23:30–05:30): the prune log said `Prune dead charge slot 0 ... 23:30` with nominal 329.84→329.84, and the blended metric went from 346.22 to 348.56p. Nominal import, export and final SoC were identical with and without the slot, so an energy check cannot catch it; the nominal SoC path can. **Fixed in PR #5735 (merged 2026-10-25):** a nominal-neutral slot is dead only if it does not do its own job in its own window in nominal. For a charge, that job is raising SoC across the window by more than 0.1 kWh and ending above the no-slot path; an export is the mirror image. Holds that only limit discharge, and a charge to 50% while nominal sits at 55%, stay dead: a replan when the slot comes round puts them back if needed. To trace one dump's prune decisions, wrap `Plan.prune_dead_plan_slots` and turn `debug_enable` on: the `Prune dead` / `Prune kept hedge` / `Prune trade-off` lines name each slot. |
| An evening export drains the battery to the same level whatever the load risk, and a plan that keeps some back scores better when tried by hand | `optimise_export()` only ever trialled a target export at `best_soc_min` (0%), plus idle, freeze and 5-minute duration trims, and scored pv10 with that 0% target, so the 10% case drained as far as power allowed. In reality clipping sends the inverter the SoC nominal reaches, and a replan shortens the export if the battery is lower. Duration cannot express this, because a shorter window is the same in every scenario; only a SoC floor adapts. Replay-verified on the maintainer's 2026-10-24 dump: the chosen 17:00–20:30 export was already the best duration-only option (341.86p), while a 30% floor scored 339.80p. **Fixed in PR #5736:** every pv10 simulation floors each target-mode export at the whole percent the same plan's nominal run reaches at that window's end (raise-only; pv90 takes no floor, as a sunnier replan exports more, not less). The kernel does it in one batch call (ABI 10): `pk_run_batch` runs nominal twin jobs as phase 0 and feeds their `export_end_soc_out` to the floored pv10 jobs (`export_floor_soc`) in phase 1. `flush_batch` adds the twins, or reuses `prediction_cache_export_end`; `run_prediction_metric` runs nominal first and seeds that memo. `merge_export_staircase()` merges per-slot falling targets back into one export when the nominal metric does not move. Dead ends, in order: (1) floors only inside `run_prediction_metric`, while the charge and levels passes still batched pv10 at 0%, gave the search two objectives (random scenario 19 +11.6p). (2) Pinning the winning floor into the plan made every pass agree but was wrong: a pinned target binds, so an export added earlier cannot raise the total exported. (3) Running the levels pass with pv10 at full charge rate, because `charge_scaling10` (0.8) plus the floor strands pv10 energy in charge-to-export arbitrage: within noise on 100 fresh random scenarios (nominal -0.18p ±0.14), so dropped. The stranding is real and is the right answer: `optimise_levels` "discharge" now charges only the 1kWh its one-hour export can move. On cost the change is neutral (100 fresh scenarios, seeds 1000–1099: nominal -0.04p ±0.27, blended +0.03p ±0.25, runtime +6%); its value is that pv10 scores the export the inverter is actually sent. A single-scenario swing such as random scenario 19 (+14p, the levels region hill-climb settling in a different basin at width 120) is search path, not the floor: cross-score both plans under one scorer before blaming the rule. |
| Overnight cheap charge stops far short of full when a free electricity event sits a few hours later in the horizon | Configuration/by-design bridging (GH#5719, triaged 2026-10-24 on main; Octopus Go 9.4p + Outgoing 12p, cycle cost 2p): a 0p free slot hours later makes the planner size the cheap window's SoC target to bridge down to the free session (dump: cheap-window target 28% and one export slot ending at the free session; the live log's own notification "You will reach a minimum of 24% battery in 32 hours" matches the plan), while a same-tariff cheap window with no free session ahead charges to 100% — one replay proves both halves. The reporter's own cycle-cost sweep bracketed the flip at `metric_battery_cycle` ≈ 0.5p (2.0/1.0/0.5 capped; 0.0 → 100% plus export-to-20/42% before the next cheap window) — user-supplied per-setting trials are free corroboration whenever a report includes them. Two A/B replay traps from the same run: deleting the `octopus_free_slots` list entry is *ineffective* — the 0p is already baked into the dump's `rate_import`, so a true variant must patch the rates (the ineffective edit scored byte-identical); and the dump's config-diff table prints the CONFIG_ITEMS/apps.yaml-level value while `calculate_plan()` consumes the dump's top-level scalar — patching the top-level `metric_battery_cycle` changed the plan while the table kept printing the old value, so judge a variant by its plan, not the table. | |
| I set a config item above its maximum and it stays capped — no YAML workaround | An `APPS_SCHEMA` item's `max` is enforced as a hard clamp on **every** value source, not just the HA UI: the input_number branch of `userinterface.py` re-clamps after `float()` (deliberate — an apps.yaml override bypasses the HA entity's own min/max; `Warn: Config item ... clamping to ...` is the diagnostic). GH#5070 instance: `debug_history_count` max 50 while the capture path itself is uncapped (retention is `interval × count` in `_capture_debug_history()`), so widening the window was a schema change — PR #5071 (merged 2026-09-19) raised the max from 50 to 500, with the storage warning in docs/customisation.md (the top of the range is ~2.5 GB on disk at 2-5 MB per snapshot). |
| Hundreds of identical `Trying to write N to X didn't complete got M` warnings overnight | Before concluding control is broken, check whether the planner even had a window in that period: the writes may be the routine idle reset — `execute.py` re-writes charge/discharge rate to max every cycle while not charging so PV can still charge (GH#5073, where the planner's own overnight windows were empty and no charging was lost — pure log noise). `write_and_poll_value()` has no failure dedup: after `INVERTER_MAX_RETRY` attempts it warns once, flags `had_errors` and clears the ledger entry, so the next cycle re-attempts from scratch — a firmware state that permanently refuses a register (SolarEdge preserve-charge holding its charge-limit register at 0 is the first confirmed instance) produces ~10 refused writes + a warning per cycle, indefinitely; any brand with a refuse-silently register maps here. Exception since PR #5297: on the `GWMQTT` (Predbat hub) type the retry policy is 3 attempts, and a same-target control failing twice degrades to one write per 5 minutes until it verifies - the "indefinitely" reading is a non-gateway-type reading only. **A stable quantised read-back below the verify tolerance never converges (GH#5324, verified on main):** the entity-write tolerance is `battery_rate_max_charge * MINUTE_WATT / 20` — 5% of the **allocated** rate, and `battery_rate_max_charge = min(inverter_limit_charge, battery_rate_max_raw)/MINUTE_WATT` (`inverter.py`) — so an `inverter_limit_charge` cap (including the API override, which is in `CONFIG_API_OVERRIDE`) below ~5 register steps makes a quantised read-back (GE: a 1300 W write sits stably at 1206) fail verify forever: 10 retries plus a full repeat every cycle, while the identical write at the uncapped rate verifies (any allocation below ~1.9 kW on a register-quantised rate is the risky band). The REST layer's tolerance for the same write is sized differently — `write_tolerance_watts()/12` from `Invertor_Max_Bat_Rate` with a 2600 W fallback (`givtcp_rest.py`) — and is immune to the cap, so two layers with different tolerances answer one write and the narrower one decides. The signature is a read-back **stable** at the same value off-target every cycle (never self-heals) - distinct from the settling bounce above, where the value sits between old and target only transiently. Workaround keeps the cap exactly: pick a register-exact override value. **Fixed in PR #5348 (v9.3.4):** `rate_tolerances()` (`inverter.py`) now widens the read-back tolerance **below** the written rate to one hardware register step — `fuzzy_below = max(fuzzy, step + 1)` where step is `INVERTER_DEF`'s `rate_step_percent_of_capacity` (GE ships 1) × `nominal_capacity` — mirroring the REST layer's step, and the GivTCP REST and Gateway MQTT paths got the same rule (`49b90af6`, `e7ca9b6a`), so a rounded-down quantised read-back within one step of the write verifies instead of restarting the retry loop; a read-back over the rate is never that rounding and keeps the plain fuzzy (one step **up** is still written down to a 0W hold), and where no `rate_step_percent_of_capacity` is declared the pre-#5348 5%-of-allocated-rate fuzziness still decides. Two case notes from that investigation: the preserve-mode hypothesis could not be verified from the dump because Predbat reads no SE battery-state entity, so the preserve flag is invisible in debug dumps; and a second execute pass ~46s after the first roughly doubled the warning volume in that log — observed only, mechanism never pinned down. `'Connection to inverter ID N failed'` on SE comes from the HA SolarEdge Modbus Multi integration, not Predbat. |
| Too many inverter register writes / replans while the car charges, but the plan looks right | Hour-bucket the actual writes first to split car-conditional churn from a version regression: `grep "write_and_poll_value: Wrote" <log>` (the "No write needed" lines are noise), sed out the hour buckets, `sort | uniq -c`, and date any A/B from the`version X currently running` banners + `Predbat: Startup predbat` restart lines (GH#5434, 2026-10-07: 2-5 writes/h without the car on *both* v9.3.5 and v9.3.6, 9-24/h during the car's IOG session — car-conditional, not version-conditional, despite a mid-log upgrade). Car-conditional mechanisms, all log-verified there: (1) **dynamic-load flap** — the charger throttling load across `threshold_battery` flips `dynamic_load()` (plan.py, the `Dynamic load status changed from ... to ...`→ return True) which forces a replan (`predbat.py`,`Dynamic load adjustment changed, will recompute the plan`); (2) **`watch_list` event-entity refires** — a watched `octopus_free_session` event entity re-reports on the integration's refresh cycle (~15 min) and `watch_event()` (userinterface.py) sets `plan_valid = False` on *any* report, an event entity has no comparable old/new state so every timer bump looks new — unscheduled full replans; (3) **flat-band limit churn** — inside a near-flat cheap band overnight charging is metric-nearly-flat, so the adopted limit swings with small input moves under `metric_min_improvement_plan` (2p default) and both `should_replace_plan()` branches, the same tie surface as GH#5330; (4) **low power mode doubles each limit flip** — with `set_charge_low_power` on, a new target re-runs `find_charge_rate()`'s low-power search, rewriting charge_rate and the timed charge current together; (5) **window wipes** —`disabled charge window while waiting for schedule` (execute.py) when the plan re-picks a window start further out than `set_window_minutes` while the inverter's own window had already started, flipping scheduled charge off then back. (6) pre-#5470 (v9.3.8-) car hold during a charge window - the carHolding branch wrote discharge rate 0 even while the inverter itself was charging, which on some GivEnergy hybrids also blocks grid charging outright (GH#5419, fixed in PR #5470). Since PR #5543 (post-v9.3.8) an inverter *with* `has_charge_enable_time` also gets its charge enable written off between windows in the same branch - deliberately, to stop leaving it enabled from arming stale 00:00-00:00 slots that a GivEnergy 3-phase charges from at full rate for the first minute of the day (GH#5371). Ruled out here: #5403's surplus replan — grep `Octopus Intelligent: dispatch minutes no car needs changed to N` before reaching for it. The "Best charge window" print at the top of a recompute is the prefilled incumbent, not the optimised plan — see the attachment-forensics trap. |
| Low-rate sensors stuck on / the day rate classed as low rate while a saving session or Axle event is in the forecast | **Fixed in PR #5163 (merged 2026-10-09, first released v9.3.7) - keep the pre-#5163 mechanism for older logs**, where the signature is a whole-day low-rate classification on an event day: the auto threshold in `set_rate_thresholds()` (fetch.py) computed its stats from `rate_max`/`rate_min`/`rate_average`, which include the event-boosted minutes - with `rate_low_threshold: 0` the auto import branch's `rate_max - 0.5` rode on a `rate_max` that includes the boosted minutes, so a +100p event on a 25.95/3.49p tariff set a 125.45p threshold and every window classified as low (GH#5050, reproduced in a test through `rate_scan` → `set_rate_thresholds` → `rate_scan_window`); `rate_average` was inflated too (30.54 vs ~17.7 true in the repro), so the *manual* multiplier path was not a clean workaround either - it had to be picked against the inflated `rate_average` the log's `Import rates: min/max/average` line prints, and even `0.9` still landed above the day rate (27.49 > 25.95) in the repro. Both event loaders write the reward into both rate tables, tagging the minutes `saving` in `rate_replicate`, and `rate_scan()` ran afterwards - the in-cycle auto-correction (`rate_import_cost_threshold = highest` after `rate_scan_window`) fixed the threshold but came after `low_rates` was already built with the wrong one; the price-level search re-prices from per-minute rates so plan outcomes were usually near-correct, but candidate granularity degraded; the stamp also preceded the scan, so the provisional `rate_max - 0.5` threshold rose with the stamped `rate_max` and the scan overwrite then admitted plain day-rate windows into `low_rates`, which feeds both car planning and the `charge_window_best` seed (GH#5237, code-read + `car_charging_smart` suite 11/11 on main at the time). **On a current build:** the threshold stats come from `rate_minmax_excluding_saving()` (fetch.py), which maps each saving-tagged minute back to its pre-event rate - downward-only capping, so a genuine free/discounted session stays visible as a real low rate; a saving minute with no pre-event rate is ignored (`load_axle_slot()` builds such minutes from `rate_dict.get(minute, 0)`); user overrides are applied to the pre-saving snapshot the same as the live table (a 50p override over a +50p session reads as 50p in the stats, not the day rate); the snapshot comes from after the IOG dispatch overlay but before any session or override (`rate_import_base` predates `rate_add_io_slots()`, so capping against it would discard a legitimate IOG discount where a dispatch slot and a session overlap); and `rate_max`/`rate_min`/`rate_average` themselves are untouched - dashboard sensors, graph scaling and plan pricing still want the boosted price. The fix's other half: `find_low_rate_windows()` gives the plan the import windows before a qualifying export event (cut at the event start, priced at the day rate - what docs/energy-rates.md promises as the pre-charge), while the low-rate sensors and the non-smart car plan read `low_rates_tariff` (tariff-only windows), so pre-event day-rate windows do not light the low-rate sensor or price the car. The export side is mirrored by the same event-free stats - see the export-threshold row below (GH#5221's event-day instance is fixed by the same PR). Also re-verified and still true: the high-price charge gates (`plan.py`) skip only while `charge_limit_best[window_n] == 0` - already-limited windows are exempt, so "the gate let a high-price window through" reports must first check whether an earlier pass gave it a limit. |
| Energy totals jump or reset | Provider-side cumulative counters and any monotonic clamping. **Check the counter's read pattern for a dip-and-recovery first (GH#5388, 2026-10-04, code-verified):** `minute_data()`'s smoothing branch pads a dip forward at the low value or interpolates clamped to `last_state`, but only zeroes an **upward** recovery when its per-minute step exceeds `MAX_INCREMENT` = 1.2 kWh/min (`const.py`) — so a dip held long enough that its recovery ramp is gentler than 1.2 kWh/min (gap of at least R/1.2 minutes for a recovery of R kWh) is counted in full as that day's energy (GH#5388's 181.3 kWh was exactly a recovery size), while a single-read upward jump is zeroed instead; the asymmetry is the signature. Applies to any integration binding `pv_today`/`load_today`/`import_today`/`export_today` to a cumulative counter; on SolaX the same shape was behind GH#5356/#5388 (see the SolaX row) and PR #5389 added a SolaX-side `hold_counter_dip()` — the `minute_data` asymmetry itself is unfixed and explains the symptom on other integrations. |
| Import cost far above the actual bill on a 3-phase install with per-phase import/export/load sensors | Per-phase lines are **summed, not netted**: `minute_data_import_export()`/`minute_data_load()` (`fetch.py`) accumulate each phase entity's series into one dict, and the docs tell 3-phase users to add one line per phase — so with cross-phase offsets the measured today-so-far cost figures count the offset twice (import on one phase + export on the other where the netted meter bills ~0) (GH#5746, code-read 2026-10-25). The plan's forward-simulated cost is unaffected — the prediction uses one net virtual meter by construction. Companion map for "one phase imports while another phase's battery has SOC and my plan did nothing": there is **no per-phase model at all** — `execute.py` builds fleet sums and drives the fleet in lockstep, so a fleet discharge window inherently lets one inverter cancel another phase's import at a vector-netted meter, and "the site imported while a battery had SOC" is expected whenever exporting at that price loses money (discharge windows are price-driven); export allocation (`allocate_fleet_export_rates()` → `allocate_export_rates()`, utils.py) splits planned export by each inverter's remaining shed above its own target; `balance_inverters()` is SoC-alignment only and never dispatches to offset net grid import. #4856 note for whoever picks that umbrella up: its body predates two restructures — the balancer is now a pure function in `utils.py` whose docstring says deliberate holds can no longer be overwritten, and its workstream-2 export allocator has landed as those two functions; F1 (target split by charge-rate share that never reads `soc_max`) is still live in `execute.py`. |
| The plan is too optimistic about solar — overnight under-charge and peak-rate imports when the expected solar doesn't land, on an AC-coupled site whose load history reads ~0 in daylight (GH#5552) | On such sites (GE AC-coupled, SolaX, Enphase behind a Fox — anywhere the load sensor is derived from grid/battery flows and cannot see the PV inverter) the load data is already net of **all** generation, and the netting is lossy by design: the incrementing counter never records a net decrease (`minute_data()` in `utils.py`, run with `smoothing`+`clean_increment` — small dips are floored at the last value, ≥1 kWh drops are padded as resets), so midday surplus never shows as negative load. Prediction then subtracts the PV forecast **again** — `get_diff()` (`prediction.py`): `diff = load_yesterday − battery_balance − pv_ac` — so the solar is counted twice toward import avoidance and the model under-predicts import. PV calibration cannot rescue it: `pv_today` bound but reading 0 every day makes every past day a down-day, valid days fall below 3 and `pv_calibration()` (`solcast.py`) silently disables itself — grep the log for `SolarAPI: PV Calibration: Not enough valid (non-down) days for calibration`. Verified by code-read on main 2026-10-16 (feature request; no debug dump needed). No generic netting option exists — the closest knobs are provider-only (`ge_cloud_automatic_split_pv` GE Cloud, `solis_cloud_pv_load_ignore` Solis). Suspected same-mechanism: #3255 (GE Cloud direct on 2× AC-coupled — body read only). Related, different causes: #5388/#5356 (SolaX counters), #4922, #3746. |
| Works in HA, broken in Docker/standalone | `ha.py` websocket and `userinterface.py` callback return values. |
| Every charge/export window executes ~N minutes late | The inverter's own clock. Compensation is manual-only via the four `inverter_clock_skew_*` settings. The warn band has moved twice: originally only ≥30 minutes was flagged (restart threshold, `INVERTER_CLOCK_SKEW_RESTART_MINUTES`), so a consistent 25-minute drift sailed under it while still logging a `difference -25.47 minutes` line every cycle (GH#4927); since PR #4991 (`87d4703a`, v9.0.2) `check_clock_skew()` also warns on a moderate 5-29 minute skew, repeated at most hourly per inverter (`INVERTER_CLOCK_SKEW_WARN_MINUTES` = 5, `INVERTER_CLOCK_SKEW_WARN_REPEAT_MINUTES` = 60), so a silent moderate skew is no longer the explanation on a current version — but "current" means v9.0.2+: a reporter on v9.0.1 or earlier is back to "only ≥30 minutes was flagged". Measured from one reporter's log: an export window written at 23:05 drew house-load import until 23:35, and a charge window enabled at 00:30 did not start until ~01:00 (GH#4927). |
| GE (GivEnergy-mode) discharge window times only appear in the inverter portal once the window starts | As-coded, not a bug (GH#2649, verified on main): `adjust_force_export()` nulls the start/end times when `inv_has_ge_inverter_mode and not force_export` ("no point in changing times before we enable export", `inverter.py`); both the pre-window call (execute.py, inside the `set_window_minutes` look-ahead — it passes `False` + the plan times for GE-mode inverters) and the every-cycle outside-window clear (`adjust_force_export(False)`) therefore carry no times, and times are written only by the in-window TARGET engagement branch (`adjust_force_export(True, ...)`) — with the ~10 s-per-polled-write chain (idle start → discharge start → discharge end → enable switch → mode) plus the 30 s "Sleeping (workaround) as start/end of discharge window was just adjusted" settle per real change, so full rate can lag the window start by ~10 min including the inverter's own ramp. That sleep is unconditional on any just-written export window on current main — the v8.37.x `if changed_start_end and not self.rest_data:` guard that exempted GivTCP-REST is gone since the #4864 split (`if changed_start_end:` remains, `inverter.py`; GH#4180, verified 2026-10-12) — so the pause now also applies to GivTCP-REST setups (open PR #5647 exempts the cycle in which the eco toggle itself cannot be written — nothing settles; narrow on merge), and since the charge path never calls `adjust_inverter_mode()` it can only delay export-side service calls (`has_service_api` was never wired to it either: the key has zero occurrences on main). The pre-window TARGET programming is gated on `set_window_minutes` (= `plan_interval_minutes` since v8.27.5 — `db3b78c3`/#2865 + `91f60d51`/#2875; a hard-coded 30 on v8.24.x), and `inverter_set_charge_before: false` zeroes **both** the charge and the discharge look-ahead (`fetch.py`, `fetch_config_options()`) though docs/customisation.md describes the switch as charge-only — no discharge-only knob exists (the reporter's ask stands). A freeze-sentinel (99) export limit never engages a timed export on any version (`export_mode_of()`, `utils.py`) — no start/end writes in any cycle until a mid-session replan downgrades the 99 to a real TARGET limit; the DFS window that started 15 min late did exactly that. Discriminator for "Predbat writes X randomly" with an HA Activity screenshot: Predbat writes appear as rows marked **"triggered by action Select: Select"** (attributed to the account owning the HA token); plain "changed to X" rows matching no Predbat log line are the integration echoing inverter state — the echoed `20:58:00` was the inverter's own programmed slot, which Predbat read back as `Base export window [...]`. Per-inverter asymmetry (one unit churns, the other never changes) can also be legitimate write dedup — `adjust_force_export` writes only when that inverter's read-back differs from the plan — but an unmarked echo is the discrimination above, not dedup. | `inverter` |
| SoC reads far too high, or never falls below ~90% | The sensor mapped to `soc_kw`. A cumulative "battery charge today" energy sensor makes SoC ratchet up all day and reset at midnight. Note `battery_soc:` is **not** a config key - only `soc_percent` and `soc_kw` are read, and unknown apps.yaml keys are silently ignored, so a correct-looking `battery_soc:` block mapping the right sensor does nothing at all (GH#4884). Or the mapped entity itself froze: fingerprint `grep "Inverter N SoC:" predbat.log` — kWh+% frozen at one value for hours while the power numbers in the same line move means an entity Predbat names for SoC stopped changing (GH#5522, 2026-10-12). That identifies *that* an entity froze, not which one — resolve it by grepping the dump's `args_from_apps_yaml` for the template prefix and serial: an apps.yaml that mixes GivTCP instance prefixes per key (per-inverter SoC/rates on a dead add-on generation, power keys on the live one; here `givtcp5`/`givtcp2` SoC vs a live `givtcp3` EMS) freezes reads the same way it strands writes, and a reporter's helper/chart averaging a *different* prefix than apps.yaml names reads "the sensors were fine" truthfully both sides. There is no staleness guard on the `soc_kw = soc_percent × soc_max / 100` read (`inverter.py`), so a frozen percent flows straight to the plan; the drain then floored on the inverters' own reserve because Predbat's reserve/rate writes went through the same dead entities. Do not flag the sign of GivTCP's `battery_power` (charging is negative, `docs/apps-yaml.md`). **The mirrored capacity-side shape (GH#5650, dump-verified 2026-10-20):** `soc_max:` bound to a "kWh **remaining**" sensor makes the modelled capacity track SoC — the reporter's sensor read 2.58 kWh while `soc_percent` was 35 and `sensor.predbat_soc_max_calculated` attributes carried `nominal_capacity: 0.77` from an earlier cycle (~10% of the real 7.9 kWh, captured at the reserve floor) — `soc_kw = percent × soc_max` then double-counts stored energy and the plan charges only what the shrunk model needs, which reads as "battery not fully charging" / "charging only in the last hour of the cheap window". With flat prices charging the *tail* of a flat-priced cheap block is correct on its own (later charge is never worse for the metric), so check the capacity model before calling the window choice wrong — the give-away is a capacity figure that swings with charge level, e.g. `nominal_capacity` on `predbat_soc_max_calculated` differing from live `soc_max` by a SoC-shaped ratio. Fix is config-side: point `soc_max` at the full capacity (apps-yaml.md documents the form). |
| An AppDaemon-published sensor disappears after an HA/Predbat restart and never comes back, while planning and values still work | Check whether the sensor's only publish site sits inside the branch that *reads* the recovered data. Instance: `battery_size_tracking()` (`inverter.py`) publishes `sensor.<prefix>_soc_max_calculated` only behind `if today_key not in existing_history:` — and since PR #4259 `existing_history` is recovered from the recorder via `load_previous_value_from_ha()`, so a mid-day restart (HA or AppDaemon — the entity is AppDaemon-published and vanishes on any AppDaemon restart) skips the publish every cycle until midnight (GH#5222). The trimmed mean *is* recovered separately, so `battery_scaling_auto` and planning are unaffected — which is why everything else looks fine. The same failure triggers on a Predbat/AppDaemon restart, not just an HA restart — wider than the issue title implies; other `load_previous_value_from_ha()` callers (savings totals, ohme energy_today) re-publish unconditionally each cycle, so this gated-publish shape was unique to `battery_size_tracking`. **Fixed in PR #5283 (merged 2026-09-30, v9.3.3)** — `battery_size_tracking()` now re-publishes the sensor from the recorder-recovered entry when a restart removed the live one, preserving a recovered `None` (today's failed measurement) rather than recomputing, and never re-publishes again once the sensor exists; the restart-regression tests now cover both the republish and the no-repeat half. |
| "Predbat Core Update shows in a group called unknown" (an HA update surface group heading) | `update.predbat_version` is a **state-machine-only entity** — published via AppDaemon `set_state` in `expose_config()` (`userinterface.py`, the `type == "update"` branch) with no entity-registry entry and no device, which is true of every Predbat entity and cannot be changed from attributes. Predbat already publishes every naming attribute it can (`title`, `friendly_name`, `entity_picture`, `installed_version`, `latest_version`, `release_url` — `config.py`), and the screen in the screenshot renders them (the bold line is `friendly_name`, the grey "Predbat v9.3.4" is `title` + `latest_version`). The literal "unknown" group heading comes from the HA surface that names a grouped update entity by its **device** and had none — Settings → Updates (`ha-config-updates.ts`) resolves device-name-or-`friendly_name` and has no literal "unknown"; the exact screen/HA version that prints "unknown" was **never pinned** (asked the reporter on GH#5374, awaiting reply) — check more-info-update.ts / ha-config-updates.ts at the reporter's HA version before treating the fallback as known. Not fixable from Predbat: no device to bind (GH#5374, enhancement). On the **skip/hide axis of the same entity (GH#5489, enhancement triage, verified on main d7d8c607, 2026-10-11):** nothing suppresses or hides `update.predbat_version` - no config option exists and `user_config_item_enabled()` gates only on an item's `enable` key, which `version` lacks; the entity is published unconditionally by the release-check path (`github.py`) and reports `auto_update: True` hardcoded in the `type == "update"` branch of `expose_config()` (`userinterface.py`) - deliberate, commit `50dcbb6f` "Predbat update, remove skip option" (#727, Feb 2024; pre-#727 the attribute mirrored Predbat's own `auto_update` switch, default False). That attribute is the *only* thing HA's more-info skip button blocks on, and the dialog the reporter quoted is exactly it - **do not attribute the blocked skip to the no-registry-entry/no-device limitation above; the frontend's skip path needs no unique_id, the two limitations are separate** (the no-unique_id one is general to all Predbat entities; both stand). Predbat already captures `update.skip` (in `EVENT_LISTEN_LIST`) but `update_event()`'s skip branch is a stub that only logs "not yet supported", and the entity is republished with `skipped_version: None` every refresh - HA core's own skip persistence (RestoreEntity) never sees a state-only entity, so any fix must persist the skipped version Predbat-side (Storage component) and publish it back, with the state going `off` when `latest_version == skipped_version` (core's display rule). |
| Charge or discharge rate pinned far below the hardware's | The rate binding in the component's auto-config, not the planner: GEC takes it from the `charge_rate` entity's `max` (GH#4908), Solis bound both directions to `max_charge_power` until PR #5308 made it publish the larger of the two per-direction limits (GH#4940), Fox sums a duplicated `batteryList` (GH#4919). Check `battery_rate_max_raw` / `soc_max` in the debug yaml against the nameplate before reading any plan code. |
| Low power charging mode does nothing / still charges at full rate | Usually working as designed, so check the window times before reading any code. `find_charge_rate()` (`utils.py:1726-1728`) returns max rate whenever the PV forecast summed over the rest of the charge window exceeds `LOW_POWER_PV_THRESHOLD` - **0.1 kWh flat** (`const.py:83`), not scaled to system size. Log signature: `Low power mode: PV forecast in window X.XXkWh > 0.1kWh, default to max rate`. The guard is deliberate (PR #4373): the planner costs charge windows at full rate and low power is applied only to the final plan, so a throttled rate in a PV-overlapping window would defer the target into later grid import. The GH#4577 dawn split (`LOW_POWER_PV_LIGHT_FRACTION`, `const.py:93`, applied in `fetch.py:1863`) only protects the *pre-dawn* part of a window, so a window lying entirely in daylight is all PV-light and low power can never engage there - midday windows are expected to fail. `execute.py` recomputes the rate every 5-minute cycle while the window is active, which is why a manual charge-rate workaround gets overwritten and "it used to work" reports follow. Classify as enhancement, not defect; the two levers if a fix is wanted are scaling the threshold as the dawn split does, or costing a low-power variant in the planner. Related: GH#3311, GH#4557, GH#4699, GH#4975. **On a dawn-split window the dark half is usually frozen, not throttled (GH#5241, code-read verified against main 2026-09-26):** the optimiser's limit for the dark half comes out at the reserve — the freeze sentinel `is_freeze_charge()` compares the limit percent against — so the plan HTML renders `FrzChrg` and no charge rate is computed at all ("rate zero" is really "frozen"); low power cannot engage there both because candidate costing runs with it off (`prediction.py`'s `set_charge_low_power` gate passes only `save in ["best","best10","test"]`, and every optimiser sim — batch path included — runs with `save=None`) and because the light half then abandons low power by design through the `solar_full_rate` guard above. Split boundaries land at the PV-bucket granularity (30 min), not at the logged dawn minute; a pre-dawn half reading the reserve (e.g. `03:00–08:30 @ 14.63p 4%`) in the `Raw charge windows` log lines is the tell, and a pre-dawn-only window that the plan genuinely needs energy from still charges and throttles normally — the freeze is an economics outcome, not a split defect. Toggling `set_charge_low_power_solar_full_rate` changes only the light half's rate. **Write-path variant (GH#5182, dup of #3311 - PR #4645 still unmerged), the answer when low power computes the right rate but the inverter still charges at full power on a service/script-driven inverter:** with `output_charge_control: "power"` the dummy `charge_rate`/`discharge_rate` entities are auto-created only when `inv_output_charge_control != "power"` (`inverter.py:630`), so there is nowhere to store the computed rate; `get_current_charge_rate()` then falls back to `battery_rate_max_raw` (`inverter.py:1952`), and `adjust_charge_immediate()` fills the service template's `{power}` placeholder from it — `{power}` is always the inverter max on a no-entity inverter, and where a #3311 helper entity exists it trails every rate change by one 5-minute cycle. Mixed-fleet extension (GH#5359, probe-verified): when the fleet's args carry the percent key at all, these same read gates take the percent branch regardless of the slot's own state — see the GE Cloud row. Log signature: `Best rate: 600W` → service payload `{'value': '2500'}` → `count register writes 0`, with no `PV forecast in window` line. **Two service-hook payload facts from the same family (PR #5765 review, 2026-10-26, code-read at the PR head — code paths since merged/unchanged):** (1) `{device_id}` is a ready-made hook placeholder — the immediate-call builders fill their `service_data` with `device_id: get_arg("device_id", index=self.id, default="")` (inverter.py) and `call_service_template()` resolves each hook value with `resolve_arg(...)` against `self.args` **plus** the call's data, hooks `indirect=False` — so a template needs no per-hook hardcoded device id: top-level `device_id:` in apps.yaml plus `device_id: "{device_id}"` in each hook works (the huawei.yaml in-hook pattern), and `device_id` is an `APPS_SCHEMA` string item; (2) window entry is the worst case of the known one-cycle `{power}` trail: while idle `apply_inverter_rates()` resets both rate helpers to the maximum every cycle, and the immediate calls run inside the per-inverter loop (execute.py) while the rate writes go through `apply_rate_intent()` after it — so the first dispatch of a window can run at the idle maximum (e.g. a −10 kW helper value), not the planned rate, before self-correcting the next cycle (~0.8 kWh off-plan at 10 kW over one step); not template-fixable, executor ordering. **The execute suite cannot see any of this (GH#5252, verified 2026-09-26):** `test_execute.py`'s mock inverter **overrides** `adjust_charge_immediate()`/`adjust_export_immediate()` (recording only the SoC/freeze arguments), so the real payload-building code — where `{power}` is filled from `get_current_charge_rate()`/`get_current_discharge_rate()` (`inverter.py`) — never executes under `execute_plan` tests, and the isolated-method tests pre-set the state so they cannot see execute_plan's ordering either; a rate-write ordering change (`72b5817a` moved the rate writes out of the execute_plan loop while the immediate calls stayed in-loop reading the stored arg) passed the whole execute suite with zero assertion edits. A green execute run is **not** evidence about the service payload — check the mock's override list before trusting it, and a fix for #5252 needs a test that runs the real `adjust_charge_immediate` against a two-cycle `execute_plan` fixture. **Re-tuning within an active low-power window is design, not churn (GH#5536, 2026-10-13):** `find_charge_rate()` (utils.py) re-runs every 5-minute execute cycle and re-descends a 100 W grid from max, ending at the lowest rate whose sim reaches the target within `end − now − margin` - `charge_low_power_margin` moves the deadline earlier, it does not stop the re-optimisation. The only rate-sticky guard is `best_rate >= highest_achievable_rate and current_charge_rate >= highest_achievable_rate → keep current` (utils.py), where `highest_achievable_rate` is the peak curve-capped kW across the simulated candidates - it mostly fires on a near-full battery in the taper. The only write filter is the 5% fuzzy in `adjust_charge_rate()`/`rate_tolerances()` (inverter.py, `battery_rate_max_charge × MINUTE_WATT / 20`), so a 200-300 W retrim on a ~2.6 kW max crosses it and writes. Design trap for a completion-window hysteresis ask: prediction.py applies low power only on the final passes (the `set_charge_low_power` gate, `prediction.py:666`), so a keep-current-rate rule can only be execute-side, and the published prediction and the executed rate can legitimately diverge (GH#2628 is the adjacent deadband ask). |
| The plan tab's tooltip charge kW is far higher than the plotted `battery_power_best`, while the plan itself is right | Display-only (GH#5394) — **fixed in PR #5643 (merged 58b0bc36, 2026-10-28); keep the mechanism for older versions.** The tooltip's `get_charge_rate_kw()` (`output.py`) used to re-run `find_charge_rate()` with the prediction's inputs and never apply the "Clamp at inverter limit" block (`prediction.py`) that converges the combined flow to `inverter_limit` through `get_total_inverted()` — the reporter's 14.4 kW request landed at 10.56 kW DC. The fix caps the tooltip at `min(rate, inverter_limit × inverter_loss + pv_dc)`, which the review-derived algebra made **exact** for hybrids with PV while the clamp binds. The known remainder is the `battery_rate_max_charge_dc > battery_rate_max_charge` boost (the `pv_above` lines in `prediction.py`): the prediction's requested rate can exceed the AC-side battery rate it actually uses, so the tooltip under-reports there. An export-side tooltip (the reporter's follow-up ask) must mirror the specific branch per state — TARGET export vs freeze route through `export_limit`/`inverter_limit_export`, and on hybrids `pv_ac/inverter_loss` enters `get_total_inverted()` while `pv_dc` is subtracted 1:1. |
| An export window is in the plan but moves nothing | Fractional export limits in the sentinel band. A 99% floor combined with a low-power rung emits limits like 99.3/99.5/99.7, and both `prediction.py` and `execute.py` split at the `EXPORT_LIMIT_FREEZE`/`EXPORT_LIMIT_IDLE` sentinels, so those land in "Hold exporting" - simulator and executor agree, making it consistently inert rather than a plan/execute mismatch. With the full-power rung the same 99% floor emits exactly 99.0, the freeze sentinel itself, so a plain "export to 99%" window silently becomes a freeze (GH#4914). Related, and worth knowing before concluding an overlapping charge candidate should have won: `remove_intersecting_windows()` runs **inside every scoring simulation** (`prediction.py`), not just as a final-plan post-pass, and a freeze-export window's 99.0 limit counts as enabled and does the clipping - so the optimiser scores an overlapping charge candidate as zero benefit and "never a candidate" is accurate. The clip is shared with the kernel path (`tests/test_kernel_parity.py`'s `run_intersect_window_tests`/`run_clipping_parity_tests`), so a threshold change needs both engines updated together. |
| Predbat exceeded my site's import ceiling (or the plan would) | **There is no import-side clamp anywhere in the planner** (GH#5490, enhancement triage, verified by grep on main d6985b6d, 2026-10-11): `import_limit` in the tree is per-inverter *discovery ratings* only - `sunsynk.py`/`fox.py`/`deye.py` surface the device-reported cap as a capability record, never read by the plan or prediction paths - `config.py` has no `import_limit` setting, and the per-minute prediction loop clamps export only (`export_limit = self.export_limit * step`, `prediction.py`) with no import twin. So a report maps to "no such setting exists", not to a misbehaving clamp, and a components-grep landing on the ratings can be misread as if a planner cap exists. Joint EV+battery co-optimisation is the GH#5490 umbrella ask (the GH#2449/GH#1199 family; the car planner's import-price-only ranking is GH#5384, in the Car charging row), and the recorded direction is draft PR #5486 (a diagnostics-only whole-site `import_limit`, no enforcement) plus the staged design note on GH#1199. |
| Predbat plans export windows although the site is not permitted to export to the grid (DNO limit, installer permission, hardware restriction) | **On a Tesla/Teslemetry site this is now detected — fixed on main by PR #5710 (b37f25af, 2026-10-27); keep the pre-#5710 reading for older logs.** `update_battery_export_permitted()` (`teslemetry.py`) reads `site_info.components.net_meter_mode` — `battery_ok` means battery export allowed, anything else or missing means installer-level Solar Only (Tesla One > Import & Export Limits, which sits above `customer_preferred_export_rule` and cannot be changed over the API) — publishes `binary_sensor.predbat_teslemetry_battery_export_permitted`, warns once per boot when blocked, and wires a per-inverter `battery_export_permitted` arg that turns export-window planning off fleet-wide while it reads off and replans on any change. Only blocked sites re-read site_info hourly (`EXPORT_PERMISSION_RECHECK_SECONDS`, `recheck_battery_export_permitted()`); `fetch_site_info()` itself stays once per process. `teslemetry_battery_export: true/false` overrides the detection (the `teslemetry_hybrid` unset-becomes-None convention). The battery still covers load and spare solar is still exported. Pre-#5710 mechanism (why the two look-alike levers mis-model, worth keeping): `export_limit: 0` caps the site's *total* AC export — it also models spare-solar export as zero, wrong when solar export is allowed — and `battery_rate_max_discharge: 0` stops the model discharging at all, while the hardware restriction blocks only *grid* export and the battery still covers house load by self-consumption, so the model priced the house as importing where the battery covered it. `net_meter_mode` read `battery_ok` at 23/26 exporting sites in the maintainer's survey and was absent at the three that cannot. TESLA's `support_discharge_freeze: False` is a different axis (freeze export) and unaffected. Not a duplicate of #5490: that is the import side, a different lever. | |
| Every ordinary export window vanishes from the plan when a saving session or Axle event is inside the horizon (battery sits full through peak PV and clips) | The export-side ratchet (GH#5221, replay-verified on main, **fixed for the event-day instance in PR #5163, v9.3.7** - keep the mechanism for pre-9.3.7 logs): with an event inside the horizon the automatic export threshold started at `rate_export_min + 0.5`, and the `rate_scan_window()` rescan then **overwrites** it with `highest` — but `rate_scan_window` initialises `lowest = 99` (`fetch.py`) and only ever lowers it, so windows averaging the event price (120p) never move it and the threshold **latched at 99, not the event price** (the issue's own write-up saying "ratchets to the event price" is wrong on this point); `high_export_rates` then contained only the event windows and every ordinary export window disappeared (`export_window_best = clone_windows(high_export_rates)` when `calculate_best_export` + `set_export_window` are on). **Do not read the dump's stored `rate_export_cost_threshold` as the operating threshold** — `publish_rate_and_threshold()` (`output.py`) overwrites the member from the plan's own price level (`rate_best_cost_threshold_export`), which is 120 precisely because the plan exports nowhere but the event; the June log line "High export rate found rates in range 99p to 120.0p" is the sentinel pair in the wild. Counterfactual that settles it: strip the event minutes from `rate_export` and re-run the same sequence — the threshold settles at ~20 and the PV-peak export window reappears. **Fixed in PR #5163 (v9.3.7):** the threshold stats are now event-free (see the low-rate row above) and the ordinary windows are admitted again - the fix's own test pins the control reproducing the unfixed "event-windows-only / ratchet-to-99" behaviour on a #4036/#5221-shaped Axle event; the `lowest = 99` init in `rate_scan_window()` (fetch.py) is itself unchanged, so the overwrite path is still what any future rescan change must respect. Method note: exercise the fetch-layer threshold logic on a dump via a temporary `TEST_REGISTRY` test calling `set_rate_thresholds()` + the rescan directly (see the replay section above), not via the dump's stored values. |
| Export icon/status says "off" while the battery really is exporting | `isExporting` is set at only two sites in `execute.py` — the immediate-export branch and the in-window freeze-export branch (at/below target), the latter setting it exactly like a genuine export while only zeroing the charge rate so Eco discharge stays live — and the demand-freeze block appends `[Freeze exporting]` to the status text without setting it - so a demand-period freeze export publishes `binary_sensor.predbat_exporting` as "off" (GH#5125; PR #5131, open and unmerged, builds the active-vs-freeze distinction), and any consumer reading the flag as "genuinely force-exporting" also trips on the freeze branch — which is how the car-hold pause gets skipped during in-window freeze export (GH#5612). Read the status sentence (`[Freeze exporting]`), not the binary sensor, before concluding nothing exported. |
| Freeze exporting (in-window at/below target, or `set_freeze_export_during_demand`) plus a car hold on an inverter with a timed pause (`inv_has_timed_pause: true`) — the battery still drains, or the "Hold for car" status is a lie | The timed-pause holds share one `resetPause` flag per execute pass (`execute.py`): the in-window freeze-export branch and the demand-freeze branch each call `adjust_pause_mode(pause_charge=True)` and consume it (`resetPause = False`), so the later car hold and iBoost hold find it spent and never request `pause_discharge` — on a timed-pause inverter the discharge keeps feeding the car. Charge branches that request the pause themselves are fine. Inverters without a timed pause use the `discharge_rate = 0` + reserve path instead and don't hit this. **Fixed in PR #5640 (merged ae4e9c43, 2026-10-24) — keep the mechanism for pre-#5640 versions:** the fix gates that `Hold for car` log/status on the pause actually landing — on it, `Demand [Freeze exporting]` plus the "timed pause is already pausing charge only, so the hold for car N cannot also pause discharge" log line means discharge is **not** paused: check the version before trusting the status. Why a one-line fix flaps: `adjust_pause_mode` compares against the live `pause_mode` entity, so upgrading to PauseBoth from inside the car hold, after the freeze branch wrote PauseCharge, would rewrite it every cycle — the pause needs resolving once per inverter after all holds are known. Two traps from the same PR: `carHolding` is not status-only — in the post-loop charge-off branch it swaps `adjust_charge_immediate(0)` for `adjust_charge_immediate(soc, freeze=True)` (a real `charge_freeze_service` write on non-exporting cycles, execute.py), so don't clear it just to fix a status; and `run_execute_test` infers the expected immediate-charge SoC target from the `assert_status` string, so a scenario whose status loses "Hold for car" but still holds needs `assert_immediate_charge_soc_target_array` passed explicitly. |
| Predbat can't self-update, "unable to fetch" or a 403 | The unauthenticated GitHub API quota shared across the egress IP, not a broken release (GH#4886). |
| A downloaded debug yaml has empty step arrays | Expected, not corruption. The arrays are built during `calculate_plan()` and freed at the end of the same `update_pred()` cycle by the `Free memory` block (`predbat.py`, re-anchor on that comment — the line cite has drifted twice) whenever `debug_enable` is off, so a UI download taken afterwards never sees them. Deterministic on every download, not a race in the 2-hour auto-disable window (GH#4853). **A replay of such a dump crashes before any plan runs (GH#5650, verified by replaying the #5650 dump):** with the step arrays cleared, `prediction.py`'s direct indexing of them raises `KeyError: 0` right after the `Prediction kernel stale binary` warning — grep the dump's `pv_forecast_minute_step:` / `load_minutes_step:` keys for `{}` before burning a replay attempt; `charge_window_best`, `charge_limit_best`, `charge_window`, `rate_import` and the full `args:` section are still present and were enough to diagnose GH#5650 completely. |
| Memory usage high / addon RSS creeps up | Measured on Linux against a live config (no Load ML, ~40 threads): the idle floor is ~75 MB of imports before any data (stdlib 23, aiohttp 12, numpy 13 even with ML off, requests 6, own modules 13), live app data is only ~25 MB (HA state cache 8, minute arrays 5, rates 3, config 3), and the rest was allocator residue. Two things made most of the difference and are now in the tree: `yaml.dump()` builds a node tree ~30x the text (~150 MB for a full debug dict) so `create_debug_yaml()` streams one top-level key at a time through `DebugYamlDumper`, whose shared anchor counter is what stops the pieces failing to load with "found duplicate anchor"; and glibc keeps freed pages plus one malloc arena per thread, so `update_pred()` calls `malloc_trim()` after `gc.collect()` and `initialize()` caps arenas with `limit_malloc_arenas()` (both no-ops on macOS/musl). Baseline 266 MB peak / 162 MB idle became 138 / 126. If someone is still well above that, ask whether Load ML is on (adds ~30 MB idle plus 16 OpenBLAS threads) and check `Threads` in `/proc/<pid>/status`; the next lever is lazy imports (numpy when ML is off, components on enable), not the state cache. |
| Plan seems stuck / doesn't adopt a clearly better replan | `should_replace_plan()` (plan.py) compares pre-clip snapshots; a retained incumbent keeps its stale snapshot without the expired-window pruning `charge_window_best` gets, so the improvement gap can read identical for hours while real costs move. Combined with the fragmentation branch inside `should_replace_plan()` (PR #4301), which on a near-tie favours the less fragmented plan and even adopts a cost-neutral less-fragmented plan outright, a genuinely-better-by-less-than-the-gate plan can stay rejected until something else invalidates the plan outright. Confirmed from a 24h log: 180 consecutive comparisons with an unchanged gap, only 7 of 187 total ever adopting a new plan (GH#4736).
